# Changelog — v5.x

All notable changes to the v5.x release line are documented in this file.
The full changelog index lives in [`../CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md).

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

## [5.1.1] - 2026-10-07

### Changed

- **The Stop hook no longer asks the agent to label core candidates by default ([#1740](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1740)).** In 5.1.0, after each transcript ingest, the Stop hook added a turn to your conversation with a classifier prompt and an `aelf core-gate accept` command ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)). That batch is now off unless you set `[core_gate] session_end = true` or export `AELFRICE_CORE_GATE_SESSION_END=1`. With it off, unlabeled beliefs stay in core, as they did before 5.1.0, and `aelf doctor core-gate --emit` still labels the backlog when you ask. Labeling without a conversation turn is tracked in [#1741](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1741).

### Fixed

- **The context rebuilder now reads aelfrice's own turn log from a linked worktree ([#1706](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1706)).**
  - **The cause:** to find `turns.jsonl`, the rebuilder walked up from the hook payload's directory to the first `.git` entry. In a linked worktree, `.git` is a file, so it built a path that could never exist and fell back to the host's own transcript. The transcript logger writes the log under the git common dir.
  - **The fix:** the reader now uses the logger's own resolver: `AELFRICE_TRANSCRIPTS_DIR`, else the git common dir, else `~/.aelfrice/transcripts/`. It resolves from the process's working directory, like every other in-turn reader ([#1630](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1630)), rather than from the payload's.
  - **Affected readers:** the compaction rebuild, the cadence rebuild, the conversation-aware query, and `aelf rebuild`.
  - **Two side effects:**
    - A hook payload without a `cwd` now reads the turn log too.
    - The conversation-aware query, which is on by default, now makes one `git rev-parse` call per prompt to find the log.
  - **Tests:** the test suite now pins `AELFRICE_TRANSCRIPTS_DIR` to its sandbox, so no test reads a contributor's real turn log.
- **The context rebuilder reads only the current session's turns from the shared turn log ([#1744](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1744)).** Every session and linked worktree of a repository appends to one `turns.jsonl`, and the readers took its last turns whatever session wrote them. When two sessions ran at once, each one's compaction rebuild, cadence rebuild, and conversation-aware query could read the other's turns and pull in its session-scoped beliefs. Sessions in the main checkout had this since the turn log shipped; [#1706](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1706) extended it to linked worktrees.
  - **The fix:** the hook readers keep only the turns of the hook payload's `session_id`. When the log holds none for that session, or the payload has no `session_id`, they read the host's per-session transcript instead.
  - **After compaction:** the transcript logger archives the turn log when the conversation compacts, so the rebuild after compaction now usually reads the host's transcript. Before, it read whatever another session had logged since.
  - **`aelf rebuild`:** without `--transcript`, it reads the turns of the session that wrote the log's last line carrying a session ID. If another session writes in between, that's the session it reads; pass `--transcript` to choose one.

## [5.1.0] - 2026-10-06

**Upgrading from 5.0.** Most changes in this release are fixes that need no action. These change behavior you may rely on:

- **`~/.aelfrice.toml` isn't read anymore ([#1582](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1582)).** Configuration discovery now stops at your home directory and at the nearest `.git`, so settings in `~/.aelfrice.toml` no longer apply, and a submodule or nested linked worktree no longer inherits the outer repository's `.aelfrice.toml`. Move home settings into the `.aelfrice.toml` at your project root. When the project has no `.aelfrice.toml` of its own, the SessionStart notice and `aelf doctor` tell you the home file is ignored ([#1652](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1652)).
- **Praise raises confidence by default ([#1647](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1647)).** `[feedback] sentiment_from_prose` now defaults to `true`, but only the positive half applies. To turn the lane off, set it to `false`. If you had opted in and want complaints to keep counting, also set `[feedback] sentiment_negative = true`.
- **Commit ingest runs from a git `post-commit` hook ([#1698](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1698)).** Upgrading doesn't remove the old `PostToolUse` entry; `aelf setup` removes it and installs the git hook for the current repository only. To keep recording a repository's commits, run `aelf setup` inside each repository.
- **Two unused knobs are removed ([#1655](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1655)).** `AELFRICE_META_BELIEF_EXPANSION_GATE_TOKEN_THRESHOLD` and `AELFRICE_META_BELIEF_POSTERIOR_TEMPERATURE` have no effect. Neither was ever enabled by default, and retrieval output is unchanged. You can delete them from your environment.
- **Core selection applies the core admission gate ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** Unlocked beliefs can leave `<core>` and `aelf core` output when their cached label excludes them; locked beliefs are never gated. `aelf core --json` adds a `core_gate_label` field to labeled rows.
- **The Stop hook can add a turn to label core candidates ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** After each transcript ingest, a Stop can add one turn with a classifier prompt and an `aelf core-gate accept` command. It doesn't fire on Codex or in headless sessions. To turn this off, set `[core_gate] session_end = false` or `AELFRICE_CORE_GATE_SESSION_END=0`.
- **Hook output is capped at 9,500 characters ([#1639](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1639)).** User locks still come first, but when they don't all fit, the locks past the limit are cut whole, and one line names up to 20 of them and counts the rest.
- **The claude-memory mirror can get consent from a linked worktree ([#1657](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1657)).** If you ran `aelf setup` from a worktree and the mirror never turned on, run `aelf doctor`: it now warns and gives the fix.

The store schema migrates on first open: `belief_corroborations` gains a nullable `speaker` column ([#1650](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1650)), and the `core_gate_labels` and `core_gate_batches` tables are added ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)). No command is needed.

Follow-ups for older installs:

- **Codex:** re-run `aelf setup --host codex`. It replaces the old `uv run` skill text and refreshes changed skills ([#1413](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1413)). Until you do, `aelf doctor --host codex` reports the stale skills and exits 1.
- **Stores onboarded before this release (optional):** `aelf doctor --repair-utc-created-at --apply` rewrites the commit dates that onboard stored with a local offset as UTC ([#1660](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1660)), and `aelf doctor --gc-filesystem-corroboration --apply` removes the filesystem corroborations that inflate `aelf core` and `corr=` ([#1669](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1669)). Run each without `--apply` first to see what it would change.

### Added

- **K3: the budget census's noise floor now carries a measured A/A term, and it is 0.0pp ([#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546)).** `docs/design/budget_discriminability_k0_preregistration.md` builds the floor as `NF = max(1.96 * sqrt(0.25 / N), 9.5pp, A/A_band)` and records that the third term was missing, so the band covered grader disagreement and binomial sampling noise and no instrument noise at all. `scripts/budget_discriminability_aa_replicate.py` supplies it, and `grey_band()` now takes it: `AA_BAND_PP` is a pinned literal with `AA_BAND_MEASURED` beside it, because a zero that *was measured* and a term that *does not exist* were previously the same number, and the flag is what tells them apart. A test compares the constant against the producer's own figure, so the two cannot drift. The census is deterministic — `--check` hashes two reports on every run — so a replicate that re-ran the same inputs would measure zero by construction; the perturbation is instead belief **insertion order**, re-ingested under a seeded permutation that holds the corpus, the gold sets, the grid, the lane set, and every belief's content fixed and moves only the rowid SQLite assigns. The producer reuses `budget_discriminability_census.report(queries=...)` rather than reimplementing EC, so the lanes, grid, cost functions, exclusion rules, block-identity check, and containment check are the census's own. Measured over 9 replicates at base seed 1546: `aa_band_pp` 0.0, on all six lane cells and all twelve lane-by-corpus cells, with N holding at 7 and 0 bound violations. `NF` is unchanged at 37.0398pp, because the binomial term dominates at N=7 — the census's `--check` hash moves only because `grey_band_has_aa_term` flips from `false` to `true`. The zero is earned rather than asserted: a positive control that makes retrieval read the rowid drives the band to 100.0pp and 210 of 2070 order-sensitive grid arms at the same replicate count, and a sweep of every lane, query, and grid cell finds 0 of 2070 whose retrieved list moves under permutation — so the band is not zero because movements cancelled, the inputs to EC are invariant. Two limits are published beside the figure rather than left to a reader: the term is estimated where **no cap binds on any cell**, a regime in which EC is 0 by construction and cannot move, and the temporal spine — the one lane that orders by rowid — never runs on an edgeless corpus.
- **A bench-gate corpus counts only when it defeats its own null model ([#1581](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1581)).** The quality tier's headline number is "N tests executed against the corpus", and that number was not evidence: a corpus can pass its gate while being unable to represent the distinction the gate claims to measure. Measured on the mounted lab corpus, `query_strategy` had `len(expected_top_k) == len(beliefs)` on 30 of 30 rows and `k < len(beliefs)` on 2 of 30, so a deterministic shuffle of each row's own candidate pool scored **P@10 0.9933 / NDCG@10 0.8525** against a gate whose floor was `> 0.0`. Both halves of that are fixed below, and the corpus half alone would not have been enough. Operator ruling 2026-09-20 makes the check a precondition to counting rather than an advisory: a corpus counts only when the gate's own metric, recomputed with the shipped implementation replaced by the module's declared null model, **fails** the gate's bar. `tests/bench_gate/test_directive_detection.py::test_directive_corpus_defeats_a_first_token_baseline` ([#374](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/374), [#1349](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1349)) already did exactly this for one module over K=200 salted partitions, and is the reason `directive_detection` is the one module in the tier anyone should currently believe; this generalises that test rather than inventing a second mechanism.

  Every module under `tests/bench_gate/` now declares its family and its null model in one place, `GATE_DECLARATIONS` in `tests/bench_gate/null_model.py`, and the declared null model runs **inside the gate, on the same rows, every time the gate runs**. Ranking gates are scored against the row's candidate pool in deterministic shuffled order; classification gates against a constant predictor returning the corpus's majority label; ablation gates against the ablated arm plus a requirement that the ablated arm reaches the gold on at least 10% of rows, which is what rejects "the feature is the only path to the gold" — a corpus shaped that way reports +1.000 uplift and proves the weight table rather than the feature. `Family.EXEMPT` is available where no null model is constructible from the rows, and it has to state the reason, so an exemption is visible rather than silently counted as validated. Three enforcement tests make the declaration unskippable: one fails when a module under `tests/bench_gate/` has no entry, one parses each gate file and fails when a module declares a family but never calls a guard, and one compares each declaration against the module name the gate passes to `load_corpus_module` — `test_edge_rerank_potentially_stale` declared `rerank_relevance` while grading `bfs_potentially_stale`, and nothing consumed the field, so the two names printed side by side in the tier block unnoticed.

  Omitting `gold_key` and `pool_key` turns the structural pre-filters off, which is a claim about the corpus, so the guards check it rather than trust it: a gate whose rows carry gold entries drawn from a candidate pool must wire the keys. The condition tested is that the gold entries *are* pool ids, not that a pool-shaped key is present — `compression_a4_fidelity` seeds a store from `beliefs` and scores free-text answers against it, so its gold and its pool are different populations. `test_intentional_clustering` was the one gate using the switch and was wrong about its rows: all 14 `multi_fact` rows carry `beliefs`, and it now declares the keys along with `k_key`, because the guard's `default_k` of 10 exceeds every `multi_fact` pool and would reject a corpus that is separable on all 14 rows at the cutoff the gate scores with.

  **Both scores now reach the release record.** Only `test_directive_detection.py` recorded anything before, so every other gate printed its number solely inside a failure message and a green run left no evidence — which makes a degenerate +1.000 uplift indistinguishable from a real +0.06 to a reviewer reading a passing run. Each guard records the shipped score, the null score, the bar, whether the null cleared it, and the verdict through `record_property`, before the assertion. The `null_clears_bar` field is why the no-regression gates stay honest: a zero uplift clears a `>= 0` bar by construction, so `retrieve_uplift` and `compression_a4_fidelity` record that their green is regression evidence and not positive evidence about the feature.

  **The headline count now requires a verdict.** Every bench-gated report used to increment "N tests executed against the corpus", so a gate that graded nothing was reported as evidence it never produced: the verdict was recorded after the shipped arm, and any exception in that arm discarded it. The verdict is a finding about the corpus and never depended on the shipped score, so it is settled first. A rejected corpus no longer drives the shipped arm at all, and an arm that raises records `UNVERIFIED` with the null score it did produce, then re-raises unchanged. A scored module's test counts as executed only when it recorded `ACCEPT`; every other bench-gated report is listed by test ID under a `UNVERIFIED` heading. That requirement is also what closes the limit of the wiring test: parsing the file shows a `guard_*_gate(...)` call that is present, not one that is reachable, and a guard that does not run leaves no verdict.

  **The tier summary reports rejection as its own state**, distinct from executed and from no-verdict, and a rejected gate is not counted as executed — it graded nothing. Two structural pre-filters run before the null model because they cost milliseconds against a driver that rebuilds a store per row, and the rejection names which one fired: `len(gold) < len(pool)` and `k < len(pool)` on at least 90% of rows, and `max(len(gold)) <= 3 * median(len(gold))` — a micro-averaged metric lets one 60-target row supply most of the denominator of a 30-row corpus, which a `MIN_ROWS` floor does not defend against.

  Verified 2026-09-20, before the corpus replacement and the floor raise described above, against the 12 corpus modules that have rows anywhere on the author's machine, mounted as one root. Seven of them have a scored gate, and all seven produced a verdict: `query_strategy` **REJECT** (separability pre-filter, 0.0% of 30 rows; shuffle NDCG@10 0.8525 against a `> 0.0` floor — the state the two changes above fix, and the only verdict in this paragraph they move); `directive_detection` ACCEPT (majority constant scores −0.800 headroom against the P≥0.80/R≥0.60 conjunction); `sentiment` ACCEPT (majority constant 0.4182 against a 0.500 bar); `contradiction` ACCEPT (majority constant 0.3418 against 0.500); `compression_a2_recall` ACCEPT (ablated arm reaches the gold on 72.0% of rows, uplift +0.3733); `compression_a4_fidelity` ACCEPT (100% of rows, uplift +0.0159); `multi_fact` ACCEPT (100% of rows, uplift +0.0357). Of the remaining five mounted modules, `compression_uplift` and `wonder_consolidation` belong to gates declared `Family.EXEMPT`, and `dedup`, `enforcement` and `promotion_trigger` have no gate at all since [#1579](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1579) retired the three that graded absent code. The run reports 11 tests executed, one module rejected, and five bench-gated tests with no verdict — the four corpus round-trip and latency smoke tests that call no guard, plus the K=200 first-token baseline, which is an assertion beside the gate rather than a guard inside it.

  **`query_strategy`'s floor is raised into the measured window, and the rebuilt rows alone would not have cleared it.** The gate stated `> 0.0` twice — as the bar the declared null model must fail and as the assertion the shipped arm must clear — and `> 0.0` is a tripwire for "retrieval returned nothing", not a null-model trap: every null model clears it, so the #1581 bar could only ever reject on a structural pre-filter. The 30 rows are replaced lab-side by 40 that pass both pre-filters (gold == pool on 0 of 40, `k < len(pool)` on 40 of 40, max gold 1.33x the median), and on those rows the declared null still scores **NDCG@k 0.1499** — which clears `> 0.0`, so the rebuild on its own leaves the gate reporting REJECT. The floor is now `_NULL_FLOOR = 0.35`, and it is derived rather than picked: `benchmarks/query_strategy_null_distribution.py` re-shuffles every row's pool under 200 independent seeds (min 0.0607, mean 0.1346, **max 0.2307**) and the floor is that maximum times 1.5, rounded up to two decimals — half again above the worst draw, because a sweep maximum is an empirical maximum and not a bound. The producer imports `null_model.shuffled_pool` rather than reimplementing it, so the canonical figure it publishes is computed by the code the gate runs. It is store-backed in the #1469 sense — the rows are lab-side and do not go to a public runner ([#1456](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1456)) — so its four markers carry `corpus=` and `producer-sha=` and CI holds them to self-consistency and staleness rather than re-running them. On the other side the floor leaves room: shipped `legacy-bm25` measures **0.8716** (+0.5216, 2.49x the floor) and the losing `stack-r1-r3` measures **0.6508** and clears it too, deliberately, since a floor only the current default could pass would pre-judge the re-flip this gate's comparison exists to detect.

  **The raised floor is proven load-bearing rather than asserted to be.** Substituting the declared null model for the shipped arm — `dataclasses.replace` on the runner's result, both arms set to the shuffled-pool score — fails on `assert scores[DEFAULT_STRATEGY] > _NULL_FLOOR` at `0.14988237797810616 > 0.35`, and specifically not on the arm comparison, which a tie satisfies (`uplift=+0.0000`). The counterfactual is the point of the change: with the assertion's floor alone put back to `0.0` and the bar left at 0.35, that same substitution passes green and the tier counts the test as executed against an ACCEPT verdict. Mounted against the rebuilt corpus the tier reports `query_strategy: family=ranking shipped=0.8716 null=0.1499 bar=> 0.35 null_clears_bar=False verdict=ACCEPT n_rows=40`, and the run moves from 5 executed with one module rejected to 6 executed with none.

  Eleven further modules have no rows anywhere on this machine — `bfs_potentially_stale`, `bfs_relates_to`, `derived_from_edge`, `doc_linker`, `implements_edge`, `reasoning`, `rerank_relevance`, `retrieve_uplift`, `temporal_next_edge`, `tests_edge` and `wonder_online`. They are reported as having no verdict, which is the state they were already in.

  **A half-built module no longer vanishes from the summary.** `_MODULE_SKIP_RE` in `tests/conftest.py` matched only `missing|empty`, so a module that skipped for being under its row floor printed neither "executed" nor "no verdict" and disappeared — eleven such skip sites across ten gate modules, plus one in `compression_uplift` for a corpus it cannot grade. Every corpus-state skip now goes through `skip_corpus_module` or `require_min_rows`, an enforcement test fails on a bespoke `pytest.skip` that mentions a corpus, and the summary maps the state to a sentence: `underfilled` and `ungradeable` report as `UNVERIFIED`, and a state nobody has written a sentence for still prints rather than disappearing. Observed on the real corpus: `contradiction_v3` skips with 27 `contradicts`-labelled rows against its floor of 30, and now says so.
- **`scripts/check_posterior_writers.py` gates the set of code paths that can write a belief's posterior ([#1592](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1592)).** #1592 AC2 asks which paths write `beliefs.alpha` / `beliefs.beta`, and [#1359](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1359) established that writing the answer down is not enough — that enumeration surfaced a new unguarded writer every review round, because nothing stopped one being added between rounds. The checker enumerates writers from the AST, runs as a CI job, and fails when the set changes without its manifest changing with it. It finds 21, of which **12 can move a posterior mean and only two are meant to**: `apply_feedback` on evidence, and `clamp_ghosts` deliberately lowering an alpha. Eight of the remaining ten are callers of `update_belief`, a whole-row write of an in-memory snapshot, so promotion, unlock, devalidate, scope change, review, orphan reclassification, autolock, and the lock command each load a belief, change one unrelated field, and write `alpha`/`beta` back along with it — the shape [#1168](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1168) found losing 180 of 240 concurrent feedback events. Detection is AST-based rather than textual because `clamp_ghosts` documents the SQL that reverses a clamp inside its module docstring, and a regex sweep reports that as a writer which does not exist; literal strings in argument *and keyword* position are read, f-strings through their literal parts, and quoted or schema-qualified table names are matched while the `beliefs_fts` and `beliefs_new` siblings are not. The check is a backstop against accidental additions and not a proof: nine of ten evasion shapes an adversarial review enumerated remain invisible to it, and both they and the two writers no static check can classify are named in the module docstring and in an explicit `UNDETECTABLE` list, with tests that fail if either record is deleted. `benchmarks/posterior_prior_grid_sweep.py --sources` measures the other half — which writers actually fire, how many of their events carry a posterior-moving valence, and how many of those land on a user-locked belief where the #1168 lock floor refuses the bump regardless of any flag.
- **`benchmarks/posterior_prior_grid_sweep.py` measures how much real evidence has reached belief posteriors, across every store on a host ([#1592](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1592)).** #1592 reported that all 19,875 beliefs in one store sit on their insertion prior, so `posterior_weight` reranks by `(belief_type, source)` rather than by observed confidence. That store reproduces — 7 distinct pairs, none off the prior grid — and the sweep confirms the conclusion while correcting its mechanism. Sitting on a prior does **not** mean no evidence arrived: `apply_feedback(update_posterior=False)` writes the audit row and skips the bump, which since [#1086](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1086) is the default for retrieval exposure, and `clamp_ghosts` writes a *lower* alpha that can push a moved belief back onto a prior. Across 26 stores and 172,089 beliefs, 6,220 sit on a prior while carrying a real non-zero-valence feedback event — 283 of them on this repo's own store, which took 734 such events and moved nothing. Of the 9,413 beliefs that are off the grid, 2,392 are dedupe mass (the canonical row of a collapsed duplicate group holds the *sum* of the group's alphas and betas, so `n` copies of one prior land at `n * prior` with the posterior mean exactly unchanged, and retrieval blends `log(posterior_mean)`), and **6,760 of the remaining 7,021 — 96.3% — have their earliest feedback event before the 2026-07-04 commit that made retrieval exposure audit-only.** They moved under a rule the tree removed as a defect. Fewer than 300 beliefs on the entire host carry a posterior moved by the feedback path as it ships today. The producer classifies against the complete set of insertion priors, built by calling `get_source_adjusted_prior` over the real `TYPE_PRIORS` so the grid cannot drift from the shipped resolver, and identifies the pair #1592 left unexplained: `(0.3, 1.0)` is `wonder/lifecycle.py`'s speculative ingest default, another prior rather than an update. Each store is byte-copied with its write-ahead log and opened `read_only=True`, since a read-write open runs schema DDL and migrations, which is a write.
- **A transcript belief now anchors to the turn it came from, not to its source label ([#1602](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1602)).** Every transcript anchor used to be `file:<source label>`. The labels in use are a few constants, so an anchor recorded that some transcript existed without naming which part of it a belief came from. When a turn is ingested with both a session id and its own timestamp, each sentence's belief now anchors to `file:<label>#<session_id>/<ts>/<turn_sha>`. That covers `aelf ingest-transcript` (which the transcript-log flush runs), `aelf doctor --backfill-ingest`, and a direct `ingest_turn(..., session_id=..., created_at=...)` call. `turn_sha` is the first 16 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the whole turn text, and the same fragment is stored as the anchor's `position_hint`. The label stays the URI's prefix, so a consumer that groups anchors by source still can. The anchor is provenance, not a truth check: with the transcript at hand, a reader can tell whether a turn still says what it said at ingest, but nothing adjudicates the belief itself. For each ingest row, the writer produces the turn anchor instead of the label-only one, never both. A belief asserted in several turns gets one anchor per turn, and re-ingesting the same file writes no second row. A turn with no timestamp of its own still gets the label-only anchor, because ingest fills that timestamp from the clock, and a re-ingest would then get a different anchor. `derive()` never reads the fingerprint, so replay equality does not depend on it. There is no backfill: existing label-only anchors stay, so a belief that already had one keeps it alongside any turn anchors that later corroborations add. `scripts/turn_anchor_census.py` counts transcript rows, turns, and reachable beliefs, and how many transcript anchors are turn or label-only. On a store written before this change, its turn count is an upper bound, because nothing on an old row records whether its timestamp came from the clock. `DerivationInput.raw_meta` is now typed `dict[str, object] | None`, which lowers the pyright baseline from 972 to 965.
- **A typed `/aelf:lock` that fails now stays visible until you apply it ([#1622](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1622)).** Since [#1626](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1626), the `UserPromptSubmit` hook runs a typed lock itself and reports a failure in that turn, but nothing reports it afterwards. The hook now records each typed lock's outcome, and `aelf doctor` and SessionStart show every failed lock that is still not applied.

  - **Recording:** the hook writes each outcome to `command_outcomes.jsonl`, next to the store, immediately after the command returns and before retrieval runs, so an exception or a timeout in retrieval can't drop the record. Each row holds the outcome reason, a SHA-256 of the statement, its length, the session, and the first 500 characters of the statement.
  - **Reporting:** `aelf doctor` has a new section, rendered on every run, that lists each lock that failed with an exception or a non-zero exit and is still not applied, with its statement and the command that fixes it. SessionStart prints one line while any such lock remains. The section is informational and doesn't change `doctor`'s exit code.
  - **No runnable fix command on Windows:** no single quoting is safe in both `cmd.exe` and PowerShell, so `aelf doctor` tells you to run `aelf lock` with the statement quoted for your shell. On other platforms it prints the command.
  - **No command for a statement it can't reproduce:** for a statement longer than 500 characters, `doctor` prints no command and asks you to run `aelf lock` with the full statement, because a command built from the prefix would lock text you never typed. For a statement that contains an unpaired surrogate, it asks you to retype it.
  - **Input errors aren't listed:** the turn already reports an empty argument, an argument over the length cap, an argument that starts with `-`, and an argument that contains an unpaired surrogate. The hook records these, but `doctor` doesn't list them.
  - **The store decides when a lock is resolved.** A gap closes when one of these holds:
    - a belief with the statement's hash is locked at user level and active, which covers a lock you apply later from the CLI;
    - the belief has a `lock:unlock` row later than the failure;
    - the belief's time-boxed lock lapsed later than the failure. The detector reads the lapse instant from the belief's `lock_expires_at`, not from the `lock:expire` row, because the sweep stamps that row when it runs, which can be long after the lock lapsed;
    - you retire or delete the belief with `aelf retire`, `aelf delete`, or the `/aelf:review` remove verdict after the failure, and it stays retired or deleted. If you run `aelf restore`, the statement is back and unlocked, so the gap is listed again;
    - you lock the statement from the CLI after the failure and then delete it. This still holds after `aelf doctor --gc-orphan-feedback --apply` removes the delete's own audit row, because the detector reads the later `aelf lock` from `ingest_log`.
  - **"Later" means a later second:** the outcome's timestamp has whole-second resolution, so a removal stamped in the same second as the failure might have come first, and the gap stays open. Timestamps are compared as instants, so the `lock:expire` format (microseconds and `+00:00`) compares correctly with the others.
  - **Unknown is reported as unknown:** `doctor` reports `unknown` and the reason, not zero, when the hook audit is disabled (`AELFRICE_HOOK_AUDIT=0` or `[hook_audit] enabled = false`), when no hook audit exists beside the store, when the outcome log can't be read (for example, a directory at its path or no read permission), when the store can't be read, and when the store is in memory. A single outcome line that isn't valid UTF-8 or can't be parsed is skipped, like a line cut off by a killed hook. A failure in this check never stops `doctor` or the SessionStart hook.
  - **Limits:** the first two can hide a gap; the next two can list a lock you already dealt with.
    - Removals are judged per belief. Retiring or deleting any one belief that carries the statement closes the gap, even if another belief with the same text stays active and unlocked.
    - A hook killed while the lock command runs (for example, at its timeout) records no outcome, so that attempt isn't listed.
    - A deleted belief is found only if `aelf lock` created it. A belief with the same text from transcript, onboard, or another ingest path has an id that depends on its source, so deleting it leaves the gap open. Retiring it does close the gap.
    - After `aelf doctor --gc-orphan-feedback --apply` removes a delete's audit row, the delete is visible only through an `aelf lock` of the statement later than the failure. If there's no such lock, the gap is listed again.
  - **Breaking for direct callers of `CommandOutcome`:** it gains a required `reason` field (`CommandReason`) and optional `command` and `argument` fields, so a two-argument `CommandOutcome(line, took_effect)` no longer constructs. Branch on `reason` to tell an input error from a failed command, instead of reading the prose line.
  - **Forward-only:** the check covers locks typed after you upgrade. Earlier failures have no recorded outcome and aren't listed.
- **A typed `/aelf:` command now runs itself, instead of depending on the model to invoke it ([#1626](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1626)).** aelfrice's promise is that memory does not depend on the model choosing to act — beliefs are injected every prompt precisely so that locked ground truth cannot be lost or ignored. A slash command broke that promise, because a slash command is a *skill*: nothing runs it unless the model decides to, and when the model does not, the command silently does not happen. A user's `/aelf:lock` was lost exactly that way; they restated the same instruction twelve times over six weeks without it binding, and `lock_level='user'` was set on one belief out of 20,176 ([#1620](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1620)). The `UserPromptSubmit` hook now parses the prompt, runs the command, and writes the belief, verified end to end through the real entry point. **The allowlist is narrow, and the boundary is "additive and idempotent" rather than "useful":** `lock`, `confirm`, `promote`, `scope-out`. Read-only commands are absent because the model running `aelf search` or not costs nothing durable. Destructive and environment-changing commands — `delete`, `retire`, `unlock`, `restore`, `uninstall`, `upgrade`, `setup` — are absent and must stay absent: running one off raw prompt text that no human confirmed, and which the model may merely be quoting, is a worse failure than the one being closed. Seven are asserted inert with arguments attached, because three of them used to be tested bare and were refused by the empty-argument guard rather than by the allowlist — passing on the wrong rule. The executable set itself is pinned so the list cannot go vacuous by deletion. The hook is **authoritative**, and it is authoritative in both directions: on success it injects a line telling the model the command already ran, so the command runs exactly once from the path that cannot forget — and on a refusal, a missing argument, an exception or a non-zero exit it injects a *different* block saying the command did **not** take effect. A single unconditional frame was #1620's own failure class reproduced inside its fix: nothing had been written, yet the model was told the work was done and instructed not to redo it, which disabled the one remaining fallback for exactly the cases that needed it. **`/aelf:scope-out` also had to move.** The executor originally ran ahead of `is_session_first_prompt`, which is the only writer of the key `aelf scope-out` resolves its target session from — so the exclusion attached to whichever session most recently *started*, `load_exclusions` returned `[]` on the mismatch, and the model was told it had applied. On a session's genuine first prompt the state file did not exist yet and the command failed outright, then the same turn created the file, so it worked on the retry and looked like a flake. This was a regression introduced by executing early: a model-invoked `aelf scope-out` necessarily ran after the hook. The session stamp is now taken first. A prompt that merely *mentions* a command is inert. **An argument over `COMMAND_ARGUMENT_CAP` is refused, not truncated** — the CLI has always accepted an arbitrarily long statement, but nothing confirms a Bash call any more, so a 200,000-character paste beginning with `/aelf:lock` reached the highest-trust tier in one turn and could crowd the whole lock budget; truncating instead would store something the user never typed. **The argument is the first line only** — an earlier revision matched under `re.DOTALL`, so `/aelf:lock Always use uv.` followed by any further prose locked the entire message as one user-locked belief, re-injected as standing ground truth on every later turn; typing a command and then continuing the message is the most natural thing a user does. "First line" means every terminator `str.splitlines` recognises, not just `\n`: the first fix for this excluded `\n` alone, which left the entire defect reachable from a CR-only client, from `\v`/`\f`, and from the Unicode separators `\x1c`-`\x1e`, NEL, U+2028 and U+2029 — ten of the eleven terminators still swallowed the rest of the prompt, so the fix closed one case out of eleven and read as if it had closed all of them. **The argument may not begin with `-`**, because `argv` is `[command, argument]` and a leading dash reaches argparse: `/aelf:lock --help` emitted 2,265 characters of usage onto this hook's stdout protocol channel, outside the character cap and outside the writer enumeration meant to prevent exactly that, and `/aelf:lock --advanced` exited 0 and reported a lock that never happened. The CLI call additionally runs under a stdout redirect, because argparse writes to the real `sys.stdout` and that stream *is* the protocol. Failure is loud on every path — a missing argument, a flag, an exception, and a non-zero exit each report to stderr and to the model — while a clean `SystemExit(0)` is no longer mis-reported as failure, which `int(exc.code or 1)` used to do. The executor never raises, because a command must not cost the prompt its memory injection, and that is asserted rather than assumed. A blind adversarial review then retracted three claims made here, and each retraction is recorded rather than quietly fixed. "Seventeen mutations, all killed" was **false**: removing the stdout redirect and dropping the install's `and not removed_superseded` term both left the suite green. The redirect arm was vacuous for a specific reason worth naming — two guards covered one path, so mutating either was survived by the other, and the leak is only reachable with both gone. "Never raises" was also false; the executor raises if its own `stderr` is closed, so the caller keeps its guard and the docstring now says so. And the cap's stated justification, that `aelf lock` echoes the statement back, was wrong in three places: `aelf lock` prints `locked: <id>`, 25 characters whatever the statement's length, which is exactly why the first cap arm proved nothing. A second blind pass then upheld all eight claims but found four more things worth fixing before merge, two of which contradicted rules this change had just written down. **The report now echoes what was stored**, because `/aelf:lock Always use uv` followed by U+2028 and more prose stores `Always use uv` and `aelf lock` prints only `locked: <id>` — so a sentence that looked like one line to the person typing it was silently halved. The length cap can refuse, since "too long" is decidable; "they meant this to be one line" is not, so the answer there is to show the result rather than guess at it. **A mistyped command name is no longer a command**: `[a-z0-9-]*` stops at the first character outside the class, so `/aelf:lockFOO bar` parsed as `lock` with argument `FOO bar` and wrote it at the highest trust tier. No destructive command was ever reachable that way — none has an allowlisted prefix — but a user-locked belief from a typo is its own defect. **The writer-enumeration gate now keys on the block's tag, not only on the variable written**, which matters because this change is what first put two blocks under one key: the executed and failed frames both write `command_note`, the table recorded the first tag alone, and a third block reusing that local passed the gate while the ceiling docstring claimed a sixth writer would red it. And a hand-edited settings file that stores an inner hook as a bare string rather than an object now has its superseded entry retired too. The hook's import of `cli` **widened an existing import cycle**, which CodeQL caught. The first version of this note claimed the change *created* the cycle and that removing one edge closed it. Both claims were wrong, and the measurement is recorded here because the wrong version was written twice. `hook` was already cyclic on main, with `provenance_render`, alongside three other pre-existing cycles; what this change did was grow hook's strongly-connected component from two modules to five, because the live path runs `hook -> cli -> doctor -> hook` and never touched the edge that was removed:

    main   : hook SCC = 2  {hook, provenance_render}
    branch : hook SCC = 5  {cli, doctor, hook, hook_search_tool, provenance_render}

The edge that *was* removed is still worth removing on its own merits, and is kept for those: `cli` imported `AELFRICE_SESSIONSTART_RECAP` from `hook` only to interpolate its name into a help string, and a string constant belongs in a leaf module rather than in an eight-thousand-line one. It now lives in `aelfrice.env_names`, which imports nothing from `aelfrice` by construction, and `cli` no longer imports `hook` at all — asserted as an absence rather than as "not at module scope", since permitting the lazy form is how this accumulated. `hook` reads the name through a function rather than re-exporting it, because a module-scope import would take the modules loaded by `import aelfrice.hook` from 18 to 19, a ceiling pinned deliberately since every process importing the hook pays it; A/B over 9 fresh interpreters put that at ~1.0 ms median, inside the run-to-run spread and so not cleanly separable from noise, but not worth spending on a constant with one reader. **Breaking hook out of the cycle is not attempted here** and is tracked in [#1631](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1631): it needs `provenance_render` and `doctor` to stop importing from `hook`, which is four modules and not this change's subject. **Thirty-two mutations now run and all thirty-two fail the suite** — seventeen original, eleven for the first review round, four for the second, each killed by the test written for it rather than by a neighbour.
- **`aelf search` now runs before the web search tools, not just the local ones ([#1626](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1626)).** The value of the search hook is *ordering*: aelfrice runs first, so the model already holds the relevant brain-graph context before it chooses grep, the web, or anything else. A search the model runs afterwards, or not at all, is worth much less. Coverage was partial — `SEARCH_TOOL_MATCHER` was `Grep|Glob` plus a separate `Bash` matcher — so a model reaching for `WebSearch` or `WebFetch` proceeded with no brain-graph context at all, and whether it took that path was exactly the model choice aelfrice exists to remove. Both now trigger the lane, and the query comes from each tool's own field: `pattern` for Grep and Glob, `query` for WebSearch, and `prompt` for WebFetch. The prompt, not the URL: a bare URL tokenises into host fragments that match nothing useful, so searching it would satisfy the matcher while defeating the point. Widening is bounded — `Read`, `Write`, `Edit` and `TodoWrite` are asserted *not* to trigger the lane, so every tool call does not now pay for retrieval, and the web tools join the uncapped Grep/Glob path rather than the Bash per-turn fire cap. **Upgrading an existing install no longer leaves a duplicate hook entry.** `_install_or_replace_entry` keys on `(command, matcher)`, so widening the matcher appended a second entry rather than replacing the first; both then matched Grep and Glob, firing the hook twice per call — two store opens, two retrievals, and the locked block injected twice, since `filter_against_ring` lets locked beliefs through as new every time. `prune_broken_aelf_hooks` did not clean it either, because the entry was superseded rather than broken, and **every existing install was the affected case**. Installation now retires entries on a superseded matcher first, scoped to this hook's own command, so a user's own `PreToolUse` entry on the same matcher is left alone and a changed install prefix cannot strand the old one. Two corrections came out of review, because the first version of this claim was broader than the code. It compared with `Path(command).name`, which is *not* this project's ownership rule: it keeps a trailing argument, keeps a closing quote, and on Windows keeps the `.EXE` suffix, and in each of those shapes the old entry quietly survived. It now uses `_command_basename` and `launcher.command_program_keys`, the pair the rest of the module already uses, hardened for exactly those shapes by #1412 and #1482 — and deliberately asymmetric, most-specific on the path this project resolved and any-reading on a command someone else may have written. It was also all-or-nothing per entry: a settings file that grouped our hook and a user's onto one matcher kept the superseded entry, so "no longer leaves a duplicate" was untrue for those installs. Retirement is now granular to the inner hook — ours is dropped out of the list and the entry survives if anything of theirs does. A third gap was reachable and untested: with both the old and the new entry present, install saw the new matcher already there and returned before writing, throwing the retirement away, so a half-upgraded settings file never converged. Nine tests transcribed the matcher as a literal `"Grep|Glob"` and went stale the moment it widened; they now derive `SEARCH_TOOL_MATCHER` from the source, because a transcribed constant is a second copy free to drift from the one that ships.
- **`aelf doctor core-gate` drains the core admission gate's backlog ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** The backlog is the active, unlocked beliefs that meet the core rule without the gate and have no label under the current classifier version. `aelf doctor core-gate` counts the core candidates with each label and with none, and writes no batch or label. With `--emit`, it splits the backlog evenly, in belief id order, into as few batches of up to 50 beliefs as it can, with sizes that differ by at most one (151 beliefs make 38, 38, 38, and 37, not 50, 50, 50, and 1, because a one-snippet batch can only score a C share of 0 or 1 and would trip the self-check). It records each batch and prints each batch's id, its full classifier prompt, and the `aelf core-gate accept <batch-id>` command that takes the model's reply. `--out DIR` writes each prompt to a file instead, `--limit N` prints at most N batches, and `--json` prints the result as one object. Each emit is its own run for the accept self-check, so emitting with a small `--limit` and accepting between emits gives runs of fewer than four batches, which the check skips; the `--limit` output advises accepting all batches of one emit before emitting again. The batch records are the only writes, and the whole emit, reads included, runs under one write lock, so two emits at once can't batch the same beliefs twice. If `--out` can't write a prompt file, the command exits 1, but the recorded batches stay and the next emit prints them again.

  An emit doesn't batch a belief twice. If a batch from an earlier emit isn't accepted yet, and all of its beliefs are still unlabeled, unchanged, and in core, the next emit prints that batch again, with the same id and prompt. Otherwise the emit sets the batch aside and puts its unlabeled beliefs in a new batch. A set-aside batch stays open, since its beliefs can return to the backlog, for example when a lock is lifted. Accepting it is harmless, because each label is keyed to the content the model judged, but it replaces a label a newer batch wrote for the same belief, and that label then counts toward the set-aside batch's emit run in the self-check.

  `aelf core-gate accept` now runs the spec's self-check after it accepts a batch from `aelf doctor core-gate --emit`. Over the accepted batches of the same emit run, it names on stderr each batch whose share of C labels differs by more than 0.25 from the median share of the other batches. The check reports only: the labels stay cached, and the exit code is unchanged. All the batches of one emit share one creation time, which identifies the run, so the check needs no new column. The check runs only when the run has at least four accepted batches; with three, each batch's median of the others is the mean of two shares, so one failed batch flags the healthy ones too. Below four, the command says on stderr that it skipped the check.

  `aelf doctor core-gate --rerun <batch-id>` re-runs one accepted batch, which is the spec's step 2 for a flagged batch. In one transaction, it deletes the labels the batch still owns, so a label that a later batch wrote for the same belief stays, and batches again the beliefs whose labels it deleted and that are still in core and unchanged. The new batch carries the original creation time, so it stays in the original emit run. Until it's accepted, the re-run beliefs have no label and follow today's core rule, including the ones the batch had labeled C, and the command's output says so. When every belief is kept, the new batch would derive the original's id, so the command reopens the original batch instead, and the self-check checks it as usual. When some beliefs drop out, the smaller batch is left out of the self-check: it isn't checked when accepted, and it isn't a sibling for the run's other batches, because it isn't comparable with its full-size siblings, and the operator ruled it out on 2026-10-05. A subset re-run is recognized as a batch whose content hashes are a strict subset of another batch's in the same run, so no new column is needed and the store-open statement counts are unchanged. It prints the batch like `--emit` does, and supports `--out` and `--json`. It exits 1 and writes nothing for an unknown batch, one never accepted, one from an earlier classifier version, or a batch that owns no labels any more, and exits 2 when combined with `--emit` or `--limit`.

  The graph report of `aelf doctor`, and `aelf health`, now include a `core admission gate` block with the same counts as `aelf doctor core-gate`, and `aelf health --json` adds them under a `core_gate` key. The counts never change the exit code. Counting them walks every belief, and no read the graph report already makes carries what the core rule needs. On one machine, on a synthetic 20,000-belief store, the walk took 0.24 seconds, and `aelf health` took 0.51 seconds instead of 0.25.
- **The core admission gate's classifier rubric and prompt ship in `aelfrice.core_gate` ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** The module holds the A, B, and C rubric that labels a core candidate as a self-contained claim, a claim that needs context, or not a claim. It also holds the batch prompt the host runs and a strict parser for the reply. The definitions and decision rules are the ones the reference raters used, word for word. Only the examples are new. `CLASSIFIER_VERSION` is derived from a digest of the assembled prompt, the label set, the batch limit, the model tier, and the source of the prompt builder and the reply parser. Any change to them is a new version, and cached labels from the old one stop applying, with nothing to bump by hand. A test pins the current digest so such a change is deliberate. The parser rejects a reply as a whole unless it labels every snippet in the batch exactly once, so a failed run can't pass for labels. Core selection, `aelf core-gate accept`, `aelf doctor core-gate`, and the Stop hook's session-end batch use the module.
- **The Stop hook runs the core admission gate's session-end batch ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** "Session end" means after the session's beliefs are ingested. On a Stop, the transcript logger starts a background ingest of the session's turns once 12 turn lines have built up, by default, and the batch fires on the first Stop that finds the ingested beliefs, so at most once per ingest, not once per session (operator ruling, 2026-10-06). The host runs a Stop's hooks in parallel, so the ingest usually lands after that Stop's hook has looked, and the batch fires a turn later; a session's last ingest can land after its last Stop, and the backlog drain covers those beliefs. On every Stop, the hook runs one indexed query for the session's core candidates: active, unlocked beliefs whose `session_id` is this session, that meet the core rule without the gate, that have no label under the current classifier version, and whose content hash no open batch claims. An open batch claims a hash when it is under the current version and is either this session's own session-end batch or a doctor batch, so a Stop never asks about a belief an earlier Stop asked about. If there is at least one candidate, the hook records one batch with origin `session_end`, holding the newest candidates that fit, up to 50 beliefs and 9,500 characters, and writes `{"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "Stop", "additionalContext": ...}}` to stdout. The host inlines at most 10,000 characters of a hook's output, and the hook never cuts a snippet, because the label is keyed to the whole content; a candidate that doesn't fit waits for the next Stop or the backlog drain. The host continues the conversation once with the context, which holds the classifier prompt and the `aelf core-gate accept <batch-id>` command to run with the reply on stdin. The query, the batch record, and the context are one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, so two Stops at once can't batch the same belief and a failure before the commit leaves no batch behind. The context is written after the commit, so the hook never asks about a batch that doesn't exist; a failure between the two, such as a closed pipe or a host timeout, leaves an open batch nobody was asked about, whose beliefs later Stops don't ask about either. Those, and the beliefs in a batch the model declined, stay unlabeled and keep today's rule until `aelf doctor core-gate --emit` batches them again; the hook doesn't retry, because a retry window would also re-ask a model that declined on purpose, and the drain already covers every case. aelfrice calls no model.

  The context asks the host to use its smallest model, but that's a request, not a guarantee. If a larger model labels the batch, the labels are kept under the same classifier version, and nothing checks them automatically: the accept self-check runs on doctor batches only. To relabel an accepted session-end batch, run `aelf doctor core-gate --rerun <batch-id>` (operator ruling, 2026-10-06). It drops only the labels that still carry the batch's id and puts the beliefs still in core into a new doctor batch, stamped with the time of the re-run, which starts an emit run of its own. A session-end batch belongs to no emit run, so the new batch joins none; as a run of one batch, it's below the self-check's minimum of four, and the command says so. Beliefs from earlier sessions that reach core in this one, and beliefs in a session-end batch nobody answered, stay unlabeled and keep the earlier rule until `aelf doctor core-gate --emit` batches them.

  The hook writes nothing while the host's `stop_hook_active` is set, in a headless session ([#1634](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1634)), or on the Codex host, which documents `decision` for Stop but not `additionalContext`. The batch is on by default. To turn it off, set `[core_gate] session_end = false` in `.aelfrice.toml` or export `AELFRICE_CORE_GATE_SESSION_END=0`; the environment variable overrides the key in both directions. The lock-prompt listing still goes to stderr, and a failure in the batch is logged to stderr without affecting the listing or the hook's exit code. On a synthetic store of 5,200 beliefs with a 200-belief session, half of which never qualify for core, a Stop that finds nothing new took about 1 ms longer than before (median, in process); the query re-reads the session's beliefs that never qualify on every Stop, at about 6 µs each. The query parses the items of open doctor batches only when they were created at or after the session's earliest belief, since an older batch can't hold the session's beliefs; the timestamps are compared as instants with `julianday()`, because the hook's `...Z` form and the `+00:00` form `--emit` writes don't order correctly as strings. Measured on the session-end path alone, with the session's 200 beliefs already batched and open doctor batches of 50 items: with no doctor batches, 0.28 ms before the bound and 0.29 ms after; with 100 batches older than the session, 5.0 ms before and 0.33 ms after; with 1,000 older, 57.9 ms before and 1.1 ms after. Batches newer than the session are still parsed: 5.1 ms for 100 and 57.1 ms for 1,000, which needs a drain run during the session to leave that many open.
- **The core admission gate's label cache and `aelf core-gate accept` ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** Two new tables hold the gate's state. `core_gate_labels` caches one A, B, or C label per belief content hash and classifier version, so a label never applies to changed content or under a changed classifier. `core_gate_batches` records each classifier batch that is emitted, under an id derived from the classifier version, the creation time, and the batch's content hashes. Both tables are additive: a fresh store has them, and an existing store gains them the next time it opens. The hidden `aelf core-gate accept <batch-id>` command reads the host model's reply from stdin, checks it with the strict `core_gate` parser, and caches the labels in one transaction. It exits 1 and writes nothing if the batch is unknown, already accepted, or from an earlier classifier version, or if the reply doesn't label every snippet exactly once. `aelf doctor core-gate --emit` and the Stop hook's session-end batch emit batches.

  The two tables add two statements to every store open. At the repo-store layout, a settled store's writable open now issues 89 statements instead of 87: 58 `CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS` instead of 56, a DDL battery of 60 instead of 58, and 84 statements that a read-only handle avoids instead of 82. Outside the repo-store layout, the open issues 88 instead of 86. On a store with one expired lock, it issues 97 instead of 95. The [#1561](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1561) entry in the 5.0.0 notes keeps the counts measured at that release, and the markers that bound those counts to `benchmarks/store_open_cost.py` move here, re-derived.
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/store_open_cost.py#statements = 89 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/store_open_cost.py#creates = 58 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/store_open_cost.py#ddl_statements = 60 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/store_open_cost.py#avoidable_statements = 84 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/store_open_cost.py#no_repo_identity_statements = 88 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/store_open_cost.py#expired_lock_statements = 97 -->
- **Automatic phantom promotion on your own evidence, off by default ([#1650](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1650)).** This is the first three parts of the plan in `docs/design/feature-supports-writer.md`:
  - **Speaker on corroborations:** `belief_corroborations` gains a nullable `speaker` column. Transcript ingest fills it from the turn's role; every other source, and every earlier row, leaves it empty. A later write from a user turn upgrades an existing row to `user`.
  - **`evidence_promoted` origin:** a phantom promoted automatically gets this origin. It ranks with `user_transcript` in retrieval and in contradiction precedence, below `user_validated`, and renders with the inferred tier. `aelf demote` returns it to `speculative`, and the rule never promotes that phantom again.
  - **`[phantom_promotion] auto_promote` / `AELFRICE_PHANTOM_AUTO_PROMOTE`:** when it's on, a phantom is promoted once it has at least 3 supports from turns you typed, across at least 2 sessions. Each support must be recorded after the phantom was created and come from a session other than the one that created it. The phantom also needs no CONTRADICTS edge and no negative feedback. A support is a complete restatement: ingest stores what you type one sentence at a time, and a phantom is usually a paragraph, so a session counts only when you typed every sentence of the phantom that ingest would store as yours, each matched by exact text. A phantom with anything outside those sentences other than whitespace and a short list of ASCII structure marks, such as list markers and sentence punctuation, is never promoted automatically, because promotion keeps its whole text. Each turn promotes up to five phantoms and adds a notice with the undo command.

  The spec's first version assumed a restatement corroborates the phantom directly. It doesn't, because a phantom's identity comes from wonder's inputs rather than its text, and the spec now says so. SUPPORTS edges from reworded restatements will count once their writer exists. Nothing changes until you turn the switch on.
- **Aelfrice flags an ignored `~/.aelfrice.toml` ([#1652](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1652)).** Since #1582, aelfrice never reads a per-user config, so settings kept there stopped applying with no message. When `~/.aelfrice.toml` exists and the project has no `.aelfrice.toml`:
  - **SessionStart hook:** adds one line to the agent's context naming the file, saying it's ignored, and giving the fix (copy it to the project's root). It goes on stdout, like the failed-lock notice, because a hook's stderr reaches only the host's debug log when the hook succeeds. It resolves the project from the session's working directory and doesn't repeat after a compaction.
  - **`aelf doctor`:** reports the same as a warning, which doesn't change its exit code.

  Both stay quiet when the project has its own config. `docs/user/CONFIG.md` now also explains that a linked work tree reads only the `.aelfrice.toml` at its own root.
- **`aelf doctor --repair-utc-created-at` rewrites the offset-form dates that onboard stored before #1611, and re-chains the temporal spine ([#1660](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1660)).** Before [#1611](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1611), onboard stored each commit date with its author's local offset, such as `2026-01-01T10:00:00-08:00`. Next to `Z` timestamps, text order then differs from real order, and the temporal spine links some beliefs backwards in time. The command reports the affected beliefs, the sessions they're in, and the spine edges a re-chain would remove and write. It changes nothing unless you add `--apply`. With `--apply`, it rewrites each value as UTC, such as `2026-01-01T18:00:00Z`, and rebuilds the spine of those sessions only. It also rewrites the matching ingest-log timestamps, so rebuilding beliefs from the log doesn't bring the offsets back. A session whose spine you cleared stays cleared, and prose-derived `TEMPORAL_NEXT` edges are kept. Values already in UTC (`Z` or `+00:00`) and values that don't parse are left as they are, so a store with no offset dates is left untouched. The command holds the store's write lock while it runs, about 0.3 seconds for 12,000 rows.
- **`aelf doctor --gc-filesystem-corroboration` removes the corroboration rows that re-onboarding wrote before #1615 ([#1669](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1669)).** A store that was onboarded or scanned more than once before [#1615](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1615) still holds a corroboration row for every paragraph each rescan read again, and those rows still count toward `aelf core` and the `corr=` value on injected beliefs. The command reports how many rows there are, how many beliefs they sit on, and which beliefs would leave `aelf core` without them. It changes nothing unless you add `--apply`, which deletes those rows and leaves every other source's rows as they are. The dry run measures the core change by running the delete and rolling it back, so it reports what `--apply` would do, not an estimate.

### Changed

- **Core selection applies the core admission gate ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** `aelf core`, the SessionStart `<core>` lane, and the core membership that `aelf doctor --gc-filesystem-corroboration` reports now read each candidate's cached label under the current classifier version, in one batched lookup per selection rather than one per belief. The gate applies only to the corroboration and posterior arms; locked beliefs aren't gated. A belief with label A is admitted. A belief with label B is admitted only if fewer than two of its corroborations count under the [#1635](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1635) episode rule: its corroboration count when its sightings span two or more episodes, and zero otherwise. A belief with label C is never admitted. A belief with no label under the current version follows the earlier rule, so nothing leaves core before it's classified. Core changes only as labels are accepted.

  `aelf core` text output doesn't show labels. With `--json`, the row for a labeled belief gains a `core_gate_label` field, and a row without a label has no such field. A store written before the label table existed and opened read-only reads as having no labels.

  The `--gc-filesystem-corroboration` report now also lists the beliefs that would enter core once the rows are deleted, on a line that appears only when there are any. Deleting rows can admit a belief: a B-labeled belief can fall below two corroborations, and removing one sighting can join two short gaps into one gap long enough to start a second episode.
- **Praise now raises the confidence of the beliefs behind the answer by default; complaints still need an opt-in ([#1647](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1647)).** Sentiment-from-prose feedback was off by default. Short replies were measured on 5,398 logged prompts (2,999 of them not harness records), with a random sample of 200 labelled by two blind graders (they agreed on 191 of 200, Cohen's kappa 0.82):
  - Positive matches ("perfect", "looks good") were 95–98% precise.
  - Negative matches were 58–64% precise. A bare "no", "fix it", and "try again" mostly open a new instruction rather than judge the answer.

  **Positive half:** `[feedback] sentiment_from_prose` now defaults to `true`, so praise applies with no setup. Setting it to `false`, or `AELFRICE_FEEDBACK_SENTIMENT_FROM_PROSE=0`, still turns the whole lane off.

  **Negative half:** complaints need a new key, `[feedback] sentiment_negative = true`, or `AELFRICE_FEEDBACK_SENTIMENT_NEGATIVE=1`. The negative patterns were tightened:
  - "no" counts only when it opens the prompt;
  - "wrong" counts only as a verdict;
  - "still wrong", "still broken", and similar are matched;
  - "fix it" and "try again" are gone.

  On 34 graded negative matches the tuning never saw (35 drawn; one was unclear to both graders), the tightened patterns scored 73.5% and 61.8%. The pre-registered bar was 70% on both graders, and one fell short. That's why negatives stay opt-in.

  The narrowing costs recall: the tightened patterns keep 57 of the 116 negative matches the old ones made, and some lost ones were real verdicts, for example "no" after an image reference ("[Image #1] no, that's still the old version"). Four prompts that used to match a negative pattern now match a positive one instead. Positive precision was measured under the old pattern set; it was not measured again. A negative match with the key off moves nothing, but still lands in the hook audit marked `negative_disabled`, so the lane can be measured again on fresh prompts.

  **Harness records:** the detector no longer scores them, such as a background task's notice, as your reaction.

  **CI audit:** `benchmarks/posterior_channel_audit.py` now asserts the new defaults: the positive lane on, the negative lane off.

  If you had opted in with `sentiment_from_prose = true` and want complaints to keep counting, also set `sentiment_negative = true`.
- **Commit ingest now runs from a git `post-commit` hook, so it sees your commits ([#1698](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1698)).**
  - **Before:** commit ingest was a `PostToolUse:Bash` hook. It acted only on a command that started with `git commit`, and it read the commit hash from the `[branch hash]` line that `git commit -q` doesn't print. In this project's sessions it ingested about 2 of 399 commits ([#1683](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1683)).
  - **Now:** `aelf setup` adds a marked block to the current repository's `post-commit` hook, shared by every worktree. It removes the old `PostToolUse` entry from both the user and the project settings. Git runs the block after `git commit`, `cherry-pick`, and `revert`, so quiet, chained, scripted, worktree, and `-C` commits are each recorded, in that repository's store. A merge that commits by itself and `git am` don't run it.
  - **Never blocks a commit:** the block names the exact commit, then runs the ingest in the background. It adds about 70 ms per commit. Errors go to `commit-ingest.log` next to the store.
  - **Skipped commits:** commits that `git rebase` replays are skipped, since each was ingested when first made. So is a commit you make while a rebase is paused, and a `git revert` commit.
  - **Session key:** a commit's session is keyed on its parent and author date, which an amend keeps. A commit session never corroborates a belief it created itself, so an amend adds only its new phrases.
  - **Existing hooks:** setup edits an existing `post-commit` only when it can restore it byte for byte. It installs only into a hooks directory inside the repository's own git directory, refusing a shared or tracked `core.hooksPath`, and prints the lines to add by hand instead.
  - **To record a repository's commits:** run `aelf setup` inside it. The upgrade-time auto-installer no longer adds the `PostToolUse` entry. `aelf setup --host codex` is unchanged. Removing aelfrice takes the block out of the current repository only.

### Removed

- **Three v2.0 bench gates graded code that does not exist, and they are retired rather than left in the denominator ([#1579](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1579)).** The bench-gate tier scaffolded 20 modules reporting "no verdict", which reads as *unfunded labelling*. For `dedup`, `enforcement`, and `promotion_trigger` it was not: there is no function to grade, so no corpus of any size could produce a verdict, and the count overstated how much of the tier a labelling push could recover. The denominator is now 17 and every module in it names work a corpus would actually unblock. **`enforcement` was dropped, not deferred.** `docs/design/v2_enforcement.md` records the disposition in its status line — "H2 dropped per § H2 below" — and § H2 states the wording is deliberate: "Final decision: drop, not defer. Removing the temptation to revisit 'just port the safe predicates' prevents incremental erosion of the security stance." The dropped predicate set included `command_succeeds:cmd`, which would have executed arbitrary shell from belief content with no allowlist and no sandbox. The gate was scaffolding for a capability the project decided on security grounds not to ship, so keeping it implied a reopening that is not coming. **`promotion_trigger` graded the rule [#229](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/229) rejected, and the rule it ratified is already covered on every public CI pass.** #229 ratified that a phantom's origin advances only through explicit user acknowledgment, and it named three count-based triggers — N positive feedback events, retrieval count, and `user_corrected` adjacency — as *non-triggers*. That rule shipped at v2.1 via [#550](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/550): Surface A is `aelf promote <id>`, and Surface B auto-promotes a phantom whose text matches an `aelf lock` statement (`aelfrice.promotion.find_phantom_lock_matches`, Jaccard >= 0.9). The retired gate graded `promotion_trigger.decide(row["belief_sequence"])` against `should_promote` / `should_not` labels — a function that infers promotion from a sequence of beliefs, which is the shape #229 ruled out. A perfectly labelled corpus would therefore have certified the rejected design, not the shipped one. `tests/test_promotion_adversarial.py` covers Surface B on every public CI run, rather than once per release cut. Do not read `find_promotable_phantoms(min_corroborations=..., min_sessions=...)` as the #229 trigger: that method is [#1132](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1132) Q2, and its docstring states it is a read-only detector that never promotes, because a corroboration count is the non-trigger. **`dedup` was the live landmine.** Its guard caught `ModuleNotFoundError`, but `aelfrice.dedup` imports — the module is real, and ships `jaccard`, `levenshtein_ratio`, `cluster_pairs`, `find_near_duplicate_locks`, and `dedup_audit`. What it has never had is `classify`, which the gate called on the next line, so the first row mounted under `<corpus>/dedup/` would have turned a clean skip into an `AttributeError` at a release cut. There is a second mismatch underneath: the corpus label set is ternary (`duplicate` / `near-duplicate` / `distinct`) while the shipped predicate is one binary threshold, and nobody has defined the `near-duplicate` band — so anyone labelling rows today would be labelling to a boundary that does not exist. If `dedup` is ever revived, define the band and write `classify` first, then re-mount rows; do not author new ones.

  The reason this cost anything is that nothing failed at authoring time. The whole tier skips without `AELFRICE_CORPUS_ROOT`, so a gate against a module that was never written is indistinguishable from a gate waiting on rows, and the bill arrives at the release cut — the one place [#1477](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1477) made the tier mandatory, and where [#1502](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1502) already showed that a check nobody can turn green teaches readers to skip the reds beside it. `tests/test_bench_gate_graded_code_exists_1579.py` closes that: it walks every file under `tests/bench_gate/`, asserts each `aelfrice.*` module it imports has a file under `src/aelfrice/`, and asserts each attribute read off one of those modules exists on it. The second assertion is the one `dedup` needed — an importable module is not a gradable one, and a module-existence check alone passes the exact case that broke. Shadowing is resolved per scope rather than per file, so one unrelated `dedup = {...}` inside a helper cannot disarm the attribute check for the rest of the file. A third check pins the three retired names out of the tier, but only while the function their gates called is still missing, so the revival route `docs/design/dedup.md` prescribes retires the pin instead of tripping it. It all runs unmarked and corpus-free, so the next scaffold-before-code gate reds on its own pull request. Mutation-checked five ways: a planted gate importing `aelfrice.nonexistent_module` reds the module check; one importing a missing name off the package reds the member check; one reproducing the `dedup` shape — a real module, an absent attribute — reds the attribute check, and still reds with an unrelated same-name local assignment elsewhere in the file; a planted file in a subdirectory of the tier reds too, which it does not under a non-recursive glob; and re-adding `tests/bench_gate/test_dedup.py` reds the pin until a hand-added `dedup.classify` turns it into a skip.
- **The relevance sweeper and the two knobs it fed ([#1655](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1655)).** The detector was meant to mark which injected beliefs an answer used. It had marked 0 of 1,684 scored injections, because it needed the whole belief to appear verbatim in the reply. Looser word-overlap matchers scored only 1.3 to 1.4 times a random-session null, which measures topicality, not use. Nothing consumed the signal, and the sweeper scanned the transcript on every prompt, which took 40 to 50 ms. Removed:
  - the sweeper, `relevance_detection`, the two store methods only it called, and the `aelf doctor` close-the-loop section;
  - the #760 expansion-gate token-threshold knob (`AELFRICE_META_BELIEF_EXPANSION_GATE_TOKEN_THRESHOLD`), so the gate uses its fixed threshold of 80, and `should_run_expansion` no longer takes `store` or `now_ts`;
  - the #758 posterior-temperature knob (`AELFRICE_META_BELIEF_POSTERIOR_TEMPERATURE`), so the γ rerank uses `T = 1.0`.

  Neither knob was ever enabled, and both learned only from the relevance signal. Retrieval output is byte-identical to before on 84 queries across 5 entry points, with the γ rerank on and off; the method and the measurements are in the #1655 comments. `injection_events` rows are still recorded, because exploration reads them, now with an empty consumer list. The #756 half-life and #757 anchor-weight knobs keep their own evidence paths.

### Fixed

- **Generated Codex skills invoke `aelf` directly instead of wrapping it in `uv run` ([#1413](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1413)).** Every `$aelf-*` skill `aelf setup --host codex` generates carried the `uv run aelf …` form — 96 occurrences across all 31 skills — which added a writable-cache requirement to a CLI that is already on `PATH`. `uv run` initializes and locks its cache before aelfrice starts, so in a Codex sandbox whose cache is read-only the skill fails at the wrapper: measured on this host, `uv run aelf --version` exits 2 with ``failed to open file `…/CACHEDIR.TAG`: Permission denied`` where `aelf --version` exits 0.
