It also resolves a project environment, so an unactivated checkout holding a different aelfrice version silently shadowed the `uv tool` install that generated the skill. `codex_skill_from_slash` now rewrites the invocation at transform time, which is where it belongs: the bundled slash bodies are shared with the default host, where `uv run` is correct for a source checkout, so the bundle is unchanged and only the Codex port is rewritten. No absolute path is baked in — the skill resolves `aelf` through the invoking shell's `PATH`, so one generated bundle stays valid across machines and across the POSIX and Windows shim families. **This deliberately ends the "body carried through verbatim" invariant**, which `tests/test_codex_skills.py` and `docs/user/SLASH_COMMANDS.md` both asserted; the amended test pins that the rewrite is the *only* difference, so nothing else in the body can be edited, reordered, or dropped unnoticed. Existing Codex installs keep their old skill text until you re-run `aelf setup --host codex`; `aelf doctor --host codex` already reports that case as a fault naming the skills and the command to run.
- **The budget census's containment guard had no mutation coverage on three of its six lanes, and its inertness disclosure named the wrong lane ([#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546)).** `tests/test_budget_census.py` drives the census against a fake packer that returns less at a higher budget, and asserted only that the pooled violation list was non-empty. The fake fired at `token_budget >= 700`, which is below the entire grid of three lanes — `ups` runs 750-3000, `retrieval_default` 1200-4800 and `rebuilder` 2000-8000 — so on those it shrank the probe and every arm by the same item, the comparison stayed consistent, and no violation could arise; the three lanes it could not mutate were carried by the three it could and read as covered. The threshold now sits above every arm and below the probe, and the assertion is per lane. Separately, `NOT_EXERCISED["graph edges"]` claimed the cluster packer was inert on an edgeless store; `pack_with_clusters` in fact runs on every query of every cell with singleton clusters — 2,208 calls in a full run, all of them packing — so the non-monotone fill the module docstring flags is live on every measured cell, K0's included. A new arm pins what the absent edges were hiding: containment is a property of this corpus, not a theorem. With one `TEMPORAL_NEXT` edge an arm reaches a belief the unbudgeted probe did not, because the temporal spine seeds from `l1_packed[:DEFAULT_SPINE_SEED_COUNT]` and `l1_packed` at a low budget is not a prefix of itself at a high one, so a cheap belief lands inside the seed window and its off-lexicon neighbour escapes. Published K0 figures are unchanged — `n=7`, `ec_pp` 0.0 on all six lanes, `violations` 0 — because the committed corpora carry no edges.
- **A user lock inside a `<cadence-resume>` recap is a lock again, and a reference lock's only element render survives the trim ([#1570](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1570)).** `hook.py` recognised a user lock by the literal `lock="user"`; `context_rebuilder.py` renders one `locked="true"`. The two spellings never met on the ordinary hook path, so nothing had failed — but the [#871](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/871) recap is rebuilder output spliced into a hook-rendered envelope, and there the ceiling's dropper could not see that an element was a lock. [#1564](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1564)'s lock exemption therefore never fired on shipped output, and the [#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379) always-injected contract did not hold on that path. `hook._element_is_locked` reads both spellings now. **The hook was widened and the rebuilder was left alone**, for two reasons: the rebuilder's attribute is a published output schema with consumers this module does not own — the `<aelfrice-rebuild>` block the PreCompact lane writes straight to stdout, `docs/design/context_rebuilder.md`, and the `locked="false"` half of the pair, which has no counterpart in `lock="user"` — and the recap is read out of `cadence_resume_cache.json`, which a prior session wrote and which stays injectable for an hour, so an upgrade inside that window hands the hook a recap the previous version rendered. A hook that knew only one spelling would breach #379 again one release later, whichever spelling that was. The enumeration of what reads the rebuilder's attribute outside the hook is in `tests/test_recap_lock_spelling_1570.py`, alongside the guard that pins the rebuilder's emitted schema so a later change to it reds a test that names those consumers.

  **What the exemption keys on is "the last render of a lock", not "a lock".** Exempting every recognised lock was implemented first and reverted on measurement: `<locked>` renders the same locks uncapped a few lines below in the same envelope, so on the `--resume-drop` fixture's 40 locks it pinned about 2,000 tokens of duplicate text and took the prompt's own matched beliefs from 6 of 6 to 0 — #1564's AC3 loss, restaged by the fix. `_recap_shed` now keeps a recap element only when nothing outside the recap renders that id, which is the rule its caller already applied to `dropped_ids`, and the invariant both halves serve is single: no user-locked id ever reaches `dropped_ids`. On a store of frozen locks that keeps the recap shedding whole, wrapper included, and `scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py --resume-drop` reports the same 65 → 0 elements and 6 → 6 matched beliefs as before.

  **The exemption is asked inside the shed, never before it.** `enforce_block_ceiling` picks the elements it may schedule before it reaches the recap, and a recap element is in that set whatever its attributes say; `_recap_shed` is the only thing that decides what a shed keeps. Testing the lock in both places instead leaves a recap of nothing but recognised locks — which is every recap on a store whose rebuild set is locks, and any mixed store once L0 fills the rebuilder's token budget — contributing no element to the drop order at all, so the shed never runs and the whole span pins. Measured on the 20-core / 20-hit store at 120 locks, that shipped 120 duplicate elements and 18,054 tokens against the same store's 10,902; the prompt's own hits paid for them.

  **The class this changes is the [#1558](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1558) reference lock**, whose `<locked>` render is a `ref` manifest line rather than an element. Its bounded-topic element in the recap is the only element render of that content in the envelope, so shedding it removed the content from the fire while leaving a manifest line naming it. That element is now kept and the wrapper with it. Reachability is narrow: cadence is `DEFAULT_ENABLED = False`, so only an opt-in install with a resume cache, a reference lock, and a block over its ceiling was affected. Guards are mutation-proven one claim at a time in `tests/test_recap_lock_spelling_1570.py` — including the reverted over-broad exemption, which reds the arm that measures the prompt's own hits.
- **A live test asserted general budget monotonicity on a fixture that could not disprove it ([#1574](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1574)).** `tests/test_retrieval_token_budget.py::test_tighter_budget_returns_no_more_results_than_looser` claimed "as budget shrinks, output length is non-increasing". Every belief in its fixture is `body[:content_len]`, so all of them cost the same; under equal prices the greedy fill is a prefix and cannot evict, which made the assertion unfailable — the same shape that hid the defect in the census fixture. The property is false in general: with a dear top-ranked belief and two cheap ones, raising the budget from 60 to 61 takes the output from two hits to one. The test is now scoped to its fixture, asserts that the fixture really is equal-priced, and is paired with an arm that pins the eviction on unequal prices.
- **The corpus schema validator read an empty scaffold, so no corpus row had ever been checked ([#1580](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1580)).** `tests/test_corpus_schema.py` pinned its root to `tests/corpus/v2_0/`, the public scaffold, which holds 17 module directories, a `.gitkeep` in each, a README, and no rows at all. All 19 modules the table parametrizes resolved against it. Every per-module assertion skipped on every run, and the skip said nothing, so a green tail was indistinguishable from a validated corpus. The root now resolves from `AELFRICE_CORPUS_ROOT` when you set it and falls back to the public tree otherwise; a skip names the root it inspected and where that root came from, a module that runs reports how many rows it validated, and zero rows fails rather than passes. The reported count prints in a `corpus schema` section of the terminal summary, next to the bench-gate tier's own block, and it prints for a test that errored as well as one that passed or failed, since a run that died is the one where the number is most missing. The module records that count from a `finally`, so it survives the walk's own failures — an unparseable line or a row that is not an object both raise partway through, and a failure with no count leaves you unable to tell whether validation read 3 rows or 500 before it stopped. An aborted walk says so, since its count is a floor rather than the module's size. **Turning it on is what found the violations, and the violations are recorded rather than repaired,** because relabelling a corpus is the corpus owner's decision and not this change's: `KNOWN_FAILURES` carries a reason per module and per rule, and the list is a ratchet in both directions — an unrecorded violation fails its module, and a recorded one that stops reproducing fails it too, so a repair forces the entry out instead of leaving a permanent exemption behind. Every reproducing entry prints alongside the row count, because a green run plus a waiver nobody reads is the quiet exemption this was meant to avoid, and it prints how many rows it covers, because a waiver is per module and per rule and a later batch carrying the same violation would otherwise land inside it silently. Against the mounted lab corpus that is 510 `directive_detection` rows validated, breaking two recorded rules: the v0_1 batch carries no `provenance` at all, and both batches spell the negative class `not-directive` where the schema declares `not_directive` (304 of 510 rows). **A second gap was that two corpora were not merely failing but invisible:** the per-module test parametrizes over the `MODULES` table, so `compression_uplift` and `query_strategy` — which hold rows, carry no envelope of any kind, and have no public scaffold directory — contributed no rows, no failure and no skip. A new check compares the directories holding rows against the table and fails on any that is neither declared nor recorded in `KNOWN_UNDECLARED_MODULES` with a reason. Holding rows means at least one non-blank line, not merely the presence of a `*.jsonl`: a half-built module is the normal state of this corpus, the public tree ships scaffolding for modules nobody has labelled yet, and zero rows cannot go unvalidated — so an empty scaffold is not the gap this guards against, and does not have to be enumerated to keep the suite green. The label rules gain the case the issue names: where a consuming detector exposes its own label constant, the module's vocabulary is checked against it and a row label the scorer can never return is rejected at validation, since such a row is graded wrong however the detector behaves. `relationship_detector.VERDICT_LABELS` is the worked case and `contradiction`'s `compatible` is the label — verified against a lab corpus copy of 79 rows, 27 of them labelled that way; no `contradiction/` module is mounted under the canonical lab root today, so that entry is recorded but does not yet execute. Each rule is mutation-proven on its own arm, `duplicate-id` included: it compares a row against the rows before it rather than against the schema, so the walk is extracted into a helper a unit test can hand two rows that agree on `id`. The scoreability arm widens the module's declared set so the vocabulary rule passes and only scoreability can fire — on the shipped table the two always agree, so an arm that did not separate them would prove nothing. `MODULES['contradiction']` spells its three labels out rather than deriving them from `VERDICT_LABELS`, because a table derived from the constant is the same object as the constant and the drift check between them could then never fail. One adjacent correction in `tests/conftest.py`: the bench-gate tier counted any skip whose reason mentioned `AELFRICE_CORPUS_ROOT`, and the schema walk names that variable in twenty skips of its own (19 module parametrizations plus the undeclared-module check), which would have been attributed to the retrieval, compression, and clustering gates. Classification now matches the tier's shared skip reason instead of the substring.
- **`.aelfrice.toml` discovery no longer escapes the project ([#1582](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1582)).** The shared walk ascended to the filesystem root, so from any directory under `$HOME` it found `$HOME/.aelfrice.toml` — per-user configuration, from a code path `docs/user/CONFIG.md` documented as having none. The walk now stops at the first of: your home directory (checked before the probe, so `~/.aelfrice.toml` is never read), a directory holding `.aelfrice.toml`, a directory holding `.git`, or the filesystem root. Every reader funnels through `config_discovery.discover_config`, so all of them are bounded identically, and a new AST guard fails if a module grows a walk of its own. **Behavior change:** a `~/.aelfrice.toml` that used to apply to every project under your home directory is now ignored, and a submodule or linked work tree nested inside a larger repository stops at its own `.git` and no longer inherits the outer repository's `.aelfrice.toml`. Copy the keys into each project's own `.aelfrice.toml`. Only some keys have a matching `AELFRICE_*` environment variable — the `[retrieval]`, `[cadence]`, and `[implicit_feedback]` keys do, and it still outranks the file — so for `[noise]`, `[dedup]`, `[rebuilder]`, `[hook_audit] max_bytes`, and the `[relationship_detector]` thresholds the per-project file is the only remedy.
- **The calibration gate no longer calls an aggregation collision "inert" ([#1584](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1584)).** `test_disabling_the_blend_changes_the_metrics` compared ROC-AUC and Spearman ρ between the shipped `posterior_weight` and `0.0`. Both are statistics of the pooled `(score, label)` observations as a multiset, so they agree whenever per-query rank moves cancel across the corpus — and on the bundled 7-fixture corpus `posterior_weight = 1.5` does exactly that, matching the `0.0` arm to every digit of both metrics while retrieving a different ranking on three of seven queries. The assertion was therefore a false positive waiting to fire: moving the shipped default into that band reds it with a message reading "the rerank is inert on this corpus" at a weight where the ranking demonstrably changed. The guard now compares **rankings** — the property the gate exists to protect, though not the one its old name described — and claims inertness only where inertness holds; it is renamed to match. The same false cause was corrected in the two other places that asserted it: the pinned-baseline test, and the `eval-calibration` PR-blocking workflow, both of which trip on byte-identical baselines and both of which would have fired with the wrong explanation had the default moved into a colliding band. `CalibrationReport` gained a `rankings` field carrying each query's ranked candidate ids, recorded by the same loop that builds the observations so the two can never describe different runs; it is read by name everywhere, so `aelf eval --json` and the pinned baselines are unchanged.
- **The calibration gate's default arm no longer inherits an ambient `AELFRICE_POSTERIOR_WEIGHT` ([#1584](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1584)).** The helper declined to set the variable rather than deleting it, so with one already in the environment the "shipped default" arm silently measured the operator's configured value and the gate compared that against `0.0`. The old metric assertion happened to red under such poisoning; the ranking assertion does not, so the hole became silent at the moment the guard stopped watching metrics. It now deletes the variable, and a new test fails if the default arm ever reads the environment again.
- **The prior-grid sweep counted pre-#1086 legacy moves over a different population than the share it printed ([#1600](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1600)).** `benchmarks/posterior_prior_grid_sweep.py` counted a belief as a legacy move if it was off the insertion-prior grid and its earliest non-zero-valence feedback event predated the 2026-07-04 exposure change — but printed that count as a share of the *genuinely-moved* beliefs, which is `off_prior - mean_preserving` and excludes dedupe mass. A collapsed duplicate group whose canonical row carries an old feedback event therefore landed in the numerator and not the denominator, so the printed share was inflated and could in principle exceed 100%; a two-belief fixture with one of each makes it print 200.0%. `_feedback_split` now applies the same `_matches_prior_multiple` test `probe` already uses, so the numerator is scoped to the population the share reports on, and the output line names that population. The classifier is unchanged — [#1598](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1598) established the uniform/moved split is right on real data. On the measured host the correction moves the count by one, from 6,761 to 6,760: exactly one dedupe-mass belief carries a pre-2026-07-04 event, at `(5.0, 5.0)`, five copies of `derivation.py`'s git triple-extraction prior. The published 96.3% share is unchanged (6,760/7,021 = 96.28%).
- **The onboard handshake now keeps each candidate's commit date, instead of stamping every accepted belief with the handshake-completion time ([#1609](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1609)).** `onboard_sessions.candidates_json` carried only `index`, `source`, and `text`, dropping `SentenceCandidate.commit_date` — the v1.1.0 git-recency date that direct `scan_repo` ingestion already uses as `belief.created_at`. Every belief accepted through the handshake collapsed to one shared `created_at`, so a session's temporal spine (`(created_at, rowid)` order) fell back to rowid, which carries no chronology. `start_onboard_session` now builds the git-recency map and passes it to the filesystem and AST extractors, the same way `scan_repo` does, and `commit_date` rides along in `candidates_json`; `accept_classifications` uses it as `created_at`, falling back to the handshake timestamp when the scanner found no date. Older sessions whose `candidates_json` predates this fix load unchanged — a missing `commit_date` key falls back to the same timestamp as before.
- **Git author dates are stored in UTC ([#1611](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1611)).** `aelf onboard` and the onboard handshake used a file's or commit's git author date as `created_at`, and git keeps the author's local offset (`2026-01-01T10:00:00-08:00`). `created_at` is ordered as text, so once a store mixed those rows with the `Z` rows every other writer produces, some beliefs sorted out of real order and the temporal spine linked them backwards in time. The scanner now rewrites each git date as `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` when it reads it. If every row you onboarded carries the same offset and nothing else wrote to the store, you saw no symptom, which is how this went unnoticed. This change covers new writes only; rows already in a store keep their offsets until [#1660](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1660) rewrites them.
- **Files that changed in a merge commit get that merge's date ([#1612](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1612)).** git lists no paths for a merge commit by default, so a file that arrived only through a merge, such as a history import, had no git date and fell back to the ingest time, and a conflict resolved to new content kept the date of the last edit before the merge. The scanner now asks git for the paths a merge changed relative to every parent (`--cc`), which matches the per-path `git log -1 -- <path>` answer: on a merge-heavy repository it agreed on all 576 tracked files, where first-parent attribution got 364 wrong. It's still one `git log` per scan. One case still differs from the per-path answer: a side-branch edit that a merge discarded can still date the file.
- **Re-onboarding no longer counts as re-asserting your documents ([#1615](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1615)).** Every `aelf onboard` or repository scan ran as a new session, and nothing recorded which file a row came from, so re-reading unchanged text added a corroboration row on every run. On this repository, three scans gave all 12,633 doc beliefs a corroboration count of 2. Untracked files and directories outside git then reached `aelf core` after three runs. A paragraph at a second path was also reported as new, and classified again, on every run. Now filesystem ingest (onboard and repository scans) inserts new content but never corroborates an existing belief: a rescan, a moved file, or a copied paragraph adds nothing. The onboard pre-scan counts a paragraph as present when its text is already stored. Corroboration from commits, transcripts, `aelf lock` and `aelf remember`, wonder, and the memory mirror is unchanged. Rows already in a store aren't removed.
- **Onboarding skips doc paragraphs longer than 4,000 characters ([#1616](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1616)).** The scanner had no maximum paragraph length, so a `.txt` data file with no blank lines became one candidate of several megabytes (54 MB in one real repository), and `/aelf:onboard` put it whole into the classifier prompt. A paragraph over 4,000 characters is now dropped, not truncated. The limit sits just above the measured 99th percentile of real doc paragraphs (3,541). On this repository it drops 51 of 5,455 paragraphs (0.9%). The paragraphs around a dropped one keep their source index, so their belief ids don't change and re-onboarding doesn't duplicate them.
- **No gate caught an absolute home path, nothing stopped a host configuration directory being tracked, and nothing refused a personal identity in commit metadata ([#1617](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1617)).** PR #1610 added `.gemini/settings.json` to this public repo carrying a developer's absolute venv path twelve times, and `secrets-scan`, `pattern-scan` and `history-scan` all reported PASS. The first looks for credentials, and an absolute path is not one; the other two match against patterns held in the `SCAN_RULES_B64` repository secret, which — established by behavior, since the secret cannot be read — carries no home-directory rule. A sweep of the tree found the same class of disclosure already on `main`: `docs/design/hook-injection-audit.md` carried a real `/Users/<user>/` path in two table rows, landed 2026-07-20 and still live. Both rows now use the `~/` form the same document already uses elsewhere. **Removing them clears `HEAD` only — git history retains the old blobs, and the content pushed to `refs/pull/1610/head` stays reachable regardless of the branch deletion, because GitHub pins pull-request commits permanently.** Only GitHub Support can purge that ref.

  The same sweep found a worse disclosure that no gate covered: **two commits on `main` carry a contributor's real name and corporate email as the git author**, from a machine whose `user.email` was never set to the privacy form. Across the same 367-commit window, 364 commits use `<id>+<login>@users.noreply.github.com` and one is Dependabot. Commit metadata is the least recoverable disclosure there is — copied into every clone and fork, served by the unauthenticated REST API, and pinned by the pull refs, none of which a history rewrite reaches. `scripts/check_commit_identity.py` refuses it at the push, which is the only control that works. It checks the **name as well as the address**: GitHub's no-reply forms anonymise the address and do nothing about `%an`, and the commits that motivated this carried both.

  Two design choices are deliberate. The rules live **in the tree rather than in a secret**, because a gate whose patterns nobody can read is a gate nobody can audit, test, or regression-guard — the reason this gap stayed invisible until something went through it. And the path checker covers **both halves** of the failure: the path text is the disclosure, but the untracked-but-unignored host dotdir is the vector. `.gitignore` had no rule for `.claude/`, `.gemini/` or `.codex/`, and this repo's own worktrees live under `.claude/`, so the directory sat one `git add -A` from being staged. All three are now ignored, refused if tracked at **any depth**, and added to the pre-push hook's banned paths, which previously knew only about `.claude/`.

  Verified rather than asserted. Against the #1610 diff the path checker flags **12 added lines**, every one of which the three shipped scans passed; the identity checker flags the two offending commits on both name and address. Across all **1,145** tracked files the path checker reports **zero** false positives, and the allowlist that keeps `/home/runner`, `/Users/synthetic`, `C:\Users\ci\` and the container service accounts quiet is visible in the diff rather than in a secret. Refusing the synthetic fixture names this tree already uses (`dev`, `alice`, `first`, single-character segments) was measured and rejected: it flagged 61 legitimate lines across 20 files, and a gate that noisy gets switched off. That hole is recorded in the source beside the allowlist rather than left implicit.

  **The suites drive the scripts, not just the patterns.** An earlier revision tested the regexes alone and was vacuous over the thing that matters — nine mutations survived it, including `main()` returning 0 and `scan_tracked()` returning nothing, either of which turns the required CI step into a no-op while every test passes. A gate whose suite cannot detect the gate's own removal is the #1610 failure mode one layer up. The arms now run the real scripts against real dirty repositories and assert on exit codes; **all 23 mutations are killed**, including every one that previously survived.

  Escape hatches exist on purpose: a `pii-allow` marker on a line, or a glob in `.github-pii-exempt`. A gate with no override gets disabled wholesale, and both of these are visible in review where a silent bypass would not be. In the same spirit, three entries left the pre-push hook's `BANNED_PHRASES` — each named something this repo already publishes itself, so they protected nothing while forcing an override to write ordinary prose about our own CI.

  **Where it runs, precisely.** In CI: inside `pattern-scan` and `history-scan`, both required, plus the tests under the required `pytest` legs. Before a push: `.githooks/pre-push`, which is tracked, so a checkout that has run `scripts/setup-hooks.sh` gets the checks on its next pull. A disclosure failure there is deliberately **not** waivable by `ALLOW_STALE_BRANCH_PUSH`, which exists to wave through a branch that is merely behind main. `scripts/install-discretion-hook.sh` also carries them, but note that it is an *installer*: it writes a copy into `.git/hooks/pre-push`, so on a machine that uses that copy the new checks go live only after re-running it with `--force`. CI gates regardless.

  What it does not cover: content already in git history or in a `refs/pull/*` ref; a home path whose user segment is an allowlisted name; paths in binary files; a personal path in a commit *message*; and anything outside the `/home/…`, `/Users/…` and `X:\Users\…` shapes. This catches one high-frequency disclosure, not PII in general.
- **The personal-path gate forked a subprocess per tracked file ([#1620](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1620)).** `scan_tracked` read each blob through its own `git show`, measured at 74.6 ms/file and **85.9s** over 1,151 files. It had begun to blow the 60-second budget on `test_the_tracked_tree_is_clean` under the CI timeout scale, which is how it surfaced. One `git cat-file --batch` for the whole tree instead: **0.97s**, and the results are byte-identical — compared file by file across all 1,147 candidates, zero mismatches, including the 19 empty tracked files.

  Determinism is unchanged, and it is the reason this reads blobs rather than the working tree at all: following a tracked symlink would make a required check depend on the host filesystem instead of on the commit (#605). Symlink blobs are still scanned, which is correct — a symlink whose target is a home directory publishes that path just as a file containing it would.

  Two behaviour differences worth recording, neither a regression on any path this repo exercises. A single unreadable object now aborts the scan rather than being skipped, because the batch runs under `check=True`; that fails closed, which is the right direction for a gate. And a path present at several merge stages resolves to the last stage listed, where the per-file form failed on a conflicted path and skipped it.

  Pre-existing and **not** fixed here: `tracked_files()` uses `git ls-files`, which C-quotes non-ASCII paths, while the batch reads `git ls-files -s -z`, which does not. A path git quotes therefore never matches and is not scanned. The old per-file form was blind to exactly the same files — verified by running both against a repository containing tab, space, and non-ASCII filenames, where they return the identical findings — so this is a gap in the gate, not a change in it. It is the same class as [#1621](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1621).
- **aelfrice captured its own commands as beliefs, so a lock request left no usable trace ([#1620](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1620)).** A user ran `/aelf:lock <statement>`, nothing locked, nothing reported a failure, and the same instruction was restated twelve times over six weeks without ever binding. On that store `lock_level='user'` was set on exactly one belief out of 20,176.

  `is_transcript_noise` — the filter `ingest.py` actually uses — had no rule for aelfrice's own invocations, so `/aelf:lock <statement>` was written as an `agent_inferred` belief at μ=0.375, spending the evidence that a lock had been requested on a row describing the request. Two such rows exist on that store; neither produced a lock. A third row, dated 2026-08-19, has the rendered XML of another belief as its content.

  The slash form is matched unconditionally alongside the other structural prefixes. It has to be: `/aelf:lock <statement>` carries a statement that usually ends like written prose, so the category-1 escape hatch would rescue exactly the string the rule exists to drop, and no English sentence opens with `/aelf:`. The bare `aelf ` form joins the prose-escaped shell prefixes instead, because sentences do open with it. **That escape is punctuation-only**, as it has been for `git ` and `pytest ` since #1371: "aelf locks are injected verbatim in every session." survives, and the same sentence without a full stop does not. The limitation is pinned by a test rather than left implied. Measured on the live store, the new rules newly reject **45 of 20,177** beliefs, all of them command text or rendered blocks.

  **Also hardened, and honestly scoped: `aelf lock` could print success without locking.** The write was skipped when the resolved belief carried `ORIGIN_SPECULATIVE` — deliberately, because #550 Surface B owns phantom rows — but the skip fell through to the success lines. Surface B now runs *before* the write instead of at the end of the function: `find_phantom_lock_matches` only sees rows still carrying `ORIGIN_SPECULATIVE`, so promoting first means the write can no longer disqualify a phantom, which was the only reason it skipped them. The write is then unconditional, except that it preserves `ORIGIN_USER_VALIDATED` — the promotion target, and a stronger provenance than `ORIGIN_USER_STATED`. A verification reads the belief back through the same predicate `aelf locked` uses and exits non-zero if it is not there, naming any phantoms that were promoted, because Surface B commits per call and "nothing happened" would be false about the store.

  **That half is hardening, not a fix for an observed failure, and the distinction is load-bearing.** `actual_id` resolves by `sha256(text)`, while the only producer of `ORIGIN_SPECULATIVE` keys `content_hash` on `_constituent_key` — sha256 of the constituent ids plus the generator, explicitly not the content. A real phantom therefore cannot be `actual_id`, and pre- and post-change code produce identical store state on one. Restoring the skip is caught by no test in the suite, and that is expected for inert code; an earlier draft of this entry claimed otherwise and was wrong.

  Diagnosed against six hypotheses registered before the experiments ran. Three results contradict the obvious theories and are recorded so they are not re-proposed: locking over an ordinary captured belief already worked, and had since `f6f1d030` (2026-07-29), *before* both observed failures, so a content-hash collision was not the cause; the L0 read keys on `lock_level` and is not gated by origin, so the tier mechanism itself was never broken; and an earlier attempt filtered `_should_skip_bm25`, which is the BM25 *retrieval* gate (#674) with no belief writer behind it, so every unit test passed while ingest went on writing the command. The end-to-end arm here drives `ingest_jsonl` so that mistake cannot recur silently.

  **This does not prevent the reported failure from recurring**, and #1620 stays open for that reason. The lock was lost because the command never executed; stopping the capture preserves the evidence but does not make the lock happen. Reconciling "a lock was requested" against "a lock was applied" is [#1622](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1622), and that is the criterion that would have caught it.

  Also not covered: paraphrase defeating corroboration. `_content_hash` is `sha256(raw_text)` with no normalisation, so twelve restatements were twelve unrelated beliefs at corroboration 0, none reaching `aelf core`. The fix is a near-duplicate signal rather than a hash change — normalising whitespace and case would merge roughly 3 of the 12 — and it needs its own design.
- **The docs and help text name the search-tool hook's current matcher, and one tuple now defines it ([#1628](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1628)).** [#1626](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1626) widened the matcher from `Grep|Glob` to `Grep|Glob|WebSearch|WebFetch`, but `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `INSTALL.md`, `COMMANDS.md`, the `/aelf:setup` command page, the `--search-tool` help for `aelf setup` and `aelf unsetup`, the hook manifest description, and a `setup.py` docstring about uninstall still gave the old value. They now name all four tools. `docs/design/search_tool_hook.md` gets a dated note instead of a rewrite, because its body records the v1.2.x and v1.5.0 design as it shipped. The roadmap and earlier changelog entries are history and stay as written. **The matcher and the hook's tool set are one fact again.** `SEARCH_TOOL_NAMES` and `SEARCH_TOOL_MATCHER` were two hand-kept copies. Both now live in the leaf module `aelfrice.search_tool_names`, and the matcher is the `|`-join of the names. It's a leaf rather than a direct import: `setup` importing `hook_search_tool` would grow the import cycle through `aelfrice.hook` ([#1631](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1631)) from 5 modules to 8, and the reverse would load the installer on every hook fire. `import aelfrice.hook` still loads 19 aelfrice modules, and the import graph's strongly connected components are unchanged. Importing `aelfrice.hook_search_tool` or `aelfrice.setup` directly loads one more module, the new leaf, which imports only `typing`. **A guard keeps the prose in step.** A new test reads each live page that states the matcher and fails when it disagrees with `SEARCH_TOOL_MATCHER`, so the next widening can't leave the docs behind. The fixture in `test_setup_prune_stale.py` that transcribed `"Grep|Glob"` for a current install now reads the constant; the `test_doctor.py` fixture that models a legacy shell-wrapper install keeps the old value, with a comment saying why. No behavior changes.
- **A belief can't be stored with a `created_at` that isn't a timestamp ([#1629](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1629)).** `insert_belief` now raises `ValueError` for a value that doesn't parse as ISO-8601, so no writer can reintroduce the filename-as-date case. Rows already in a store aren't checked or changed.
- **Onboarding reads git's file dates without guessing ([#1629](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1629), [#1621](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1621)).** The scanner split `git log` output into dates and paths by looking at each line. A path that began with four digits and a hyphen, such as `2026-01-15-retro.md`, was read as a date: that file got no date, and the files after it in the same commit got the filename as their `created_at`. A path with non-ASCII characters, a quote, or a backslash arrived C-quoted, so it never matched the file and fell back to the ingest time. The scanner now reads git's NUL-separated output, where dates are framed and paths are raw, so every tracked path gets the date of the last commit that changed it. It's still one `git log` per scan. On this repository, no file's date changes, because it has no such names.
- **Transcript capture no longer stores a headless host session as user knowledge ([#1634](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1634)).** An evaluation harness drives the host headlessly (`claude -p`, or an Agent SDK app), with one scripted prompt per session. aelfrice stored each run's prompt as if a user had typed it, so one scripted run counted as many independent sessions of corroboration. On one store, a single afternoon of such runs put 67 beliefs into `aelf core` (#1635). The host names how a session started, as its entrypoint, in the session log's records and in a hook's `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`. The headless entrypoints are `sdk-cli`, `sdk-ts`, and `sdk-py`. `ingest_jsonl`, which `aelf ingest-transcript` uses, now skips a record with a headless entrypoint and counts it as a skipped line. The transcript logger records neither the prompt nor the reply when its hook runs under one. A log with no `entrypoint`, and every interactive entrypoint, is captured as before. The host's `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ATTENDED` flag is not used, because the host also sets it to `0` for background, daemon, and teammate sessions. The entrypoint is the host's label, and a process started inside a host session can inherit it. On a non-interactive start, the host turns an inherited `cli` into `sdk-cli` but keeps an IDE, desktop, or `sdk-*` label, so `claude -p` run inside an IDE or desktop session can be captured, and an interactive session started from an SDK process can be skipped. This covers the two transcript-capture paths only; the memory-mirror and commit-ingest hooks do not check the entrypoint. If you drive aelfrice through the SDK on purpose, set `[ingest] capture_print_mode = true` or `AELFRICE_CAPTURE_PRINT_MODE=1` to restore capture. aelfrice-format text pasted into an ordinary prompt (#1620) is not part of this change.
- **A burst of re-assertions no longer counts as independent corroboration ([#1635](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1635)).** `aelf core` and the SessionStart `<core>` lane read a corroboration count of 2 or more as independent evidence, and the two promotion selectors read at least N rows across at least 2 sessions the same way. A scripted replay gets a new session id every few seconds, so one afternoon of headless evaluation runs gave 67 beliefs 13 or 27 rows each, all inside about 1.5 minutes. All 67 reached `aelf core`, which made them eligible for the SessionStart `<core>` lane. A belief's sightings are now its creation and its corroboration rows, grouped into episodes. Sightings less than `CORROBORATION_EPISODE_GAP_SECONDS` (one hour of elapsed time, measured to the millisecond) apart are one episode, however many sessions they name. The corroboration arm counts only at `CORROBORATION_MIN_EPISODES` (two) or more. The rule applies in `aelf core` (its gate and its `corroboration` / `CORR=` labels), the `<core>` lane, `find_promotable_phantoms`, and `find_promotable_snapshots`. All four use one SQL definition: the selectors embed it, and `aelf core` and the `<core>` lane read it through `MemoryStore.corroboration_episodes`. Sightings are compared as instants, not as text, because rows mix `Z` and `+HH:MM` offsets. An unparseable timestamp is not a sighting. Transcript ingest stamps each corroboration row with the turn's own time, so a bulk backfill of old sessions keeps its real spread. A belief said on one day and re-asserted in a single sitting days later has two episodes and counts. A belief that already existed and is then hit by one burst also has two, and also counts; the operator accepted that case over a rule that would drop the first. The posterior arm of `aelf core` is unchanged. On a working store that holds no burst beliefs, measured read-only on 2026-09-29, non-lock core membership is unchanged at 698: the rule cost no real recurrence there. This change was not re-measured on the store that held the burst. The `<core>` lane's `corr=` attribute still shows the raw count.
- **An edge never links a belief to itself any more ([#1636](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1636)).** `ingest_jsonl` chains each user turn to the previous one with a `DERIVED_FROM` edge, anchored on the belief each turn resolved to. A turn that repeats the one before it resolves to the same belief, so the writer stored an edge from that belief to itself. On a synthetic session of 500 distinct turns plus 50 duplicates in 45 runs, it stored 45 such self-loops. `MemoryStore.insert_edge` now refuses an edge whose two ends are the same belief, for every caller. It returns `False` instead of raising, because hooks call it, and returns `True` when it writes. Some writers already checked for a self-loop themselves, and others cannot produce one by the shape of their data. The ingest inter-turn writer, `contradiction.resolve_contradiction`, `aelf migrate` (which copies a legacy store's edges), and the `bfs_latency_v3` benchmark corpus did neither, and all four now count only the edges actually written. A session of A, B, B, C links C to B and B to A. The chain still advances past the skipped edge, so the next edge's anchor carries the later turn's text. `find_unresolved_contradictions` no longer lists a belief paired with itself. Such a legacy row can never be settled, and before this change it would have been re-resolved, with an audit row, on every run. Existing self-loops in a store are left in place, and `aelf migrate` does not copy them.
- **A hook's whole output now fits the host's inline limit, so the model reads all of it and every user lock is either shown or accounted for in one line that names cut locks, up to 20, and counts the rest ([#1639](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1639)).** The host inlines at most 10,000 characters of a hook's output. Past that it saves the output to a file and puts only a 2,000-character preview into the model's context ([hooks reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md)). aelfrice's block ceiling, 6,000 estimated tokens (about 24,000 characters) with user locks exempt, sat above that limit. `scripts/hook_output_census.py` counted 189 of 456 aelfrice hook outputs in local transcripts as saved rather than inlined. Of the 79 saved outputs whose full text carried user locks, 36 lost at least one lock from the preview the model saw. Counting only outputs written since 2026-09-24, 17 of 70 were still saved, with a median of 13.7 KB. A new bound, `HOOK_PAYLOAD_CHAR_LIMIT` (9,500 characters), now covers everything one `UserPromptSubmit` or `SessionStart` fire writes to stdout, and the `additionalContext` the `PreToolUse` search hook writes. The command-result note, the cadence checkpoint, and both phantom notes are charged first, and the memory envelope gets the room they leave. The two phantom notes share what is left after a 4,000-character reserve for the envelope, so they cannot take it. An opportunity that does not fit is not recorded as shown: a promotion, or a new contradiction, surfaces on a later turn, and a gap or new-entity note, which belongs to its prompt, can fire again when a prompt raises it. The notes record what they showed only after they are written. An envelope over its room first drops `seen` pointers to beliefs an earlier turn showed, whose text is already in the context window, and then the whole `<recent-work>` lane. It then sheds elements in the order it already used: a `<cadence-resume>` recap, then `<core>`, then the per-turn hits. An envelope that fits its room isn't trimmed unless `AELFRICE_HOOK_BLOCK_CEILING` is set below its size, and a trimmed one is held to its room in characters, not rounded down to a whole number of estimated tokens. User locks still come first and are still never trimmed mid-content. When the locks alone do not fit, the block keeps the locks that fit in list order and cuts the rest whole. A cut lock takes every render with it: its element or the one-line `ref` entry of a reference-tier lock, its `seen` pointer, and its element inside a `<cadence-resume>` recap. The envelope decides which locks the room holds before it sheds anything, so the per-turn hits never make room for a lock that is then cut. The block is then measured as written: while it is over the room another lock is cut, and a cut lock that fits once rendered is taken back. The block then ends with one line that names the cut locks (up to 20 ids, then a count) and points to `aelf locked`. stderr also says how many were cut, so no lock disappears silently. A cut lock takes no exposure or audit record, because the model never saw it, and the search hook's telemetry counts only the lines it showed. A cadence checkpoint packs to the room left after a 4,000-character reserve for the envelope. The post-compaction rebuild block gets the room the baseline leaves. When their pack loop overruns, the checkpoint and the rebuild block are cut at element boundaries first, then at a line boundary with a marker that says the block was cut. The SessionStart recap line is charged before either block, and the search hook drops its L1 lines before any lock. Each field of the `<recent-work>` lane (the branch, the upstream, and each commit subject) is capped at 200 characters as rendered, and a `#N` reference longer than 7 digits isn't listed: the lane is repository text that a cloned repository controls, and it is dropped only as a whole. This reverses the #1560 ruling that no bound would span more than one block, on evidence that ruling did not have: the host's limit applies to the fire's whole output, and a sum of per-block bounds cannot guarantee it. `AELFRICE_HOOK_BLOCK_CEILING=0` still lifts the envelope's ceiling and its share of the bound; the cadence checkpoint and the rebuild block stay packed to the room.
- **Transcript ingest no longer stores a background task's report, another session's message, or other machine output as your beliefs ([#1649](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1649)).** A background task's completion notice reaches the transcript as a user turn, and its `<result>` body is a sub-agent's report. aelfrice's own transcript logger has dropped such a prompt whole since #747. But `aelf ingest-transcript` also reads logs other loggers wrote and the host's own session files. There it applied only the per-sentence filter, which drops tag lines and passes the report's plain sentences.

  Measured on one store on 2026-09-30, 602 ingested rows carried a notice's `<status>` tag, its closing `</result>` tag, or its wrapper, and each became a belief. 169 of those carried `</result>`. Those beliefs were retrieved into later prompts. The counts are a lower bound, because a report's other sentences carry no tag.

  Ingest now keeps what a person typed or pasted:
  - **Records skipped whole:** the host's session files mark records that aren't a person typing. Ingest skips a sidechain record (a sub-agent's prompt or reply), an `isMeta` record (harness text, which the logger already skips), and a compaction summary (the model's digest of the session).
  - **Marker lines cut:** from the rest, it cuts the host's own marker lines: the four-line `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION]` banner and `[Request interrupted by user]`. It cuts the lines themselves, so a paragraph next to one is kept.
  - **Slash commands dropped whole:** a record that then opens with a slash command's `<command-*>` wrapper is dropped whole, because the wrapper is followed by the command's expanded body.
  - **Harness blocks cut:** every other harness block that opens on its own line is cut, closed or not:
    - `<task-notification>` and its parts;
    - `<system-reminder>`;
    - `<cross-session-message>`;
    - `<aelfrice-worker-context>`;
    - `<tool-result>`;
    - the `<bash-*>` and `<local-command-*>` wrappers;
    - `<user-prompt-submit-hook>`.

  A tag you mention mid-sentence stays, because it's your text. An unclosed block is cut to the end of its record, because nothing marks where it would have ended. What remains goes through the per-sentence filter as before. One real session record that produced 120 beliefs produces none.

  On one machine's session files and a second logger's archive (2026-09-30):
  - the three record flags skip 241,847 records, including 2,209 sub-agent prompts and 15 compaction summaries;
  - of the 3,147 user records left, ingest drops 2,489: 2,391 notices, 37 cross-session messages, 10 interrupt markers, and slash-command, bash, and local-command wrappers;
  - the only words of yours in those drops are a typed slash command's arguments and shell commands you ran with `!`;
  - no record of plain prose is dropped, and none is trimmed.

  Text you paste (`<pasted_content>`) is kept on purpose, even when it quotes another session. Model prose you quote without a wrapper can't be told from your own words by a deterministic test, so it's kept too.

  Beliefs already stored from these records aren't removed by this change.
- **The claude-memory mirror now gets consent from a linked worktree, and `aelf doctor` warns when it never did ([#1657](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1657)).** Consent is recorded at `aelf setup` only when the project's memory directory exists. `derive_memory_dir` encoded the working directory itself, but the host keeps a worktree session's memory under the main checkout. So `aelf setup` or `aelf reconcile-claude-memory` from a worktree deferred consent forever, and the installed hook never wrote a row.
  - **Worktree mapping:** `derive_memory_dir` now maps a linked worktree to its main checkout by reading the `.git` file and the worktree's `commondir`, without running `git`. A plain checkout, a repository nested inside a worktree, a submodule, and a worktree of a bare repository are unchanged. Memory filed under a worktree's own path is no longer looked up there; the host doesn't write any (none of 17 worktree project directories on one machine had a `memory/` folder).
  - **Doctor warning:** `aelf doctor` now warns when the mirror hook is installed and the project has memory, but no env value, TOML key, or consent sentinel has turned the mirror on. The warning gives the fix. An explicit opt-out doesn't trigger it, and the warning doesn't change the exit code.
  - **Docs:** `docs/user/INSTALL.md` now says when consent is and isn't recorded.
- **Re-onboarding no longer offers a paragraph whose belief you retired ([#1673](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1673)).** The onboard pre-scan didn't count retired beliefs as present. So every `aelf onboard` run offered a retired paragraph again, the host classified it again, and the accept step dropped it, because capture never revives a retired belief ([#1215](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1215)). `aelf onboard --check` also never reached zero new candidates. Now retired content counts as present: it isn't offered or classified again, and it stays retired. This covers every soft-deleted belief, so it includes `aelf retire`, `aelf doctor --prune-noise --apply`, review removals, and wonder cleanup. To bring a retired belief back, run `aelf restore`.
- **Running the test suite no longer writes fixture beliefs into your real repo store ([#1678](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1678)).** `tests/test_transcript_round_trip.py` drives `PreCompact`, which spawns a detached `aelf ingest-transcript`. That child resolves its store with `db_path()`, and unless `AELFRICE_DB` is set, `db_path()` uses the git common dir of its working directory. Under pytest, that's your checkout, so each run added the fixture's beliefs and `ingest_log` rows to `<repo>/.git/aelfrice/memory.db`, the store that injects context into your real sessions. One store held five such beliefs and 35 `ingest_log` rows under the session id `round-trip-session`. The suite-wide `_sandbox_real_home` fixture in `tests/conftest.py` now sets `AELFRICE_DB` to a store in the session sandbox, so every child process inherits it. A test that exercises git-dir resolution deletes the variable with its own `monkeypatch.delenv`. Some tests still set or pop `os.environ["AELFRICE_DB"]` directly, which would change the pin for every later test, so a function-scoped fixture sets it again before and after each test. A new guard, `tests/test_suite_store_sandbox_1678.py`, starts a child the way the spawn does and fails if it resolves a store under the repo's git dir, and fails if a bare `os.environ` change in one test reaches the next. `tests/test_search_tool_hook_bash_telemetry.py` also stopped writing `./telemetry/` into your checkout: it set `AELFRICE_DB` to `:memory:`, whose parent is the working directory.

  This change doesn't touch rows that earlier runs already wrote. To remove them from your store, run these commands from inside your checkout or any of its worktrees:

  1. Find the store and back it up:

     ```sh
     DB="${AELFRICE_DB:-$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)/aelfrice/memory.db}"
     cp "$DB" "$DB.bak"
     ```

  2. Delete the fixture beliefs: `aelf introspect --session round-trip-session --limit 0 --json | jq -r '.groups[].beliefs[].id' | xargs -n1 aelf delete --yes`.
  3. Delete the fixture's `ingest_log` rows, which no `aelf` command removes: `sqlite3 "$DB" "DELETE FROM log_versions WHERE log_id IN (SELECT id FROM ingest_log WHERE session_id = 'round-trip-session'); DELETE FROM ingest_log WHERE session_id = 'round-trip-session';"`.

  Delete the `ingest_log` rows in the same pass as the beliefs. A log row whose belief is gone counts as a `derived_orphan` in the replay check, and that bucket counts as drift.
- **Pasted terminal and tool output no longer becomes beliefs ([#1691](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1691)).** Transcript ingest kept a `<pasted_content>` block whole, so each line of a pasted `git status` was stored and injected back on later turns. Pasted prose is still kept. Output is now dropped in two ways:
  - **Terminal transcripts:** a pasted block that opens with a shell prompt in fish, bash, or zsh form is dropped whole. A prompt must show a short host name with no dot, so an email address at the start of a line isn't treated as one. A prompt with a dotted host name isn't recognized; that block's lines go through the line rules instead.
  - **Output lines:** in any other pasted block, a line is dropped only when the whole line has a fixed output form. These forms include:
    - git status, hint, commit, merge, and diffstat lines;
    - the host's tool-count and timing lines;
    - a shell's own error lines;
    - log lines with an ISO timestamp, or a clock time followed by a column gap or a level word;
    - lines with two or more column gaps (a tab, or two or more spaces after a character that doesn't end a sentence) that don't end in `.`, `?`, or `!`;
    - a URL, a file path, a file name whose extension starts with a letter, or a `+`-joined slug, alone on its line. A path needs a leading `~`, `.`, or `/`, a trailing `/`, or a file name at its end.

  A sentence that only begins like one of these forms is kept, for example "Your branch is behind on reviews". So are words like "and/or", "read/write/execute", "e.g.", "3.14", "C++", and "x=1". Some prose is still dropped: a single token that looks like a file name or a path ending in one, such as "Node.js" or "A/B.test", and a line typed with two or more spaces between its words and no closing punctuation. The paste's wrapper tags are host markup and are removed, unless a tag's attributes run past 200 characters. An unclosed paste is filtered to the end of the record. A paste ends at its first closing tag, so text after a second closing tag is treated as the user's own. The tags are found in one pass and each line pattern runs in linear time, so neither a 200,000-character line nor 20,000 unclosed tags takes more than milliseconds.

  The change was measured on 12 private pasted blocks. They made 390 beliefs before the change and 42 after. Two blind graders rated 99.4% of the removed beliefs as output, and the change removed 94.8% of the beliefs they rated as output. One 313-line terminal paste accounts for most of the removals. The method and the per-block figures are in the #1691 comments.
- **An exported `GIT_DIR` no longer points the test suite at another repository ([#1707](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1707)).** Git hooks export `GIT_DIR` and related variables. A suite run with them set pointed every temporary-directory `git init`, and every store lookup that goes through git, at the outer repository. With `GIT_DIR` exported at a scratch repository, roughly 130 tests failed or errored, and an `aelfrice/` store appeared in that repository's `.git`. The suite-wide sandbox fixture in `tests/conftest.py` now clears `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, and `GIT_DISCOVERY_ACROSS_FILESYSTEM`. The same run now passes with nothing written to the outer repository. `tests/test_git_env_scrub_1707.py` runs a second pytest with all seven variables exported and fails if any of them reaches a test. The test keeps its own copy of the list, so dropping a name from the fixture fails it.
- **An exported git config or template no longer reaches the test suite ([#1714](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1714)).** The suite's sandbox `HOME` hides `~/.gitconfig`, but `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` name config files directly, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` and `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` carry settings in the environment, and `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR` names the hooks that `git init` copies. On 33 git-using test files, with each exported at a config or template that installs an external hook, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` each failed 20 tests, errored 6, and ran the external hooks 596 times; `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR` failed 6 and ran them 559 times. The suite-wide sandbox fixture in `tests/conftest.py` now points the global config at `.gitconfig` in the sandbox home, sets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, and clears the other four variables. The same runs now pass with no external hook runs. `tests/test_git_config_sandbox_1714.py` runs a second pytest with all five exported and fails if git reads any setting from outside the sandbox home or copies a hook from the template.
- **A pre-issue-create hook test no longer fails depending on test order ([#1715](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1715)).** `test_claude_dir_refused` built its path from the session's sandbox home, but the module under test captures its config directory at import, before the sandbox replaces `HOME`. Once an earlier test wrote `settings.json` into the sandbox home, the refusal no longer matched and the file was read. The test now points the module's config directory at a temporary directory and creates the file there, so the result doesn't depend on earlier tests. Because the file now exists, the test also fails if the refusal is removed, which it didn't before when the file was missing.
- **`scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py` now measures the same figures on every machine ([#1716](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1716)).** On a machine with a `~/.aelfrice.toml` and no project config, the SessionStart hook adds the #1652 notice that the file is ignored. That added about 40 tokens to the producer's `session_start` figures, so `check_derived_figures.py` reported 8 stale figures there: 7700 against the published 7660, and 272 against 233. CI's home has no such file, which is why nothing caught it. The published figures were correct. The producer now runs its measurements under an empty `HOME`, and restores the caller's `HOME` afterward.
- **A missing classifier source no longer empties the first-prompt session-start block ([#1719](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1719)).** `aelfrice.core_gate` derives `CLASSIFIER_VERSION` from the source of its prompt builder and reply parser. On an install that ships only bytecode, `inspect.getsource` raises `OSError`, so the import failed; a `.pyc` compiled from a different `.py` can raise `SyntaxError` or `tokenize.TokenError` the same way. The UserPromptSubmit hook imports the module while it builds the first prompt's `<session-start>` block whenever there are core candidates, and its fail-soft wrapper then returned the block empty, so its `<locked>` and `<core>` sections were missing. Locks still reached the model through ordinary retrieval. Now `CLASSIFIER_VERSION` is `None` when the source can't be read, and the failure stays in the core lane. The gate admits no unlocked belief to `<core>`, no stored label matches a `None` version, and nothing writes a batch or a label. Each command that depends on the classifier refuses with a message and exits 1:

  - `aelf core-gate accept` refuses before it reads stdin.
  - `aelf doctor core-gate --emit` and `--rerun` refuse before they create `--out`.
  - `aelf doctor --gc-filesystem-corroboration` refuses before it opens the store, in both the dry run and `--apply`, so it prints no report and deletes no rows. With every unlocked belief out of core, it would otherwise report nothing leaving core and delete rows that keep beliefs there once the classifier is back.

  The Stop hook emits no session-end batch, and `aelf doctor core-gate` reports that no candidate is admitted. When the source is present, the version is unchanged.

### Security

- **The `benchmarks` extra no longer locks three packages with open advisories ([#1730](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1730)).** `uv.lock` now pins `fsspec` 2026.7.0 (was 2026.2.0, [GHSA-27vj-qcqg-25rc](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-27vj-qcqg-25rc), high), `datasets` 5.1.0 (was 4.8.5, [GHSA-379c-qx7v-6h59](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-379c-qx7v-6h59), moderate), and `multidict` 6.9.1 (was 6.7.1, [GHSA-54p9-h82j-f925](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-54p9-h82j-f925), moderate). `datasets` 5.1 requires `huggingface-hub` 1.31 or later, so `huggingface-hub` moves from 1.12.0 to 1.33.0, the latest 1.x release. That release requires newer `click` and `hf-xet`, so `click` moves from 8.3.3 to 8.5.0 and `hf-xet` from 1.4.3 to 1.7.0. It no longer depends on `typer`, so `typer`, `rich`, `shellingham`, `markdown-it-py`, `mdurl`, and `annotated-doc` leave the lock. Every changed package is in the `benchmarks` extra; the core install is unchanged. All three benchmark loaders (MAB, AMA-Bench, and LongMemEval) return rows under `datasets` 5.1.0.

### Performance

- **The published-figure gate re-runs its producers concurrently, so its cost is the slowest one rather than the sum ([#1578](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1578)).** `check_producers` spawns one child per store-free producer ([#1307](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1307)) and ran them one after another. On an M-series laptop with an SSD, the five producers on this tree cost 15.0s (`benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py`), 10.9s (`benchmarks/store_open_cost.py`), 10.6s (`scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py`), 2.4s (`scripts/budget_discriminability_census.py`) and 0.4s (`benchmarks/published_constants.py`) — 39.2s serial against the 120s `pytest-timeout` marker on `test_the_repo_passes_the_producer_checks`, which duly timed out on the v5.0.0 release pull request ([#1576](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1576)) and passed on a re-run. Two of those producers were new in v5.0.0, so the check got about 40% more expensive in one release with nothing warning about it. The children are independent by construction — one process each, each emitting its own figures on its own stdout, each building scratch under its own `mkdtemp` — so they now run on a `ThreadPoolExecutor` over `subprocess.run`, one worker per producer up to `PRODUCER_MAX_WORKERS`. The same five now take 18.1s. Raising the marker instead was rejected: it works today and has to be raised again on the next producer, and nothing makes that visible until a release cut.

  **Completion order does not reach the output.** A gate whose failure list is ordered by whichever child finished first prints a different diff on every run, and a reviewer cannot tell a new failure from a reshuffled one — worse than the timeout it replaces. So the pool is submitted in producer order, `map` returns in submission order, and every `Report.fail` is made afterwards on one thread walking that same order: `run_producer` runs off-thread and decides nothing, `report_producer` decides everything and runs on the main thread. Checked against the real tree with one figure broken per producer: two runs over the same tree emit byte-identical stdout and stderr, with the five producer failures in sorted producer order. `tests/test_derived_figures_1469.py` proves both halves on fake producers — one test runs four 0.75s sleepers and asserts the concurrent wall is under half the serial one, and one gives the fakes descending sleeps so they finish in the exact reverse of producer order, asserts they really did, and then asserts two runs print the same list in producer order. Forcing `max_workers = 1` in `run_producers` reds both.

  The 120s marker is **kept**, and kept at 120s, as a hang detector rather than a budget: the same five producers cost 39.3s through the gate serially against 18.3s concurrently, and the test as a whole — text scan included — now takes 20.5s, about 5.8x under the cap where it was about 2.9x. Lowering it to track the new wall clock would hand back the margin this change bought — a hosted runner is furthest from a laptop on the disk-bound axis `store_open_cost.py` leans on, since it walks a three-size grid up to a 20,000-belief store. To price a producer before adding it rather than at a release cut, `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --time-producers` prints each producer's own wall time, the serial sum and the concurrent wall; the table above is its output and is recorded in the `check_producers` docstring. The number to watch is the new producer's own time, because the wall clock is now the maximum: anything slower than the current 15.0s worst case moves it.
- **Positive sentiment no longer reads the whole hook audit log on every fire ([#1674](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1674)).** To find the beliefs your previous prompt was shown, the sentiment lane read all of `hook_audit.jsonl` and its rotated `.1` file, which can reach about 20 MB. It now reads backward from the end and stops at the session's most recent prompt row. On a 20 MB log, the lookup went from 96.7 ms to 0.14 ms (median of 15). On this project's live 1.7 MB log, it went from 8.2 ms to 0.08 ms, with the same answer. A session with no prompt row still reads every line, and that got faster too: 99 ms to 71 ms at 20 MB. One difference from the whole-file read: a corrupt line older than the match is no longer reached, so it doesn't turn the answer empty.

### Documentation

- **K0 is re-registered, and its verdict re-derived under the replacement ([#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546)).** The operator voided K0's original pre-registration because it described the instrument wrongly in four places: it called the cluster structure inert when `clustering.pack_with_clusters` runs on every query of every measured cell, treated probe containment as a theorem when it holds only because the census's stores carry no edges, inherited a containment mutation guard with no power on the `ups`, `retrieval_default`, and `rebuilder` lanes, and attributed the absent BFS lane to the missing edges rather than to the default-off `[retrieval] bfs_enabled` flag. `docs/design/budget_discriminability_k0_preregistration.md` replaces it. It carries every decision criterion forward unchanged — the statistic, the population rule, the grey band, the required N, the cut conditions, the forbidden fallbacks, and the finding wording — and adds only the disclosed limits and a set of kill criteria split by whether these corpora can supply the input that trips them, so a criterion that cannot fire is named as a limit instead of listed as a guard. `docs/design/budget_discriminability_decision_rule.md` is retained, banner-marked as voided, and unamended below the banner. No published figure moved: all 22 keys of `--emit-figures` compare equal, `--check` is byte-identical across two runs, and `tests/test_budget_census.py` passes.
- **The read-only routing criterion says what it can truthfully assert ([#1571](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1571)).** [#1416](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1416)'s expanded matrix asked that a routed read command perform no preliminary writable open *and* emit no failed-write warning. Neither half survives the writable-first order, and nothing in the code was wrong. The first is refuted unconditionally: `open_store_for_read()` always attempts the writable open before it can know whether the fallback is needed, so no routed command satisfies it on any store. The second is refuted on one shape — a store whose directory is frozen, whose write-ahead log sidecars are live, **and whose schema needs no write to open**. There the writable attempt succeeds, the `mode=ro` handle is never taken, `aelf search` goes on to attempt its `.bm25f` sidecar write, and that write fails with a non-fatal warning. The schema qualifier is load-bearing: an outdated schema makes the open-time DDL fire and fail, which does reach the fallback, and `tests/test_readonly_store_1416.py` constructs exactly that state by dropping `exploration_events` while holding the sidecars live. Both halves are therefore unsatisfiable while the writable-first order stands — and that order stands by the 2026-08-09 ruling, because preferring `mode=ro` unconditionally imposes degraded semantics (no migration, no expired-lock sweep) on every user whose store is perfectly writable. The criterion is therefore narrowed to what holds — the `mode=ro` fallback handle emits no failed-write warning, with the preliminary writable attempt retained and expected — and the reasoning lands at `open_store_for_read()` rather than in a closed issue, so the next reader meets it at the code. Making the sidecar write conditional on directory writability was considered and declined: it silences an accurate message — a write genuinely was attempted and genuinely failed — at the cost of a writability probe on a hot path that nobody has measured. Whether a frozen directory with live sidecars occurs in the field is unrecorded, and does not matter here, because the chosen disposition changes no design.
- **`benchmarks/retrieval_metrics.py` no longer claims its metrics are monotone in the token budget ([#1574](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1574)).** The module reasoned that "retrieval fills the budget in rank order, so cutting the budget truncates the tail", and concluded that every metric is monotone non-decreasing in the budget. `clustering.pack_with_clusters` does not truncate: stage 1 abandons on the first representative it cannot afford, and stage 2 skips an over-budget belief and keeps filling. The budget therefore **selects** rather than truncating, and raising it by one token can evict a belief the lower budget admitted. With the gold-bearing belief cheap and ranked second, that takes `recall_at_k` and `reciprocal_rank` from 1.0 to 0.0. The skip-and-continue fill is deliberate — it delivers at least as many beliefs as stopping at the first unaffordable one, though it is greedy in rank order and not count-maximal — so the docs now record the non-monotonicity as an accepted property rather than a defect, and name the tests that pin each half. The guard that appeared to cover this was `test_shrinking_the_budget_never_raises_a_metric`, which truncates a list and never sets a budget, prices a belief, or calls the packer; it is renamed to `test_truncating_the_tail_never_raises_a_metric`, scoped to tail truncation because dropping an item from the *head* promotes everything below it and can raise `reciprocal_rank`. The same false premise was corrected in `docs/concepts/BENCHMARKS.md` and in the four benchmark adapters, all of which told the reader a smaller budget "can only lower" these metrics.
- **`posterior_weight` is documented as a staircase, not a dial ([#1584](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1584)).** Two candidates' scores differ by an expression affine in the weight, so any pair swaps at most once and every value between two adjacent crossings retrieves an identical ranking — a small step either does nothing or moves a rank outright. `docs/user/CONFIG.md` now says so, along with the two consequences that follow from the same algebra: a finite freeze weight always exists, and saturation discards BM25 rather than the posterior, which is the opposite of what "the rerank went inert" suggests.
- **`docs/user/CONFIG.md` no longer presents `posterior_weight` as a confidence signal ([#1592](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1592)).** The key blends `log(posterior_mean)` into the L1 BM25 score, and a reader would reasonably expect that posterior to reflect how a belief has performed. On a real store it mostly does not: aelfrice sets `(α, β)` at ingest from the belief's type and source, and about 95% of beliefs across a measured 26-store population still sit on exactly that value, so for most of a store the blend term is a fixed function of `(type, source)`. The new subsection tabulates every path that moves a posterior with its direction and default — `aelf confirm`, which is exempt from the [#1168](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1168) lock floor and so moves a locked belief; `aelf feedback`, which is not and so needs `aelf unlock` first; propagation of either, on by default; sentiment from prose, off unless enabled by environment variable **or** the `[feedback] sentiment_from_prose` key; the manual `aelf clamp-ghosts` sweep, which lowers α on exactly the beliefs that have no feedback and no corroborations; and store deduplication, which merges beliefs sharing a content hash and **sums** their `(α, β)` when the store is opened, so a merged belief's mean shifts with no user action at all. Retrieval exposure used to add `+0.1` per surfacing until [#1086](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1086) made it audit-only in July 2026, having measured that it promoted junk above real knowledge. The tuning advice is corrected in both directions: raising the weight makes retrieval more type-aware rather than more confidence-aware, and the two prior axes are not jointly ordered — an agent-inferred requirement (0.783) outranks a user-stated fact (0.750), so "user content ranks above agent content" holds only within a single type. The clustering onto a handful of posterior values that the staircase section already describes is now connected to its cause, rather than left reading as a quirk of a particular corpus. The sample TOML comment next to `posterior_weight = 0.5`, where someone tuning the key actually looks, carries the same correction.
- **A proposal for gating `aelf core` admission on content ([#1638](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1638)).** `docs/feature-core-admission-gate.md` proposes that a belief qualifies for core's non-lock arms only if an offline, cached classifier has judged it truth-apt. It records the measured problem, the expected effect, three precision configurations with their costs, a required self-verification check for silently failed batch runs, and the two questions still open. Nothing is implemented, and no shipped behavior changes.
- **The search hook's docs no longer say the model sees memory before it chooses a search ([#1646](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1646)).** The hook runs before the search tool, but the host shows its results next to the tool's own output ([hooks reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks)). By then the model has already chosen the tool and its query. The results shape the model's next step, not the search that fired the hook, and they don't let it skip that search. The module docstring, the search-tool comment, the `aelf setup --search-tool` help text, the design note and its diagram, and a test docstring now say so. The hook's own closing line, which the model reads, said "you may skip the tool call". It now says there's no need to search further if memory answers the question, and otherwise to use the tool's result to fill the gaps. Nothing else changed.

  The new closing line is 21 bytes longer than the old one, so each search-hook block grows by 21 bytes. The search-tool block measures 3,326 bytes before the budget and 2,630 after; the search-tool-bash block measures 1,931 before and 1,699 after. The 5.0.0 notes keep the counts measured at that release, and the markers that bound those counts to `benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py` move here, re-derived.
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py#search_tool_bytes_before = 3326 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py#search_tool_bytes_after = 2630 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py#search_tool_bash_bytes_before = 1931 -->
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py#search_tool_bash_bytes_after = 1699 -->
