- **The docs and code comments now state the defaults and mechanisms the code has ([#1654](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1654)).** A default-state audit found 18 statements that contradicted the code; 16 were still wrong and are fixed here, and 2 were already fixed on `main`. The onboard LLM classifier is default-on since v1.5, and `aelf onboard` falls back to the regex classifier when the SDK, the API key, or consent is missing; only `/aelf:onboard` uses the host's Task tool. Transcript ingest runs at `Stop` once 12 new lines (about six prompts) are logged, not only at the `PreCompact` rotation. The heat-kernel scorer went back to default-off in [#1162](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1162). Contradiction detection ships but is off by default, `aelf doctor --detect-stale` writes edges, no command writes a `SUPERSEDES` edge between two beliefs you name, and no command or hook creates a `RESOLVES` edge. `[cadence]` holds the off-by-default checkpoint cadence, not feedback scoring. The temporal-spine writer and the entity-persistence demotion lane are default-on, as their resolvers return. **A guard checks the listed documented defaults.** `scripts/check_doc_defaults.py` calls the resolver behind each listed `default-ON (is_...)` or `default-OFF (is_...)` source comment and each listed `Boolean, default` key in `CONFIG.md`, with no environment and no config file. It fails on a mismatch, and on a listed claim it can't find; a test runs it with the suite. No behavior changes.

### CI

- **The per-PR mutation job no longer spends its whole budget mutating a module you only commented on ([#1605](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1605)).** `.github/workflows/mutation.yml` scopes mutmut with `only_mutate`, whose entries are file paths, so a file enters the mutant set whole. A comment-only edit to a large module therefore cost as much as rewriting it: on PR #1603, whose diff changed **zero** non-comment lines of `cli.py`, all 11,048 lines entered the mutant set, the job hit its 60-minute limit twice in a row, and it was cancelled before writing a report — rendering as a red X indistinguishable at a glance from a surviving mutant, on an advisory check that still turns the PR `BLOCKED`. `scripts/mutation_scope.py` now decides the scope by comparing each changed file's AST **with docstrings stripped** between base and head, rather than by classifying lines: two versions whose stripped ASTs are equal differ only by comments, docstrings, blank lines, or formatting, none of which mutmut can mutate. The dump carries no attributes, so a comment added above a statement does not read as a change through its line numbers. It fails open — a file that will not parse on either side is mutated rather than skipped, because mutating one needlessly costs runner time while skipping one wrongly costs an unmeasured mutant. The decision lives in a script rather than in the workflow because the workflow cannot be tested in CI ([#1436](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1436) — PyYAML is not importable there); `tests/test_mutation_scope.py` covers it, including the control that a real `a + b` to `a - b` change is **not** skipped, since a scoper that skips everything passes the comment-only arm on its own. On PR #1603's diff the script keeps `host_codex.py` and drops `cli.py`. The weekly full-tree run is unchanged — it is the arm that gates, and this is only the per-PR advisory arm.
- **The per-PR mutation job mutates the functions a PR changed, not the whole files they live in ([#1632](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1632)).** A real change to `cli.py` still put all of its roughly 11,000 lines in `only_mutate`, and the job was cancelled at its 60-minute limit with no report. The cost is in mutant generation, not only in running mutants: mutmut 3.8.0 writes each mutant as a full copy of its function, so generating a whole large file such as `cli.py` takes minutes and produces tens of thousands of mutants, and filtering mutant names after generation leaves that cost in place. `scripts/mutation_scope.py` now maps the PR's diff hunks to the functions they touch: module-level functions and methods of module-level classes, which are the only units mutmut mutates. It then marks every other function with mutmut's documented `# pragma: no mutate block`, in the CI checkout only, so mutmut never generates their mutants; with one function of `cli.py` in scope, generation takes seconds. A touched function whose code is unchanged once docstrings and comments are set aside, such as one that only moved or sits in a renamed file, stays out of scope. Changed lines outside every function, such as module-level statements and class bodies, carry no mutant under mutmut, and neither do decorated functions or the methods mutmut skips by name. The step summary lists each of these with its reason, along with every function in scope. The per-PR job now pins mutmut to 3.8.0, the version this behavior is verified on. `--write-pragmas` edits files under `src/` in place, so it refuses to run unless `CI` is `true` or you pass `--allow-src-rewrite`. `tests/test_mutation_function_scope_1632.py` covers the mapping.
- **Merge-train no longer waits for the advisory mutation job to finish ([#1633](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1633)).** While `mutation (advisory, PR diff)` was running, the train counted it as a pending gate. The job can run for up to 60 minutes; on PRs that change `cli.py`, it ran for about 61 minutes before being cancelled ([#1632](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1632)). The train waits 10 minutes, so a PR with every gate green was bounced and had to be relabeled several times. Now the job's running state doesn't gate, and the train's log names it as still running. A failure result that arrives while the train is still polling still blocks. A result that arrives after the train has moved isn't read. That's safe only because the job has job-level `continue-on-error`, so a failed step doesn't make the job's result a failure. A test now fails if an exempt job loses that line.
- **Merge-train now runs only code from `main` ([#1695](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1695)).**
  - **Before:** the train triggered on `pull_request`, so its workflow file and the gate scripts it runs came from the pull request being judged, with a `contents: write` token. A pull request could change the gate that decided whether it merged.
  - **Trigger:** the train now triggers on `pull_request_target`. GitHub takes that workflow file from the default branch, the train checks out `main`, and it fetches the pull request's head only as git data, by an explicit refspec.
  - **Forks:** for a pull request from a fork, the workflow starts, but the merge job is skipped.
  - **Concurrency:** it moved from the workflow to the merge job. A run that skips the job, such as a push to an unlabelled pull request, no longer joins the queue, so it can't displace a labelled run that's waiting for its slot.
  - **Conflicted pull requests:** a labelled pull request with a merge conflict now gets the not-fast-forward refusal comment. Before, `pull_request` didn't run for it.
  - **Stacked pull requests:** they still get the refusal from [#1424](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1424).

### Internal

- **The git-recency lookup is tested with more than one file ([#1619](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1619)).** Every earlier recency test used one file and a one-entry map, so a lookup that gave every file the first entry's date passed the whole suite. New two-file fixtures, with distinct dates and the other file listed first, cover `extract_ast`, `extract_filesystem`, and the onboard handshake through `accept_classifications`. That mutant now fails two tests at each lookup site. No production code changed. The shipped lookups were already correct.
- **The lock-loss figures in [#1620](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1620) now have a producer.** Run `benchmarks/lock_loss_census_1620.py --store <path>` to recount the issue's measurements from a store. The census reports:

  - The `lock_level` distribution over active beliefs and over every row, separately. A time-boxed lock whose expiry is at or before `--now` counts as `user_expired_unswept`, because the product drops it the next time it opens the store. The census compares the two as instants, not as strings, so a `--now` in another UTC offset gives the same answer. `--now` must be an ISO-8601 instant with a UTC offset; the census rejects any other value and reports the instant it used in UTC.
  - Beliefs whose `lock_expires_at` isn't an ISO-8601 instant with a UTC offset, as `lock_expiry_unparseable`. The census doesn't count such a lock as expired.
  - Beliefs whose content is an aelfrice command: `/aelf:<name>`, or `aelf <subcommand>` with a subcommand the CLI registers.
  - Beliefs that hold a rendered `<belief id=` block, and active speculative-origin beliefs.
  - With `--pattern`, one instruction's family, with each member's posterior mean, corroboration count, and `lock:unlock` and `lock:expire` history.

  Stores are per repository, so you can pass `--store` more than once, and the cross-store view shows a lock that landed in another repository's store. The census opens each store through a `mode=ro` URI with `query_only` set, so it never writes the database file. On a WAL store that has no `-wal` or `-shm` file yet, SQLite creates both when the census opens it. The census truncates belief content in its output. `tests/test_lock_loss_census_1620.py` covers each figure on synthetic stores.
- **Tests now pin which directory a prompt's store comes from ([#1630](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1630)).** The UserPromptSubmit hook reads the store from its own working directory, and the payload's `cwd` field doesn't change that. Until now, no test held that choice, so a change could split one prompt across two stores without failing anything. New behavioral tests give the hook a payload `cwd` that names a different repository. They check that the shared store handle, the typed `/aelf:` command, the command-outcome row, and the session-state files still use the hook's own directory, that a typed `/aelf:scope-out` still takes effect on the next prompt, and that an explicit `AELFRICE_DB` still overrides both directories. A structural test covers the store consumers that a default prompt doesn't reach: it requires every `db_path()` call in the hook modules to take no arguments, rejects `chdir`, git-directory lookups at another directory, and any `AELFRICE_DB` override, and holds every `db_path()` call, every store construction in `hook.py`, and every store-layout path join to an explicit inventory. One pre-existing exception is listed in that inventory: the conversation-aware query and the context rebuilder read the transcript log under the payload `cwd`. No behavior changed. The host runs hooks in the session's current directory, and the [measurement on #1630](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1630#issuecomment-5974623437) found no genuine divergence between the two directories.
- **`aelfrice.hook` is no longer in an import cycle ([#1631](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1631)).** Run `scripts/import_cycles.py` to list every import cycle in the package. The script builds the first-party import graph from the source, counts imports at every scope, and prints each strongly connected component of two or more modules. Before this change it reported a five-module component: `cli`, `doctor`, `hook`, `hook_search_tool`, and `provenance_render`. Every edge in that component was a function-local import, so nothing failed when the package loaded. Inside the component, `doctor`, `hook_search_tool`, and `provenance_render` each imported from `aelfrice.hook`, through five import statements in all, and those statements are gone now. The two render-time escapers moved to the leaf module `aelfrice.hook_payload`, so `provenance_render` and the search hook no longer import `aelfrice.hook`. The `[memory_block]` switch and the `UserPromptSubmit` telemetry reader moved to `aelfrice.hook_audit`, so `aelf doctor` no longer imports `aelfrice.hook`. `aelfrice.hook` still exports every moved public name, so existing `from aelfrice.hook import ...` callers keep working, and `tests/test_hook_reexports_1631.py` pins that. The private names it no longer uses itself, `_escape_attr` and the three helpers behind the switch, are not re-exported; import `escape_attr` from `aelfrice.hook_payload`. Both leaf modules were already in the hook's import closure, so `import aelfrice.hook` loads the same set of modules as before. The script now reports three cycles of two modules each: `bfs_multihop` and `edge_rerank`, `context_rebuilder` and `working_state`, and `expansion_gate` and `retrieval`. This change doesn't touch them. The script doesn't draw edges built with `importlib.import_module`, such as the hook's lazy loader for its retrieval names. `tests/test_import_cycles_1631.py` reads the whole graph and fails if any cycle contains `aelfrice.hook`. Putting back any one of the five removed imports makes that test fail.
- **The Stop hook's comments no longer say that Stop has no `additionalContext` channel ([#1651](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1651)).** It does have one. The lock prompt still goes to stderr, and the comment now gives the real reason: the listing is for the user, and `additionalContext` would continue the conversation and cost the user a turn. A test comment made the same claim and is corrected too. No behavior changed.
- **The recency-keying tests use nested files that share a basename ([#1679](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1679)).** The #1619 fixtures put every file at the repository root, where a file's basename equals its relative path, so a lookup keyed on the basename passed them. The fixtures now use `p/m` and `q/m` with distinct dates, and the onboard handshake gives each of its four files its own date. A basename-keyed or first-entry lookup now fails two tests at each lookup site. No production code changed.
- **Tests now cover a file missing from a non-empty recency map ([#1682](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1682)).** Earlier fixtures gave every file an entry or used an empty map, so a lookup that fell back to another file's date when a file had no entry passed them. New fixtures for `extract_ast` and `extract_filesystem` leave `p/m` out of the map, date its sibling `p/n` and a file in another directory, and assert that `p/m` has no commit date. An onboard handshake with one committed and one untracked directory asserts that the untracked files' beliefs take the `now` passed to `accept_classifications` as `created_at`, not the committed date. A fallback to any other entry now fails two tests at each lookup site, and a fallback limited to the same directory fails one. No production code changed.
- **The search-tool and agent-context hooks no longer carry a payload-`cwd` branch that could never run ([#1701](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1701)).** Both hooks read the payload `cwd` and passed it to `db_path(cwd=...)` behind a signature probe, but `db_path()` takes no `cwd`, so the probe was always false and both always used the hook process's cwd. The probe and the unused `cwd` read are gone, and a comment states the rule [#1630](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1630) settled: the store resolves from the process cwd, which the host sets to the session's directory. `tests/test_hook_process_cwd_store_1701.py` gives each hook a payload `cwd` in another repository and fails if a hook reads that repository's store. Behavior is unchanged.

## [5.0.0] - 2026-09-18

The major bump is for three removals, not for a redesign: `src/aelfrice/uri_baki.py`
leaves the shipped package, `hook.DEFAULT_SESSION_START_TOKEN_BUDGET` and the
`token_budget` parameter of `hook.session_start()` are deleted, and the unwired
posterior-decay surface is deleted with every documentation statement that rested on it.
Each was provably unreachable — no module in the package ever imported `uri_baki`, the
deleted budget could not bind on the lane it governed at any value, and nothing had ever
moved a stored posterior toward a prior — so no working configuration changes behaviour
here. The version reflects that the package surface shrank, which is what semantic
versioning asks it to reflect.

**The theme of this release is measuring what the project had been asserting.** Several
long-standing claims turned out to be false when someone finally derived them, and the
fixes are mostly the instruments that make the claim checkable rather than the claim
restated. A published figure now names the script that re-derives it and CI runs that
script ([#1469](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1469)); the gate
enforcing it was itself blind to most of the corpus until a real block scanner replaced a
backtick-pairing regex ([#1556](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1556));
`pyright` is enforced and the four documents claiming it already passed are corrected
([#1503](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1503)); and seven
user-facing documentation statements that disagreed with the code are fixed
([#1511](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1511)).

**The injection path is the other half.** The seven injection lanes now charge the line
their own renderer emits rather than a compressed form no renderer produces
([#1526](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1526)), the injected block
is bounded again with user locks exempt from the bound
([#1551](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1551)), a belief shown in
the session-start sub-block is no longer shown a second time in the same envelope
([#1547](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1547)), and a
cadence-resume recap no longer sheds the prompt's own retrieval hits to keep a synthesis
of an earlier session ([#1564](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1564)).
**No budget constant moved.** The re-tune those corrections were expected to enable is
gated on an answer-quality instrument, and the census built to price that instrument
reports the effect is not measurable against any corpus this repository can reach
([#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546)).

Store safety gained two fixes worth calling out for anyone running against a repository
they cannot write: a `#` or `?` anywhere in the store path used to make the read-only open
read *a different database* and create it, then report that a current store needed a
migration ([#1416](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1416)).

### Added

- **An opt-in turn-differential for the injected memory block ([#1382](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1382)).** With `AELFRICE_TURN_DIFFERENTIAL=1`, a belief already injected verbatim in this session is re-injected as a one-line `seen <id>: "<topic>"` entry in the locks manifest instead of the identical `<belief>` element, because the full text is already above in the same context window. **Default-off**, ratified 2026-08-19, reversing the 2026-08-11 default-on ruling — that ruling rested on the failure direction being one-way, and adversarial review of the implementing PR showed it was not. `begin_epoch` sat inside a `if body:` guard, so a store with no locked beliefs rendered an empty SessionStart baseline, never opened an epoch, and left the pre-compaction ledger live under the same `session_id`; every later turn then emitted `seen <id>` for text the window no longer held, reducing that belief to an 80-character stub for the rest of the session. Fixed three ways: the epoch reset is unconditional, `PreCompact` resets too (it is the event actually guaranteed to precede compaction, and it fires whatever the rebuilder's `trigger_mode` is), and a boundary carrying no `session_id` now deletes the ledger rather than returning early and leaving it live. The second reason for default-off is that the saving can be negative: a `seen` entry opens the `<aelfrice-locks-manifest>` wrapper on a block that had none, a fixed ~237 characters, so a single-belief block must exceed ~310 characters of content before the change saves anything — against a recorded median content length of 86. Small blocks of short beliefs get bigger; large or long blocks get much smaller. No aggregate percentage is published, because nothing in the tree re-derives one. Writes are gated on `emit_memory_block` ([#1359](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1359)): a suppressed fire put nothing in the window, so recording it would reference content the model never saw.
- **`scripts/check_doc_preservation.py` makes a documentation rewrite reviewable ([#1509](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1509)).** A style conversion is a large diff with no behaviour change, which is the shape a reviewer skims, and the failure it hides is a lost fact: a figure rounded, a citation dropped out of a shortened sentence, a table row lost in a reflow. None of those break a build. The script compares a file against its pre-rewrite form and fails on any lost number, URL, code block, inline code span, table row, section or front-matter key, and on an in-document anchor that a rewording orphaned. It is one-directional by design — adding a subheading is a legitimate conversion move, dropping one is not — and it never reports prose differences, because prose changing is the point. Heading *wording* is deliberately not compared: removing idiom from a heading is required by the style, so a checker that flagged it would forbid the conversion it verifies. `tests/test_check_doc_preservation.py` is a mutation suite: a faithful conversion must pass, and dropping a figure, rounding one, dropping a citation, renaming an identifier, editing a code block, dropping a table row, dropping a section, renaming the title and orphaning an anchor must each fail. The known gap is pinned rather than left to be discovered — numbers compare as a multiset, so a rewrite that swaps two figures between sentences passes, and `test_a_swapped_pair_of_numbers_is_not_detected` fails loudly if that ever changes.
- **`benchmarks/scored_query_census.py` makes the #1516 population gate re-runnable ([#1516](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1516)).** #1516 parked a re-measurement of #1348's top-10 movement behind a threshold committed before any measurement was taken — run it when `input.scored_query` holds **>= 500 distinct queries spanning >= 30 days** — and the only way to decide that was a hand-written scan that went stale between askings. The census scans every `rebuild_logs/*.jsonl` reachable from a root and reports the six numbers the bound needs kept apart: rows scanned, rows carrying `input.scored_query`, **distinct** scored queries, days with at least one, the calendar span, and a per-day breakdown of rows against distinct. Both halves of the bound have a cheaper neighbour that would pass the gate early — row count is not distinct queries, and active days are not a calendar span — so `tests/test_scored_query_census_1516.py` feeds synthetic rows in which those numbers deliberately differ and pins each separately. The two bounds are module constants with **no CLI flag**, because a flag lets a re-run lower the bar it is checking and still look like the same census; a test asserts the flags do not exist. Exit codes separate "could not census" (2) from "censused, a bound is unmet, do not measure" (3). The logs are repo-local — `<git-common-dir>/aelfrice/rebuild_logs/` per repo, plus `~/.aelfrice/rebuild_logs` for work outside any work-tree — so `--discover` finds both layouts; a census that knew only about `~/.aelfrice` would report a near-empty population. Rows are read through `rebuild_log_query_population.load_rows` so the two censuses cannot drift on what counts as a parseable row, and nothing here opens a `MemoryStore`, since opening one runs pending migrations. Both flags are repeatable, so directories are resolved before they are de-duplicated: one directory named twice is read once, rather than doubling every row count under a corpus identity that still reports one file. A query's identity is its stripped form, the same form blankness is judged on, so whitespace cannot split one query into several and inflate the number the 500-query bound is checked against.
- **`aelf show <belief-id>` reads one belief back by id ([#1553](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1553)).** Every injected `<belief>` element carries an id, and nothing turned that id back into the belief's full text. None of the existing surfaces was a substitute: `aelf search` is FTS5 over *content*, so an id matches on whatever it happens to tokenize into and is neither guaranteed to come back nor to come back first; `aelf graph` is anchored by id but returns edges and neighbours; `locked`, `core` and `speculative` are tier listings, not lookups. The `show` that existed was `category show`, and it shows a category. The command prints the content in full plus the origin, lock level, retention class, posterior, created and valid-to timestamps, and scope — one field per line in a fixed order, with the content last and unindented so a multi-line body stays unambiguous to read and to parse.

  Addressing, not search. The new `MemoryStore.find_beliefs_by_id_prefix` is the first prefix lookup over `beliefs.id` in the store — `delete`, `retire`, `unlock` and `context` all demand the exact id — and it returns the whole matching set rather than the first row, so the caller names the ambiguity instead of guessing: two candidates exit 1 with both ids on stderr and nothing on stdout. An exact id wins outright over being a prefix of a longer one, so a short id still addresses itself. The prefix arm is `substr(id, 1, ?) = ?` rather than the index-friendlier `LIKE prefix || '%'`, because a `%` or `_` in a pasted id would otherwise silently become a wildcard — turning the exact lookup the command exists to provide back into the search it is meant to replace.

  Tombstones are included, unlike `get_belief`, whose lifecycle filter (#1210) hides them: the id in an injected block from last week may well name a retired belief, so `show` reaches it and labels it `status: retired`. A missing id is distinguishable from a belief whose content is empty — the first prints `no belief with id` on stderr and exits 1, the second prints its field block with an empty body and exits 0.

  The store opens through `open_store_for_read()`, making `show` the fifth command routed to the #1416 path and the first added since that ruling — a per-command decision with the audit that ruling asks for, not the blanket change it refused. What the routing buys is stated exactly: the command changes no belief, and it reads a store the caller cannot write. It does **not** make the command write-free. `open_store_for_read` attempts the ordinary writable open first and falls back to `mode=ro` only on a permission failure, so against a writable store `show` still pays the full store open (DDL, migrations, the scope-id mint, the expired-lock sweep) to print one row. That cost is real and unaddressed here; it is re-filed as its own issue over every read-only verb rather than settled inside this command. The audit's one finding is that on the *fallback* handle no expired-lock sweep has run (#1314), so a lock past its `lock_expires_at` still prints `lock: user` there — the same staleness `aelf locked` already carries on that path, on a command that reports a stored field rather than acting on it. The routing is proved behaviourally rather than by a call-site grep: against a directory at mode 555 whose schema is one table short, a writable open dies in `MemoryStore.__init__` before the belief is read, and the test asserts `show` exits 0 and leaves the database byte-identical.

  Whether an injected `<belief>` should now advertise `aelf show` is deliberately not settled here. #1547 shipped its byte reduction without a pointer, on the ruling that naming a command the CLI does not implement is worse than shipping no pointer; the command now exists, and pointing at it is a separate decision.

### Changed

- **The transcript-admission harness reports the rescued set split by kind ([#1490](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1490)).** `rescued_distinct` said how many sentences the relaxed filter admits and nothing about what kind of thing they were, and the kinds behave very differently once stored: a durable policy statement earns its slot, while an ephemeral session-status line ("No failures yet.") is true of one run and of nothing afterwards — yet both enter through `derive()`'s `role=user` transcript path at the undeflated `USER_SOURCE` prior ([#888](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/888)), storing as `factual`/`user_transcript` at `alpha=3.0, beta=1.0` (posterior mean 0.75, origin rank 3), so they compete with durable policy for the same injection slots. `benchmarks/transcript_noise_admission.py` now buckets into `ephemeral_status`, `operator_directive`, `durable_policy` and `unclassified`. Nothing reaches the ingest write path. A shape detector *would* have reached it, and that option was rejected twice — on 2026-08-11 and again on 2026-08-12 — as "the same widening #1371 §1 just narrowed". A wrong bucket therefore produces a wrong number in a manually-run report, and never a dropped belief. `unclassified` is the deliverable rather than a leftover — CI can never watch these counts, because the corpus is an untracked local archive, so a rise there is the only available signal that the closed set of three has stopped describing what the filter admits. `tests/test_transcript_noise_buckets_1490.py` pins that the split is total (a sentence matching no rule is counted, not dropped), that every declared bucket is reachable (a dead bucket is a permanent zero column that reads as a measurement), and that an unseen shape reaches `unclassified` instead of being absorbed. The disclosure comment in `src/aelfrice/noise_filter.py` is stamped with **both** figures and is explicit that the finding is no longer reproducible: the measurement of record is 6 distinct / 9 rows over 17,628 sentences at `corpus_sha256 3ad68670…` (tree `c6f0c0c3`, 2026-08-12), and a re-run on 2026-08-19 yields 0 / 0 over 18 sentences at `corpus_sha256 cffcf557…` because the transcript archive was removed from disk around 2026-08-13 — not by rotation, since `transcript_logger` contains no pruning code. Quoting only the fresh run would report a degenerate corpus as the finding; quoting only the old one would imply it is still checkable.
- **The H1 directive gate is a tripwire, so the bench tier is no longer red by design ([#1502](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1502)).** `test_directive_detection_gate` asserted `precision >= 0.80`, but that bar is the *reopening* criterion for work `docs/design/v2_enforcement.md` § H1 documents as deferred — so red was its correct, permanent, shipped state. It could never go green, and it held the whole bench-gate tier red at every release cut, in the tier that [#1477](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1477) made the project's only scheduled quality signal. A check that can never be green cannot be distinguished from a broken one, and it teaches a reader to skip the reds beside it. Inverted as `test_h1_reentry_bar_is_still_unmet`: green while the bar is unmet, and it fires when `detect_directive` clears both bars — the event that actually reopens [#374](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/374). The assertion is the conjunction of precision and recall, because that is how § H1 states the criterion, and the failure text says to reopen the issue rather than adjust the test. It also stamps the corpus digest, computed rather than pinned, because the corpus is now a union of v0.1 and v0.2 and the two score differently. Its companion `test_directive_corpus_defeats_a_first_token_baseline` goes **green** in the same change, once the v0.2 minimal-pair corpus is present: the one-token baseline clears the bar on 0 of 200 salted partitions against the union, where it cleared 196 of 200 against v0.1 alone. Latest measurement on the 510-row union: precision 0.665, recall 0.636 — H1 stays deferred, now on a number that means what § H1 intends.
- **The public documentation follows the Google developer documentation style guide.** Twenty-three files — `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `docs/README.md`, everything under `docs/user/` and `docs/concepts/`, and the four index `README.md` files — are written to [Google's style guide](https://developers.google.com/style): second person, present tense, active voice, sentence-case headings, the condition stated before the instruction, serial commas, US spelling, descriptive link text, and the guide's [word list](https://developers.google.com/style/word-list). Table cells were converted as well as paragraphs, 236 of 390 rows, the remainder being data cells that hold a flag, a default, a type, a path, or a version. British spellings outside code fall from 92 to 9, and those 9 are `analysis` and its inflections, which is already US spelling, plus one verbatim quote of a shipped CLI string. Records stay out of scope, because rewriting one changes what it says happened: `CHANGELOG/`, `docs/audits/` bodies, `docs/design/historical/`, and the numbered ADRs. `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` stays out too, as adopted normative text attributed to the Contributor Covenant. No fact changes: every numeric literal, URL, link target, fenced code block, inline code span, table row and section that appears in the pre-conversion file still appears, verified per file by `scripts/check_doc_preservation.py`. The one deliberate exception is `CONTRIBUTING.md`, whose `Documentation style` section now names this guide and so no longer carries the two sentence-length limits it used to state; that is a content change rather than a conversion, and the checker reports it rather than being loosened to permit it.

### Removed

- **The unwired posterior-decay surface, and every doc statement resting on it ([#1369](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1369), discharging [#1162](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1162) §4).** `src/aelfrice/scoring.py` defined a Jeffreys-targeted `(alpha, beta)` ager, a type half-life lookup and a four-entry half-life table. No module under `src/` ever imported or reached any of the three — `tests/test_docs_posterior_decay_1218.py` had asserted that fact by parsing the tree since [#1218](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1218) — so nothing has ever moved a stored posterior toward a prior, and the lock short-circuit inside the ager exempted locks from a mechanism that did not run. [#1162](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1162)'s acceptance criterion is wire-it-or-delete-it and the operator authorized deletion on 2026-08-05; wiring it needs a calibration bench that is not funded. Three private constants went with it (`_HOUR`, `_PRIOR_ALPHA`, `_PRIOR_BETA`) and so did the `LOCK_USER` import, which the short-circuit was the only reader of. The two pre-registered property tests written against the function — decay-required and lock-floor sharpness — are retired rather than rewritten: both exercised the function rather than the pipeline, which is exactly why decay-required passed while the property it protected was violated in the shipping product. The [#1218](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1218) guard is rewritten instead of deleted, and it now fails if the surface comes back, so the prose is revisited rather than going stale in the other direction. The documentation statements that rested on it are corrected, not just the two the issue named: `ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 5, its `scoring.py` module row, its property-test row and its claim that decay penalizes candidates from old branches; `PHILOSOPHY.md`'s "Locks, not just decay" section and its deferred-feedback paragraph; `COMMANDS.md`'s defaults table, which advertised the decay target and the four half-lives as tunable constants; `LIMITATIONS.md`'s "a belief whose evidence decayed fully"; `bayesian_ranking.md`'s numerical-safety argument, which derived a bound from the ager; `ROADMAP.md`'s "v1 ships per-belief decay"; the three "a lock short-circuits decay" lines in `README.md`, `QUICKSTART.md` and `INSTALL.md`, which promised an exemption from nothing; and the entry in `docs/design/historical/substrate_decision.md`'s single-axis consumer audit, struck in place rather than deleted, on the pattern `docs/design/historical/README.md` already uses for `RetrievalCache` after [#1418](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1418). Two mechanisms keep the word *decay* and neither touches a stored posterior: `retrieval._apply_temporal_decay` discounts a ranking score behind the default-off `temporal_sort`, and `meta_beliefs.decay_toward_default` ages a meta-belief toward its configured default. The rewritten guard pins both by name, because the corrected prose now cites them.
- **The `uri_baki` post-rank adjusters leave the shipped package ([#1369](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1369), under [#1162](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1162)).** `src/aelfrice/uri_baki.py` held three pure-function score adjusters — `apply_locked_floor`, `apply_supersession_demote`, `apply_recency_decay` — and no module in the package has ever imported it: `grep -rn 'aelfrice\.uri_baki\|aelfrice/uri_baki' src/` returned two comments pointing at it and nothing else, and the only importers were `benchmarks/uri_baki_retest/harness.py` and `tests/test_uri_baki.py`. The module is now `benchmarks/uri_baki_retest/adjusters.py`, beside the retest that is issue [#153](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/153)'s actual deliverable. It moves rather than being deleted because `benchmarks/uri_baki_retest/RESULTS.md` publishes NDCG figures this code produces, and deleting the producer would leave those numbers unreproducible; the retest returned an honest negative and the approach is filed as not-adopted there. The two comments that pointed at the old path now name the new one, and `retrieval._supersession_penalty` keeps its warning that the multiplicative primitive is wrong for a log-domain score — the point that comment exists to make survives the move, because the primitive still exists to be misread. The `src/aelfrice/` file count in `docs/concepts/ARCHITECTURE.md` is derived by `tests/test_docs_truth_1511.py`, so it tracks the tree rather than drifting.
- **`hook.DEFAULT_SESSION_START_TOKEN_BUDGET` and the `token_budget` parameter of `hook.session_start()` are deleted; the SessionStart block is bounded by the lock count ([#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546)).** The constant could not bind on the lane it governed, at any value. `session_start` retrieves through `_retrieve_baseline_with_block`, which calls `retrieve()` with an **empty query**, and in `retrieve_with_tiers` every relevance lane — L2.5, L1, HRR expansion, the temporal spine and the BFS hop — is gated on `query.strip()`. Only L0 contributes, and L0 is appended unconditionally and never trimmed ([#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379)), so the pack loop a budget drives is never entered. Reproduced before the deletion on a 300-lock store of 150-character beliefs: 300 hits and 61,144 rendered bytes at `token_budget=1`, at 1500 and at 100000 alike. **The knob the deletion exposes is the TOML key, not the environment variable.** `resolve_token_budget`'s precedence is environment variable, then the explicit keyword argument, then `[retrieval] token_budget` in `.aelfrice.toml`, then the default, so `AELFRICE_RETRIEVAL_TOKEN_BUDGET` already outranked the budget this caller passed and already reached the lane: `resolve_token_budget(1500, start=<dir>)` returns 111 with that variable set to `111`. What is new is the TOML key, which the explicit argument used to shadow — with `[retrieval] token_budget = 77` on disk, the same call returns 1500 in the pre-deletion shape (`explicit=1500`) and 77 in the post-deletion one (`explicit=None`). Both are inert here, because the pack loop is never entered: the guard below drives the production call under four environment values, under `[retrieval] token_budget = 1`, and under neither, and gets the store's whole lock set and one block length from all six arms, on a 10-lock store and on a 300-lock one alike. The bound that remains is the one [#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379) states: every locked belief is injected, and the lock count is the operator's knob.

  The guard is kept and re-aimed. `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py::test_session_start_lane_never_trims_its_l0_pool` (renamed from `test_session_start_budget_does_not_bind_on_its_own_lane`) asserts the [#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379) contract directly rather than asserting a now-deleted constant: it drives the real `_retrieve_baseline_with_block` at the production call shape, varies the budget across four orders of magnitude through the environment variable, once through `[retrieval] token_budget` and once through neither, and requires every belief from `list_locked_beliefs()` in both the hit list and the rendered block each time — by content in the block, not by id, because the id sits in the `<belief id="…"` attribute ahead of the content and a per-line cap would leave every id in place while truncating every lock. The fixture's locks run past every per-belief character cap the two injection modules ship, and the test discovers that set by name shape and asserts the lock length above its maximum rather than above one named constant. Sizing it against one constant is what the first version of this guard got wrong: it was built against `hook_search_tool.PER_LINE_CHAR_CAP = 200`, the truncation already shipped on the `Grep|Glob` lane, while `hook` — the module that renders this very block — ships `STOP_PROMPT_MAX_CONTENT = 1000` for the identical reason. Truncating every lock at 1,000 characters in `_format_baseline_hits` left the whole suite green. A maximum over a discovered set rises on its own when the next cap lands **under one of the name shapes the discovery matches** (`CHAR_CAP`, `MAX_CONTENT`, `_CHARS`); a cap named otherwise is not found, the floor does not rise, and a trim at it above the fixture's padding would pass. The two known caps are also required by name, so a rename fails loudly instead of shrinking the set — but discovery is a name convention rather than a proof, and the entry says so rather than letting "rises on its own" read as unconditional. The TOML arm asserts its own resolution before it measures, so a config file the resolver never discovers fails instead of agreeing with the other arms for the wrong reason. The assertion is absolute rather than an identity across arms, because a trim that does not vary with the budget agrees with every arm and still breaks #379; and it runs on two store sizes, because any fixture is blind to a cap above its own lock count. Proved by mutation: `hits = hits[:5]` and a budget-driven pack loop each fail both sizes, `hits = hits[:50]` fails the 300-lock size and is invisible to the 10-lock one, and giving the lane a non-empty query fails the 10-lock size, where 50 candidate slots leave room for the unlocked decoys that the 300 locks would fill. Truncating each rendered belief's content in `_format_baseline_hits` fails both sizes at 200 characters and at the discovered maximum's 1,000 — the mutation an id-presence assert could not see, because every id survives it and the block length stays identical across arms, and the one a fixture sized for 200 could not see either. `benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py` keeps measuring the lane, at an explicit `SESSION_START_PROBE_BUDGET` literal of its own with the production path's "passes none" stated beside it, and still reports which cap ended each arm (`token_budget` / `l25_subbudget` / `both` / `pool`) — the `session_start` row reads `pool` for both arms at every content length, which is the evidence for this deletion rather than a restatement of it. Both halves of that legend are guarded on the producer's own stdout, not on the helper that builds it. `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py::test_the_producer_says_the_session_start_number_is_its_own_probe` runs `main(["--curve"])` and reads the printed lines, because the helper has two call sites and reverting only those to the pre-branch f-strings — leaving the helper, its docstring and a helper-level assertion all in place — puts `session_start: budget 1500 unchanged, emitted bytes 1408 -> 1408 (+0.0%) at 92 content chars` back on stdout with the full suite green. `--curve` prints both call sites, both directions are asserted, and every lane must be found in the output or one that stopped printing would pass vacuously. The probe value carries a derived-figure marker on the SessionStart row of the [#1526](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1526) entry's table, so the `derived-figures` job re-derives it from the module and exits 1 on a mismatch — and `test_the_producer_reads_the_session_start_budget_off_its_own_constant` asserts the wire that marker runs through, by monkeypatching the constant and requiring `shipped_budget("session_start")` to return it. Written back as the literal `1500`, that entry leaves the suite green and the gate at exit 0 while the constant says 3000 and the CHANGELOG publishes 1500: a marker comparing a literal against a literal.

  **A user-facing page promised the budget this deletion removes, and it now says what the bound really is.** `docs/user/PRIVACY.md` listed "a token budget for each query … 1,500 for the UserPromptSubmit hook and the SessionStart hook" under *What aelfrice does not control*, as a mitigation limiting what reaches the cloud model. The SessionStart half was never reachable, and this deletion is what severs it from the code, so the page now states the two budgets that are real, says the budget trims the query-relevant lanes rather than the locks, and gives the SessionStart block its actual bound: the lock count, readable with `aelf locked`. `docs/user/CONFIG.md` gains the other half — `[retrieval] token_budget` now reaches that lane, and cannot change it. The page's other promises have guards and this one now has the half that CI can run: `tests/test_privacy_budget_claims_1546.py` holds the "carries no token budget" sentence to the signature of both frames on the lane and to the absence of the deleted constant, and holds the CONFIG.md claim to the call rather than to those signatures. A signature cannot see that claim: restoring `token_budget=1500` to the `retrieve()` call inside `_retrieve_baseline_with_block` leaves both signatures clean, re-shadows the TOML key and makes the page false. So the third test replaces the callable the lane resolves with a recorder and feeds the resolver exactly what the caller passed, which lands on the 77 in the file on the shipped code and on 1500 under that mutation; the narrower assertion that `token_budget` is absent from the kwargs runs after it, because behind that assertion the resolver could only ever be handed `None` and would never run at all on the mutation it exists to catch. **The page itself is deliberately not read, and that is a gap with a number.** A test under `tests/` that reads `docs/` **and spells the path inline** — `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "docs"`, or the `.parent.parent` form — fails `tests/test_ci_path_filter.py::test_code_filter_covers_every_in_repo_package_the_suite_uses`, because `docs/**` is not in `ci.yml`'s `code` filter. Its scanner is two literal regexes over those two forms, so binding the repo root to a name first and joining `"docs"` onto it passes, which is the spelling `test_ci_path_filter.py` uses for its own `_REPO`. That spelling is not the way out: the filter is what decides whether the suite runs at all, so a guard on the page smuggled past it would be skipped on exactly the docs-only pull request that changes the page — the #1160 failure mode, a required check reporting green from an `echo`. The way out is the filter, and adding `docs/**` to it fails `test_docs_only_changes_still_skip_the_suite` and `test_e2e_paths_cover_the_ci_code_filter`, since `e2e.yml`'s `paths` mirrors that filter — measured by adding the one line and running the module. Widening puts the pytest matrix and e2e's three-leg install matrix on every user-doc pull request, reversing [#413](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/413)/[#427](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/427) and [#1420](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1420) §1 AC2, so it is an operator call rather than a side effect of this branch. Until it is made, a change to the page alone is caught by review, not by CI. The [#1469](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1469) derived-figure marker is not the mechanism, because it does not work in this file: `scripts/check_derived_figures.py` blanks inline code before parsing markers and pairs backticks across the whole file, and a triple-backtick fence line carries an odd one — `PRIVACY.md` has six fence lines, and two markers planted in it parsed as zero while the gate reported green over prose that contradicted them. Removing the fence lines from a copy makes both parse. That is a hole in the gate, filed as [#1556](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1556) rather than worked around here: on the branch this one was cut from, three regex repairs each fixed the previous reading and introduced a new one, so deciding which text is code wants a block scanner and its own acceptance criteria. Until #1556 lands the two figures carry no marker, so they are held by review rather than by the `derived-figures` job.

  **The budget re-tune this deletion unblocks stays parked, and no budget constant moves.** A byte count is not an acceptable basis for choosing these numbers: the effect of the [#1526](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1526) accounting correction is the ratio of a rendered line to its content, which is largest on the shortest beliefs and shrinks towards parity as they grow, so any constant picked to hold bytes flat holds them flat at one belief length and deviates either side — and on the `Grep|Glob` lane, which truncates at `PER_LINE_CHAR_CAP`, it changes sign. Three things would unblock the re-tune, and all three are required. First, a **gold set with per-turn answer-quality labels**, and an A/B of the current budgets against the proposed ones reported at answer level, not in injected bytes. Second, a **decision rule fixed in writing before the numbers arrive**, so the budget is not fitted to whichever arm won. Third, the **per-lane latency cost**, because raising a budget raises pack work on every hook fire. LongMemEval-S has been described here as saturated on recall and therefore unable to distinguish the arms, and that description carried a recall figure. **The figure is struck, because nothing in this repo re-derives it.** No producer emits it, no file under `benchmarks/results/` records a recall key at all, and `benchmarks/longmemeval_adapter.py` — which does compute recall@k — reads `longmemeval_oracle.json` from HuggingFace, a corpus that is not committed and that public CI cannot fetch. An unsourced number stating the reason a corpus cannot distinguish the arms is the premise doing the work, so it is removed rather than re-quoted; the claim that LongMemEval-S is saturated is now explicitly unsourced and owed a measurement. The argument around it stands: if no available corpus can distinguish the arms, that is the finding, and the right outcome is to leave the constants where they are and record why.

### Fixed

- **`aelf doctor --host codex` fails when the installed wiring can't reach `aelf`, and the Codex hooks feature is named correctly ([#1413](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1413), partial).** `uv tool install aelfrice` is the one supported channel and it puts `aelf` in a bin directory, so a host whose `PATH` doesn't resolve `aelf` has that directory off `PATH` and can't run any documented `aelf …` command by name — and doctor, the one surface that could have said so, exited 0. It now appends a `[FAIL]` line naming the `uv tool install aelfrice` and `uv tool update-shell` remedy, and exits 1, under the operator ruling of 2026-08-12: the check fires only when `launcher.which_on_path("aelf")` is `None` **and** `report.owned_handler_count` is greater than zero. That gate is the same one [#1430](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1430) already ratified for this command — absent wiring stays exit 0, so a source checkout and this repo's own CI, neither of which has a `uv tool` install, stay green. The line reports the `PATH` state itself and claims nothing beyond it: the generated skills still route through `uv run aelf`, and `aelf setup --host codex` pins each hook handler to an absolute `aelf-*` path whenever it can resolve one, so neither surface necessarily breaks the moment `aelf` leaves `PATH`. `docs/user/SLASH_COMMANDS.md` also told you to turn on a `codex_hooks` feature flag that Codex retired after 0.12x; `codex features list` on codex-cli 0.145.0 reports one row for it, `hooks stable true`, and no `codex_hooks` row at all. The caveat now names `hooks`, says it's on by default, points at `[features].hooks = false` as the only setting you need to act on, and records that doctor treats that setting as a `[FAIL]` and exit 1 once the handlers are installed; doctor keeps honoring the legacy key when an old `config.toml` carries it, and the doc says so. The rest of the issue — replacing the `uv run aelf` invocations in the generated skills, re-derived on this branch as 96 of them across all 31 skills — stays open, because changing the generated skill bytes makes every already-installed Codex skill differ from the build and needs a Codex host to verify.
- **`graph`, `stale`, `introspect` and `core` dumped a traceback against a store they couldn't write ([#1416](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1416)).** These four still called `_open_store()`, so on a non-writable store they died in `MemoryStore.__init__` with a raw `sqlite3.OperationalError: attempt to write a readonly database`. The CLI catches `ReadOnlyStoreUnavailable` and nothing else, so the exception escaped the command entirely rather than becoming the one actionable line the already-routed commands give. They now open through `open_store_for_read()`, which attempts the ordinary writable open first and falls back to `mode=ro` only when that open is refused for lack of write access.

  Four handlers, each audited on its own, which is what the 2026-08-09 ruling asked for in place of a blanket change. `graph` resolves seeds and walks `expand_bfs`; `stale` runs one `SELECT` with two date thresholds; `introspect` hands the store to `introspect.build_report`; `core` composes `list_locked_beliefs`, `list_belief_ids` and `get_belief`. All four read only, proved by the database file staying byte-identical across each command on a frozen store rather than by reading the code. `stale --locked-only` and `core`'s locked arm carry one finding: on the read-only fallback no expired-lock sweep has run (#1314), so a belief past its `lock_expires_at` still counts as locked there, the same staleness `locked` already carries on that path.

  Five of the six line numbers in the issue's comments land outside the handler they name: only `_cmd_graph`'s is right, and the rest fall in `_cmd_show`, `_cmd_restore`, `_emit_introspect_text`, `_cmd_confirm` and `_posterior`. The one that bites is the number cited as `_cmd_core`, which is the last line of `_cmd_confirm` — a writer, since it calls `apply_feedback(..., respect_lock=False)`. Routing a writer onto a read-only handle would have shipped a bug. So every edited call site was mapped back to its enclosing `def` by an abstract-syntax-tree walk before the edit, and the scope test resolves the routed set the same way — by AST, over call nodes only — rather than by searching each handler's source text, which cannot tell a call from the prose that describes it. `_cmd_confirm` and `_cmd_resolve` are pinned there as writers, so an edit that lands one line off fails in the suite rather than in a user's store.

  What the routing does **not** buy is a cheaper open. Against a writable store the handle is the writable one, so these four still pay the schema battery, the migrations, the scope-id mint and the expired-lock sweep, exactly as every other command does. That cost is tracked separately, over every read-only verb.

  **Not verified on Windows, and not verified on a managed Codex host.** `tests/test_readonly_store_1416.py` is skipped entirely when `os.name == "nt"` and when the test runs as root, because it works by `chmod` and root ignores permission bits. The reproduction that motivated the issue is a sandboxed agent host whose `.git/` is not writable; what the tests reproduce is that shape built locally with `chmod`, not that host.
- **A `#` or a `?` in the store's path made the read-only open read a different database, and create it ([#1416](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1416)).** `MemoryStore`'s read-only connect built its URI by string concatenation — `sqlite3.connect(f"file:{path}?mode=ro", uri=True)` — and a filesystem path is data, not a URI. `#` starts a fragment and `?` starts a second query string, so either character truncated the path **and** discarded `mode=ro`; SQLite then opened the shorter path read-**write** and created it as a zero-byte file. The #1416 schema floor found no tables in that empty file and told the user to grant write access and migrate a store that was already current — a wrong diagnosis, a stray file in a directory nobody asked it to write to, and the real store never read. Measured on SQLite 3.50.4: `<tmp>/store#1/memory.db` and `<tmp>/store?1/memory.db` both opened an empty database and left `<tmp>/store` behind, and `<tmp>/store%41/memory.db` failed outright with `unable to open database file`, because `%41` decodes to `A`. The URI is now built by `store.read_only_uri()`, which percent-encodes through `Path.as_uri()`.

  The URI is made absolute and nothing else. Neither alternative is safe, and both were measured. `Path.resolve()` follows symlinks, while `self._db_path` keeps the caller's spelling and is what places the `.bm25f` sidecar and fills the error strings, so resolving in the URI would let the engine's path and the store's reported path drift on a symlinked store. `os.path.abspath()` collapses `..` lexically, before the kernel sees the path, so on a store path where a `..` follows a symlinked directory — and `$AELFRICE_DB` is honoured verbatim, so you choose the spelling — the read-only handle addresses a different file from the writable one. Since the read-only handle is reached only when the directory is not writable, that divergence would be a silent wrong-store read inside a sandbox and nowhere else. `Path.absolute()` prefixes the working directory, percent-encodes identically, and leaves `..` to the OS, which is where the old string form left it.

  The regression matrix is narrow on purpose. A space, an apostrophe and a non-ASCII letter all open correctly with the plain concatenation, so they prove nothing about this defect and are not in the tests. Nor is a bare `%1`: an incomplete escape is malformed, SQLite leaves it alone, and such a path opens either way — which is why the cases are whole directory names rather than one metacharacter spliced into a template.

  **Not verified on Windows, and not verified on a managed Codex host.** `tests/test_readonly_store_1416.py` is skipped entirely when `os.name == "nt"` and when the test runs as root, because it works by `chmod` and root ignores permission bits. The reproduction that motivated the issue is a sandboxed agent host whose `.git/` is not writable; what the tests reproduce is that shape built locally with `chmod`, not that host.
- **`setup` and `unsetup` refuse the options the `codex` host never reads, instead of accepting them and exiting 0 ([#1429](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1429)).** `aelf setup --host codex` hardcodes `scope="user"` into `install_codex_hooks` and reads only `--force` and `--codex-skills` off its namespace; `aelf unsetup --host codex` discards its namespace whole with a bare `_ = args`. The shared parser has no host-applicability boundary, so `aelf setup --host codex --scope project --settings-path /tmp/x --no-statusline --rebuilder` parsed, dispatched, wrote `$CODEX_HOME/hooks.json` at user scope, and returned 0 — a success report for four instructions that never reached the executor. Measured from the parser, 18 of `setup`'s options and 14 of `unsetup`'s are in that position. An explicitly supplied one now exits 2 with the offending option named in the spelling you typed — `--no-statusline` is reported back as `--no-statusline`, not as the positive form argparse registered first, and an abbreviation such as `--proj` is reported as the `--project-root` argparse matched — and it does so in `main` ahead of the auto-installer, the update check, and every executor, so nothing on disk has moved when the refusal prints. The disposition is refuse-all, with no per-option exemption: `--no-statusline` is refused even though the `codex` path installs no statusline, because an accepted option that does nothing is a false green even when the outcome happens to coincide with what you asked for. Making any of these options take effect is separate work and stays gated on a Codex host. The set is derived from the live parser rather than a second hand-kept list, so an option added to either subcommand falls under the gate the moment it is registered, and the explicitly-supplied test is a re-parse with every action default replaced by `argparse.SUPPRESS` rather than a scan of `argv` — argparse does the matching, so `--scope=user` and an unambiguous prefix such as `--proj` count exactly as they counted in the real parse. Defaults are untouched: `aelf setup --host codex` and `aelf unsetup --host codex` with no options still run the full install and teardown, and the default host is not gated at all. One first-party caller had to move with the fix. The generated `$aelf-setup` Codex skill mandates the `--host codex` form on every invocation and carried prose — "Opt out with `--no-pre-issue-guard`", "skip with `--no-statusline`", "`--rebuilder` (opt-in)", "Pass `--scope user` or `--scope project`", "`--settings-path PATH`" — that would have instructed a Codex agent to build a command line this gate refuses. `src/aelfrice/slash_commands/setup.md` is the single source for both hosts, so each of those mentions is now marked as claude-host guidance and a `codex`-host paragraph states what the host accepts and that it refuses the rest with exit 2. `tests/test_codex_option_rejection_1429.py` enumerates both halves from the parser: 51 refusal cases, one per option string including the `--no-` form of each `BooleanOptionalAction`, each asserting exit 2 with `hooks.json` and the skills directory still absent; and a scan of every generated skill that fails if any paragraph names a refused option without qualifying it as claude-host. Both isolation hazards are handled rather than assumed — the teardown resolves its skills directory through `Path.home()`, not `$CODEX_HOME`, so `HOME` itself is redirected and the omission control asserts the sandbox directory is the one reported, and `$CODEX_HOME` is created first, because setup exits 1 against a missing one and every case would otherwise pass for the wrong reason.
- **Test timeouts scale to the machine instead of losing the PR gate ([#1472](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1472)).** The suite's wall-clock budgets were sized for an idle machine, so a loaded one failed tests that had no defect; `aelf-pr-open.sh` runs pytest with `-x`, so the first such loss ended the run, and twelve gate attempts went that way. Raising the ini `timeout` could not fix it: 179 tests carry their own `@pytest.mark.timeout`, and `pytest_timeout` resolves the closest marker first and only falls back to the ini value in its absence, so that value governs a minority of the suite. A `pytest_collection_modifyitems` hook in `tests/conftest.py` now re-adds each item's resolved budget multiplied by `AELF_TEST_TIMEOUT_SCALE`, which reaches both populations, including the handful of markers built from a module constant that a static census misses. The base moves from 5 to 30, the scale defaults to 4 for developers, and all five workflows that invoke pytest pin it to 1 — explicitly per workflow rather than inferred from `CI` being set, because `CI` is commonly exported in local shells and because `ci.yml` and `publish.yml` carry no `timeout-minutes` backstop, so there the scale is the only thing bounding a hang. A malformed or non-positive scale falls back to the default rather than raising; a scale of zero would read as *disabled* to pytest-timeout and silently remove every budget in the suite. The guard test derives the workflow list from the workflows themselves, so a new one that runs pytest without the pin fails rather than inheriting the developer scale. Twelve stale comments naming the old value are corrected; two that asserted an unmeasured inequality against it lost the figure rather than gaining a new one, and one that records a past incident keeps its original number, now labelled as the then-current default.
- **A default-run wall-clock budget that failed from machine load rather than from a regression ([#1473](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1473)).** Re-measured before any work, because the issue's evidence predates [#1472](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1472): an AST census finds 16 clock-asserting tests, 10 of them running by default, and under sustained load on 11 cores 3 of 5 runs failed — every failure on the same one test, while the other nine held. That is a much narrower population than the "seven gate runs lost to seven different tests" the issue describes, because #1472 removed the dominant mechanism. `test_dotdir_plan_scales_linearly` becomes `test_dotdir_plan_dedupes_without_rescanning` and counts path comparisons instead of timing them. Its old assertion was already a ratio rather than a budget, taking `min` of three samples on the reasoning that load can only make a sample slower — true of a single measurement and false of a ratio of two, because under contention the larger case's floor inflates more than the smaller one's. Restoring the #1202 list rescan makes 1,999,000 comparisons for 2,000 strays against 0 for the shipped set-based dedup, three orders of magnitude clear of the bound and identical on an idle or a loaded machine; re-run at load average 69, the converted test passed 5 of 5. `tests/test_wallclock_budget_census_1473.py` lands the census itself as a test, capping the population at its measured members so a new budget cannot be added silently, and failing in both directions — a new default-run budget, and a stale entry left behind after a conversion, which would otherwise hide the next real addition. `hook_latency_ms`'s `assert elapsed >= 0.0` is dropped as vacuous (a duration is non-negative by construction, so it sat in the census by shape while asserting nothing); the test is kept and renamed, because its other assertion — that a future stamp floors to exactly 0.0 — is the branch that would return a negative if the floor were removed, and is load-immune.
- **Seven user-facing documentation statements that disagreed with the code ([#1511](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1511)).** The full read of the twenty user-facing documents against the source ([#1509](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1509)) read all twenty user-facing documents against the source and surfaced these; that change was scoped "change no facts", so none were corrected there. `SECURITY.md` named one default outbound call where `README.md` and `PRIVACY.md` both name two — the update notifier and the pre-issue duplicate guard, default-on since v3.4.0 — and `SECURITY.md` is the document a reader consults for exactly that fact. `docs/user/INSTALL.md` told the reader to run `aelf doctor --prune` while its own code block two lines below used `--fix`; the parser defines `--fix`, `--prune-dormant` and `--prune-noise`, and no bare `--prune`, so the prose produced an argparse error. `INSTALL.md` and `LIMITATIONS.md` promised that the full suite runs on macOS and Linux on every pull request, but no workflow has ever used a macOS runner — the claim is replaced by what the workflows provide, a Linux full suite plus a Windows smoke job. `docs/concepts/ARCHITECTURE.md` said 31 modules against 117 `.py` files where the table has 32 rows and the tree holds 127. `docs/concepts/PHILOSOPHY.md` said exploration would ship "in a future version"; `src/aelfrice/exploration.py` ships today behind the default-off `[retrieval] exploration_enabled` flag, and the determinism argument in that paragraph is why the default is off, so the argument is kept and the stale half replaced. `docs/user/LIMITATIONS.md` described the removed Model Context Protocol surface in the present tense. Nine cross-file anchor links pointed at headings that no longer exist — not the fourteen reported, because GitHub does not collapse runs of whitespace when it builds a slug, and a collapsing slugger invents five phantoms. Every corrected figure is now derived rather than typed: `tests/test_docs_truth_1511.py` reads the workflow directory, the module table and the tree, and `tests/test_docs_cross_file_anchors_1511.py` resolves every cross-file anchor offline with a non-vacuity assert, covering the class the network `link-check` workflow cannot.
- **`BASH_FIRE_CAP_PER_TURN` now binds, because its counter moved to a per-session map in the session ring ([#1522](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1522)).** The declared ceiling of three retrieval-performing Bash searches per turn could not be reached in any deployed configuration. `aelf-search-tool-hook` is registered as a `"type": "command"` hook, so the host spawns one OS process per fire; the counter was a module-level dict, empty at the start of every one of them. Five processes in one session performed five retrievals where one process performed three, and the source comment naming it a "per-process per-turn" counter stated the assumption that fails: per-process and per-turn are not the same scope here. The count now lives in `session_ring.py` — already flock-guarded, atomically written and beside the store — under a `bash` map keyed by `session_id`, holding `turn_id` / `fires` / `fires_turn_id` / `seq` per session, and the `UserPromptSubmit` hook stamps that session's turn boundary, being the only hook guaranteed to fire exactly once per turn. The count names the turn it was taken in and a reader reports zero fires as soon as the two disagree, so the reset is a consequence of that comparison rather than a second write a crash between the two could skip — the cap resets each turn instead of becoming the permanent per-session cap of three that a naive move-it-to-a-file fix would have shipped.

  A *map* rather than three fields on the ring record, because the record is one per repo checkout, keyed by a single `session_id`, and a write from a different session replaces it wholesale. Every git worktree of a repo shares one `session_injected_ids.json`, and several concurrent sessions against it is a normal configuration, so counters held in that record are zeroed by any neighbour's fire: an A,A,A,B,A,A,A interleave of real hook processes emitted 7 of 7 blocks, six of them session A's, against a cap of 3. `scripts/bash_fire_cap_latency.py interleave` runs that sequence against both designs — 7 of 7 for a record-scoped counter, and 4 of 7 for the per-session map this ships, being A's three plus B's first. The map survives the record reset that a session switch performs, is bounded to `BASH_STATE_MAX_SESSIONS` (8) entries evicted least-recently-touched-first so one file serving a machine's worth of sessions cannot grow without limit, and a turn stamp bumps only the stamping session's `turn_id` — a prompt submitted next door does not re-open this session's budget.

  Both writes that maintain the map — the turn stamp and the fire count — touch the `bash` key alone and write every other key back as it was read, rather than going through the record-reshaping mutator the injection writes use. Through that one they would be worse than the defect they fix: the stamp runs on *every* prompt, so in the shared checkout above each prompt session A submitted would discard session B's dedup ring, `next_fire_idx` and #876 cadence state, and B would then re-inject beliefs it had already injected. `tests/test_search_tool_hook_bash_cap_1522.py::test_a_turn_stamp_leaves_a_co_tenants_ring_record_intact` drives the real `UserPromptSubmit` hook for A against a record owned by B, asserts every field of B's record unchanged, and asserts A's stamp did land — so a stamp that stopped writing at all cannot pass it.

  Writing the `bash` key alone raises the question of what happens when the rest of the record is not there to leave alone, and "is there a record here at all" turned out not to be the same question as "is the record complete". A file holding `{"bash": {}}` — what an aelfrice that had only ever stamped a turn leaves behind, and what a hand edit can leave behind — is a record, so an emptiness check passes it through and the Bash map is written back on top of a record still missing `ring_max` and `evicted_total`. The raw readers of this file do not normalize: `aelf doctor` renders that record as `injection ring: 0/None ids (evicted None this session)`. Both writes now fill in whichever of the record's fields are absent, each with the default a fresh record gets, and pass through every field the record already carries — a co-tenant's `session_id` and ring included, which is the property above. A field that is *present* but of the wrong type is left as it was found, because coercing it would mean rewriting a neighbour's record; every reader that consumes the record for its owner already coerces on read.

  Every ring failure (absent, malformed, unwritable, no record yet, no `session_id` in the payload) degrades to "no cap", never to "cap reached": a hook that suppressed retrieval because it could not read a file would be worse than the unbounded firing this bounds. Two overshoots follow from that direction and are documented in the spec rather than closed: a payload with no `session_id` is uncapped, and the read is lock-free, so Bash calls dispatched in parallel can each see the same count and exceed the cap by that parallel width.

  The cap check also moved behind `_extract_bash_query`, which imports nothing: consulting the ring pulls `session_ring` -> `db_paths` -> `store`, and ordered ahead of extraction that cost landed on *every* Bash tool call in the session rather than on the few that are searches. `scripts/bash_fire_cap_latency.py` ships in this branch, so every claim here names a command that re-derives it (#1469). `bash_fire_cap_latency.py order` copies `src/` twice and swaps those two statements in one copy, so both arms are real source trees differing only in that order, then runs one cold hook process per sample with the arms interleaved: a non-search Bash call is faster in the shipped order, and the paired delta was positive in every pair of every run. `python -X importtime -m aelfrice.hook_search_tool` shows the mechanism rather than the timing — `cat notes.txt` imports `aelfrice.session_ring` 0 times and `rg needleprobe src/` imports it once — and `tests/test_search_tool_hook_bash_cap_1522.py::test_a_non_search_bash_call_does_not_import_the_session_ring` pins that module set so the statement order cannot silently revert. `bash_fire_cap_latency.py cap` likewise puts a suppressed fire below a firing one in every pair of every run, against an empty store, where a populated store makes the firing arm heavier still.

  Both probes print millisecond medians and a per-turn saving; this entry deliberately publishes neither. They are wall-clock on a developer laptop that other work shares, and re-running both probes later on the same machine moved every absolute median and put the reorder delta outside the range earlier runs had spanned. The sign is what held: unanimous in every pair of every run of both probes. Run them on the machine you care about rather than reading a number from here.

  What the cap costs is that the fourth and later allowlisted searches in a turn get no injection at all, and the value 3 dates from #213 — it has not been re-derived against how many searches a turn issues today. That share is not measurable from the shipped telemetry: `search_tool_hook.jsonl` records a `session_id` per fire but `user_prompt_submit.jsonl` records no `session_id`, so fires cannot be attributed to turns without the hook audit log. Raising the value is a one-constant change once that attribution exists.

  The regression tests are subprocess-driven, one process per fire with a distinct search token each, because an in-process test structurally cannot observe either defect — it passes against the dead counter and the wired ring alike, which is how the cap shipped non-functional, and it cannot interleave two sessions against one ring file at all.
- **The seven injection lanes charge the line their own renderer emits, at unchanged budgets ([#1526](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1526)).** The seven are the ones `benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py` enumerates and packs, and they are the table below. The PreCompact rebuilder is not among them and is the one deliberate exception; it has its own paragraph. The injection budgets were denominated in belief-content characters while what reaches the model is a rendered line. A packed belief is never emitted as its content: on most lanes it is a `<belief …>` element on its own line, and that wrapper does not shrink when the content does — 52 characters for the per-turn line shape, with a 16-character belief id. So each block overran its cap by the wrapper's share of the line. The packers now charge the line, so each block comes in at or under the cap it claims.

  **Every budget constant is unchanged.** An earlier draft of this change re-tuned all seven so the emitted bytes would not move; two adversarial reviews refuted that, because byte-neutrality is not achievable with a fixed constant. What the correction removes from a pack is the ratio of a rendered line to its content — largest where beliefs are shortest, shrinking towards parity as they grow, and changing sign on the lanes that truncate — so a constant chosen to hold bytes flat holds them flat near one belief length and nowhere else.
