The old whole-block scan did match a forged line: on a lock whose content is `  ref <id>: "topic"` for the per-turn hit's id, `_REF_MANIFEST_RE.findall` over the rendered sub-block returned that id and would have dropped the live pointer. `test_belief_content_shaped_like_a_ref_line_cannot_drop_a_pointer` fires that fixture through the envelope and both pointers survive.

  **A store with no reference lock is byte-identical.** `_manifest_block_lines([])` emits nothing, so the wrapper and its note cost nothing until someone runs `aelf lock --reference`, and a frozen lock's `<belief>` element is unchanged to the byte — `test_a_frozen_lock_renders_the_bytes_it_rendered_before_1558` compares against a golden captured from the pre-change renderer rather than asserting a shape.

  **This reverses a decision [#1552](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/pull/1552) made.** That PR deleted `_write_memory_block`'s "move long-form locks to `aelf lock --reference`" sentence rather than qualifying it, because #1551 had measured the remedy a no-op on exactly the writes most likely to reach the note. The sentence is back, with the clause that makes it checkable: it now says the tier bounds a lock on *every write this hook makes*, and the table above is those four writes — 273 / 273 / 256 / 233 estimated tokens at reference tier against 7700 / 7796 / 7683 / 7660 frozen. It claims nothing about whether demoting will bring a given block under the ceiling; a store of ordinary short locks overruns at 66 of them and a reference entry is not free. The producer's vacuity guard is what keeps the claim honest, and it is now per write rather than over the set: until this change the two first-prompt columns were equal while turn two differed, so an "equal on every write" guard was satisfied by the defect it was meant to catch. Disabling the diversion makes that guard exit 1 with `the two lock tiers agree on gate_skip_first, retrieval_first`.

  `tests/test_reference_lock_manifest_1558.py` holds 26 tests, one per emit path — the gate-skip branch, the retrieval branch and the `session_start` baseline — because the defect is that one of the two lock-rendering call sites diverted and the other was never asked, plus the `<core>` exclusion, the single wrapper and the producer's guard. Ten mutations were run on this tree and all ten go red, by failing test count: disabling the `<locked>` diversion (7), dropping the manifest block from the sub-block (5), emptying the already-pointed-at set in the dedupe (2), removing the escaping from the manifest entry (1), widening the dedupe pattern to match `seen` as well (1), pinning `lock_attr` to `"none"` in the frozen render (3, one of them the golden), neutering the `<core>` locked-id filter (2), giving `_core_belief_line` an `is_reference_lock` branch (1), skipping the lift so the envelope wraps twice (3), and returning the producer guard to the over-the-set form (4). The tree was verified clean by SHA-256 after each revert and `__pycache__` cleared before each run.
- **A cadence-resume recap no longer displaces the prompt's own retrieval hits ([#1564](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1564)).** `_ceiling_drop_order` bucketed the block ceiling's droppable elements by "not inside `<core>` and not inside `<recent-work>`" and called the result the per-turn hits. That is a negative test, and it silently accepted a third kind of element: the [#871](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/871) `<cadence-resume>` recap, which `user_prompt_submit` prepends ahead of `<session-start>`. Because the recap is *prepended* it landed at the head of that bucket and `reversed(lane)` shed it last, so the prompt's own hits were deleted to keep a synthesis of an earlier session — the exact inversion of what the shed order exists to prevent. A second mechanism compounded it: `_format_hits_with_session_start` read `_ids_rendered_verbatim_in` off the sub-block *after* the recap had been concatenated onto the front of it, so every id the recap rendered collapsed the prompt's own hit to a `seen` pointer, and `enforce_block_ceiling` deletes a pointer with the element it names. Measured on a real P1 resume cache against a 40-lock / 20-core / 20-hit store at the shipped 6,000-token ceiling: **6 prompt-matched beliefs reached the model without a recap and 0 with one**. The operator ruled on 2026-09-17 that **the recap sheds first, and whole, and never suppresses a hit**, and this is that ruling: a fourth lane ahead of `<core>`, classified positively by the recap's own wrapper rather than by what it is not; `_recap_shed`, which removes the whole `<cadence-resume>` span in one cut so no wrapper is ever left holding a fragment of its beliefs; and `_session_start_dedupe_ids`, which cuts the recap's span out before the dedupe scan so only the sub-block's own `<locked>` and `<core>` elements can point at a hit. The same fixture now reaches **6 of 6 either way**, at a cost of **444 estimated tokens** on the untrimmed envelope — the part of [#1547](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1547)'s dedupe that fell on recap-carried ids, given back. **Taking the recap out of the dedupe changes what "dropped" means, and the ceiling now says so.** Since the recap no longer suppresses anything, every belief it carries is rendered a second time below it — in `<locked>`, in `<core>` or as the prompt's own hit — and cutting the recap's span does not take that copy with it. Reporting the span's ids wholesale therefore named beliefs the model was shown, and `BlockCeilingOutcome.dropped_ids` is read by four accounting surfaces as the opposite claim: `user_prompt_submit` filters `emitted_hits` by it, and that list is what reaches the `feedback_history` exposure rows, the audit row `aelf tail` prints, the [#740](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/740) dedup ring, and the [#1382](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1382) ledger. On the same fixture that put **5 rendered prompt hits and 40 rendered user locks into a list every one of those surfaces reads as "never shown"**, and took the audit row from 37 injected beliefs to 1 beside a payload carrying 65. `enforce_block_ceiling` now reports an id from a whole-recap shed only when nothing outside the recap renders it, so the count is beliefs the block lost every render of rather than elements one cut removed — which is what the four call sites were already written against. The stderr note is derived from that count and reads `1` where the span was 65; `scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py --resume-drop` is what reports the span. A `lock="user"` element inside a recap keeps the wrapper, because the [#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379) always-injected contract outranks the whole-shed rule; no shipped render produces one today, since `context_rebuilder` spells a lock `locked="true"`, and the branch is tested rather than assumed. **A fourth change was asked for, implemented, and refuted by the suite.** An adversarial read of the call site held that `_verbatim_ids` must take the renderer's augmented set — `already_rendered` unioned with the sub-block's own ids — rather than the caller's `read_rendered(session_id)`, on the grounds that [#1382](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1382) AC4 asks for one derivation of the manifest-vs-verbatim predicate and this is two. The two sets differ on exactly the hits the sub-block rendered verbatim above the pack, and those are the hits whose text *is* in the context window, because the sub-block put it there: recording them is the true claim and the union drops it. Under the change, `test_ups_seen_pointer_on_turn_two_names_a_belief_turn_one_rendered` went red with zero `seen` pointers on turn two — #1382 stops working across turns. The change is reverted, the call site now says why the caller's set is the right one, and `test_the_ledger_keeps_a_belief_the_sub_block_rendered` states the property positively so the next reader does not repeat it. `tests/test_hook_ceiling_cadence_resume_1560.py` pinned all four of the old claims and is renamed to `tests/test_hook_recap_shed_order_1564.py` and rewritten against the ruling, with eight tests and one mutation per claim: dropping the recap lane fails the ordering claim, skipping the whole-shed branch brings back a wrapper holding a fragment, restoring `_ids_rendered_verbatim_in` at the render site fails both hit claims, handing `_verbatim_ids` the augmented set fails the ledger claim, removing the `lock="user"` fallback fails the #379 claim, and dropping the test that keeps a still-rendered id out of `dropped_ids` fails the accounting claim on both of its arms — the invariant on a hand-built body, and the fired hook's audit row, which goes back to naming 1 injected belief beside 5 prompt hits it rendered. Out of scope by ruling: bounds across blocks ([#1560](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1560)), whether cadence should default on, and the `_LOCKED_ATTR` / `locked="true"` spelling mismatch that keeps the #379 contract from holding on the recap path. That last one is deferred, not closed, and it is not free: a user lock the recap carries now sheds first rather than last, where before it survived on this fixture. It is bounded by `<locked>`, which renders the same locks uncapped in the same envelope, so the text still reaches the model and the belief stays out of `dropped_ids` — with one exception, recorded at the `n_locked` call site in `hook.py`: a [#1558](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1558) reference lock, whose `<locked>` render is a `ref` manifest line rather than an element, so the recap's bounded-topic element is the only one there is to shed. The manifest line survives and still names it.

### Performance

- **The BM25 sidecar is warmed by a detached child at `SessionStart`, so the session-first full rebuild is no longer paid on the user's first prompt ([#1513](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1513)).** `benchmarks/sidecar_rebuild_rate.py` now buckets scored fires by position within the session, and the two populations do not resemble each other: the `full_rebuild` rate on the first scored fire of a session runs materially above the rate on every later fire, on the prompt where a user is least willing to wait. Run the script to see the current split. **No magnitude for it is published here.** The population is a live, growing, single-slot-rotating audit log; successive re-derivations weeks apart moved the session-first rate substantially while the sign never flipped, and the rows the earlier runs scored are gone to rotation, so any percentage frozen into this file would be one nobody could reproduce.

  **The pre-registered target, corrected.** The first statement of it (committed in `a34bbb58`, and left in the history rather than rewritten) could not be read against the command it named. All three of its parts were wrong, for one underlying reason:

  - *It never named its population.* Its baseline came from every `hook_audit.jsonl*` on the machine, but the command a reader would run — `benchmarks/sidecar_rebuild_rate.py` with no arguments — globs one repository's log. Those two have no denominator in common, so the settling run could never be compared against the baseline it was meant to be read against.
  - *Its read gate was already met when it was written.* "Read only once at least 26 sessions carry a scored fire" was satisfied at more than 26 sessions on the baseline population itself, so it gated nothing.
  - *Its LATER clause was already false.* "The LATER bucket does not rise above its 1.0% baseline" was false on the day it was written: re-deriving over the same machine-wide population put LATER above 1.0% with nothing shipped. Held as stated, the gate would have read ordinary population drift as a regression the fix caused.

  The one underlying reason is that it compared a number taken at one time against a number taken at another, over a log set that grew and rotated in between. That difference is not the fix. The restated target compares two windows cut out of a single log set, in one pair of invocations that differ only in the window, which is what `--since` / `--until` were added for:

  ```
  find ~ -name 'hook_audit.jsonl*' -not -path '*/node_modules/*' | sort > /tmp/logs.txt
  xargs uv run python benchmarks/sidecar_rebuild_rate.py --until "$WARM_SHIPPED_AT" < /tmp/logs.txt
  xargs uv run python benchmarks/sidecar_rebuild_rate.py --since "$WARM_SHIPPED_AT" < /tmp/logs.txt
  ```

  `$WARM_SHIPPED_AT` is the ISO-8601 UTC instant the operator's installed hooks began running a build that carries the warm — not the merge date, because until then no fire in the log was spared by anything. The bound is half-open, so the two runs partition the log set with no fire in both and none lost.

  - **Read condition.** Read the pair only once the `--since` run's own `sessions beginning in this window` line reports 30 or more. That count is over the same population as the rate it gates, which the "26 sessions" condition was not.
  - **Primary.** The session-FIRST `full_rebuild` rate in the `--since` run is at most half the rate the `--until` run reports for the same log set. Relative, because the baseline is re-derived inside the pair rather than frozen into this file.
  - **Check that the pair partitions the sessions, not only the rows.** The `--until` and `--since` session counts must sum to the whole-set run's `sessions with a scored fire`. They did not before this branch's last pass: a session that began before the boundary and ran past it had its first in-window fire promoted to session-FIRST in the `--since` run and was counted on both sides. That promoted fire is a mid-session prompt no `SessionStart` warm inside the window could have spared, and LATER fires carry the lower rebuild rate, so each one pulled the `--since` session-FIRST rate down — toward satisfying the primary above. A session's position is now fixed by its first scored fire anywhere in the input, so a straddling session contributes only LATER fires to the `--since` run. The `sessions carried in from earlier` line counts those sessions: a `--since` run whose fires are mostly theirs holds few first prompts, and its primary rests on that much smaller sample.
  - **Deferral guard.** Compare the LATER rate between the two runs of the pair, never against a frozen figure. A fall in session-FIRST matched by a rise in LATER means the rebuild moved rather than went away, and fails the target however far session-FIRST fell.
  - **Sanity check that the warm ran at all.** The child writes one `sidecar_warm` row per spawn, unless the hook audit is off (`AELFRICE_HOOK_AUDIT=0`, or `enabled = false` under `[hook_audit]`), so `grep '"hook": "sidecar_warm"'` over the same logs says whether the `--since` window contains any warm at all. A window with none is an unshipped warm, a warm suppressed with `AELF_NO_SIDECAR_WARM=1`, or an audit the user turned off — not a failed target.
  - The fix cannot reach 0%: a user who types immediately, or a sibling session that ingests between the warm and the first prompt, still meets a stale index.

  The warm is a **detached child process** spawned from the SessionStart hook, chosen against the failure mode rather than the happy path. *A thread the hook does not join* was rejected: the hook is a short-lived process, so a non-daemon thread keeps the interpreter alive until the build finishes and the host waits on the hook — that moves the latency into `SessionStart` instead of removing it, which the issue's AC2 names explicitly; a daemon thread inverts the failure and is killed mid-build, writing nothing. *Warming on a later fire* was rejected on the measurement: the later bucket already runs far below the session-first one, so a warm that runs on the second fire arrives after the fire that pays. The parent does one environment read and one `Popen` and nothing else — no store open, no retrieval flag, no numpy — because every decision that costs anything (is the L1 lane on, is the sidecar fresh, what tokenisation parameters apply) is made inside the child. That is structural rather than measured, and no spawn-cost figure is published: a wall-clock millisecond number taken on a loaded developer machine does not reproduce.

  Both halves are fail-soft and neither can change what the hook emits: a spawn that raises returns False, a warm that raises returns None, and `SessionStart`'s stdout, stderr and return code are pinned byte-identical between a suppressed warm and one that explodes. The child builds through the *same* helper and resolvers the L1 lane uses, which is load-bearing rather than tidy — a sidecar written under different tokenisation parameters is rejected as describing different documents, so a warm that resolved its own would pay the whole build and leave the next fire rebuilding anyway. Sharing the resolvers is not by itself enough: three of the four parameters come from env and TOML and so agree across processes by construction, but `anchor_weight` decodes a *decaying* posterior when the #757 meta-belief is enabled, and the warm's clock at T and the fire's at T+30s were observed decoding adjacent integers out of the `[1, 10]` band under the shipped 30-day half-life — both fires then recorded `full_rebuild`. While a sidecar is fresh, the weight it was built under is read back off its header and is the answer both sides take; the next write to the store lifts the pin, which is where a moved meta-belief takes effect. `AELF_NO_SIDECAR_WARM=1` suppresses the spawn.
- **A gate-skipped hook fire stops importing the retrieval subtree it exists to skip ([#1527](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1527)).** The prompt-shape gate refuses trivial and system-generated prompts so they never retrieve. They never did retrieve — and imported the whole retrieval stack anyway, because the skip branch calls `_emit_user_prompt_submit_rebuild_log` unconditionally and that function reached into `aelfrice.context_rebuilder` *above* its own `session_id` and `:memory:` early returns, pulling `retrieval`, `triple_extractor`, `scoring`, `clustering`, `bfs_multihop`, `correction`, `derivation*`, `doc_linker*`, `exploration`, `compression` and `np_pattern` behind it. The import could not be gated on config either: deciding whether the log is enabled *is* `load_rebuilder_config`, which lived in the module being avoided. That circularity is why the fix is a module extraction rather than a lazy import — the same remedy #1407 applied to `sidecar_outcome`, for the same reason, that every hook fire is a fresh process. `aelfrice.rebuild_log` now holds the `[rebuilder]` / `[rebuild_log]` config resolution and the phase-1a diagnostic log, and `entity_extractor` / `triple_extractor` are function-scope imports below every early return, so a skipping fire (which passes an empty candidate list) never reaches them. `context_rebuilder` re-exports every name in `rebuild_log.__all__` except `_extracted_entities_for_log` and `_recent_turns_hash`, which nothing outside `rebuild_log.py` calls, so the CLI, the benchmarks and the existing `from aelfrice.context_rebuilder import ...` callers import what they ask for unchanged. `tests/test_rebuild_log_reexports_1527.py` holds both halves: the exception list as a set equality, and every such import under `src`, `tests`, `benchmarks` and `scripts` resolving. It is not a claim about the namespace, and an earlier draft of this entry wrongly made one. Twelve names — `Any`, `LEGACY_STRATEGY`, `VALID_STRATEGIES`, `dataclass`, `datetime`, `discover_config`, `extract_entities`, `extract_triples`, `hashlib`, `os`, `timezone`, `tomllib` — were reachable as `context_rebuilder.<name>` before this change and are not now; every one was an import that had leaked into the namespace, and no caller in the tree reaches for any of them through `context_rebuilder`. One consumer did have to move with the code: `tests/test_triple_collision_verbs_1376.py` AST-walks the module holding the `extract_triples` call, so it is repointed at `rebuild_log` here — a re-export cannot satisfy a test that looks for a `Call` node. The other half is `hook.py`'s own module scope: `retrieve`, `search_for_prompt` and four `context_rebuilder` entry points now resolve through a `_lazy()` helper that reads `globals()` before it imports — which is where `monkeypatch.setattr(aelfrice.hook, ...)` writes, so the test modules that patch those names keep working — with a PEP 562 module `__getattr__` so `from aelfrice.hook import retrieve` and `setattr(..., raising=True)` still resolve on an unresolved module. Neither half moves the number alone; the leaf extraction is worthless while `hook.py` imports `context_rebuilder` eagerly, and the deferral is worthless while the rebuild-log call re-pays it. **The measurement is the module closure, and `scripts/measure_1527_import_closure.py` is what produces it**: it exports each git ref with `git archive` so neither arm is the installed package, then probes it in cold subprocesses with every `AELFRICE_*` / `AELF_*` variable stripped and `HOME`, the dotdir and the DB pinned to a scratch directory, because the lane resolvers are env-first and opening a `MemoryStore` is a write. Against `github/main`: **`import aelfrice.hook` falls from 35 `aelfrice` modules to 18, and a driven gate-skipped fire from 40 to 26**; `aelfrice.rebuild_log` costs 12 where `aelfrice.context_rebuilder` cost 28. `tests/test_hook_import_cost_1351.py` pins 18 and 26 as ceilings and asserts that none of the twelve retrieval-subtree modules loads on a skipped fire. Two further tests stop that absence assertion from degrading quietly, and they catch different things. A second test drives a *retrieving* fire and asserts a set **equality** between the entries of the same tuple that fire loads and a separately pinned nine-name set, which fails if a deferred import was deleted rather than moved, if the retrieval lane stopped running, or if either list was edited on its own. A third asserts every entry of the tuple resolves through `importlib.util.find_spec`, which is the only check covering the three entries (`correction`, `derivation`, `triple_extractor`) that neither of that module's two fixture fires reaches — a cadence-driven `Stop` fire does load all three, so this is a statement about the fixtures, not about the modules. Neither is a claim that the ban list is complete — nothing in the repo re-derives its membership — and the `26` ceiling remains what catches a module leaking back onto the skip path under a name the tuple never held. **No millisecond figure is published.** An earlier draft of this entry carried one; independent paired re-derivations on the same tree did not reproduce its interval, and on a loaded developer machine none of them is worth publishing. The module closure is deterministic, so that is the claim. `query_understanding` stays eager: `_rebuild_and_format` binds `DEFAULT_STRATEGY` as a def-time default and `RebuilderConfig.query_strategy` binds it in a frozen dataclass field, and deferring it from `hook.py` alone saves **0** modules, because `rebuild_log` binds it too; deferring it from both files saves **5**, none of them on the retrieval path. `scripts/measure_1527_import_closure.py --marginal` re-derives both.

### Documentation

- **`benchmarks/README.md` states the rule that agreement is not quality evidence ([#1453](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1453)).** Top-10 movement rate, Jaccard overlap at k, Kendall tau and "N of M queries changed" are **change** statistics: they say two configurations return different results, not which one is right. A movement rate becomes a quality statistic only against labelled relevance, external to both rankers. Without labels a large movement and a small movement are equally consistent with an improvement, a regression and noise. The note names the two failure modes that have actually occurred — reporting a movement rate as if it were an uplift, and treating agreement between two approximations as validation — and cites [`848dbf83`](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/commit/848dbf83aab6e2c5198b1bc5b0f70ebcc7f11ea9), the revert that retracted a published table for the second reason, after `extracted_query` turned out to be handed to `retrieve()` by neither caller (97.7% of recorded rows come from `user_prompt_submit`, which scores a conversation-aware composition the context rebuilder never sees). It lands in `benchmarks/README.md` rather than in a design document because that is the file a person running one of these harnesses opens. `tests/test_docs_agreement_rule_1453.py` pins that the rule is still stated and that the cited SHA still resolves to a commit — a citation to an unreachable object reads as sourced while pointing at nothing. The note's wording is deliberately not pinned.
- **The SessionStart baseline block's token budget bounded nothing on the lane it governed, and that is how it was found ([#1526](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1526)).** `session_start` retrieves through `_retrieve_baseline_with_block`, which calls `retrieve()` with an **empty query**. In `retrieve_with_tiers` every relevance lane — L2.5, L1, HRR expansion, the temporal spine and the BFS hop — is gated on `query.strip()`, so only L0 contributes, and L0 is appended unconditionally and never trimmed by the budget ([#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379)). The pack loop this budget drives is never entered. Measured on a 300-lock store of 150-character beliefs: 300 hits and 61,144 rendered bytes at `token_budget=1`, at 1500 and at 100000 alike. The block is still bounded — by the lock count, which is the #379 contract — but the constant was inert, so re-tuning it would have been meaningless. It is deleted in this same release under [#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546); read that entry for what the deletion exposes. `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py` pins the lane's behaviour against the real call rather than a monkeypatch of it.
- **The documented developer setup now installs the extras it needs, so a local green is the green that gates the merge ([#1548](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1548)).** `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Development setup told a contributor to run `uv sync --all-groups`. That installs dependency **groups**, and `archive` is an **extra** (`cryptography>=50.0`, `[project.optional-dependencies]`), so the documented command left `cryptography` uninstalled and two things went quietly wrong. Eight tests skipped — `test_uninstall_archive_roundtrip`, `test_uninstall_archive_wrong_password_rejected`, `test_archive_gate_says_the_extras_are_deleted_not_encrypted`, `test_archive_of_a_live_store_recovers_every_belief`, `test_archive_removes_the_derived_plaintext_artifacts`, `test_archive_destination_inside_the_store_dir_is_not_deleted`, `test_cli_removes_consent_in_every_disposition_mode[archive]` and `test_cli_archive_gate_discloses_the_dotdir_paths` — and they cover the one path where a bug destroys data a user asked to keep, so a contributor touching `lifecycle.py` under the documented setup got a green suite that never exercised it. Measured on the four modules: **130 passed with the extra, 122 passed and 8 skipped without it**. And `pyright` could not resolve `cryptography.fernet` or `cryptography.hazmat` in `src/aelfrice/lifecycle.py`, so a cascade of unknown types pushed that file from **31 errors to 54** and `scripts/check_pyright_baseline.py` failed on a correctly set-up machine for a reason that was not in the diff. That file is the **whole** per-file difference between the two environments — a full `pyright src/` run gives 973 errors over 75 files with the extras and 996 without, and no other file moves by one — so the phantom 23 was the entire gap. CI never saw it, because `pyright-ratchet.yml` syncs `--all-extras` and `ci.yml`'s `pytest` job syncs `--group dev --extra archive`. Both `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `RELEASING.md` step 6 now sync `--all-groups --all-extras`, which covers the strongest of the workflows, and both say why. A skip caused by a missing optional dependency also has to name the extra rather than the import: the eight gates share one `requires_archive_extra()` helper in `tests/conftest.py` whose reason reads "requires the 'archive' extra: uv sync --all-groups --all-extras", and `pytest_terminal_summary` reports the count in an `optional extras` section, so the skips are visible in the tail and not only under `-rs` — the same shape the bench-gate tier already used, and for the same reason, that a skip deletes coverage as quietly as a failure blocks work. The drift is what `tests/test_documented_setup_1548.py` prevents from recurring: it parses the `uv sync` line out of the setup block and out of `ci.yml`'s `pytest` job and `pyright-ratchet.yml`'s `ratchet` job, and fails when the documentation installs less than either, so an extra added to a workflow and not to the documentation is a red test rather than a silent gap. It parses by line rather than with PyYAML, because `yaml` is not in the dependency set CI syncs and an `import yaml` there passes locally and fails on the runner; the parser is small enough to be wrong quietly, so its shape is pinned directly against the three `uv sync` spellings the workflows use. Whether `archive` should be a group rather than an extra is out of scope and unchanged — it is optional because `cryptography` is heavy and the feature is opt-in.
- **The UserPromptSubmit payload is bounded per block, and that is now written where the bound is ([#1560](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1560)).** [#1551](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1551) bounded the `<aelfrice-memory>` envelope and [#1552](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/pull/1552) scoped the claim to it; what was left open was whether one bound governs the whole payload. It does not. `HOOK_BLOCK_TOKEN_CEILING`'s docstring now states the contract positively: every block a fire writes names its own bound, no bound spans them, and there is deliberately no payload ceiling. The two bounded blocks and the two mechanisms that enforce them are named — the `<aelfrice-memory>` envelope by this constant through `enforce_block_ceiling`, hard with the #379 lock exemption, since that function never drops a `lock="user"` element and a block whose locks alone are oversized is emitted over the ceiling with a note on stderr; the `<cadence-checkpoint>` block by `DEFAULT_REBUILDER_TOKEN_BUDGET` through the rebuilder's pack loop, **soft**. Calling the two equivalent would be false: [#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546) already records that `<retrieved-beliefs budget_used="N/M">` can report `N > M` on the rebuild lane, and the measured fire below is an instance of it rather than a citation of it: that block's own `budget_used` attribute reports 22106 characters packed against a budget of 16000, and the block comes to 5813 estimated tokens against a budget of 4000.
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_rebuild_budget_used_chars = 22106 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_rebuild_budget_chars = 16000 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_checkpoint_tokens = 5813 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_rebuilder_budget = 4000 -->

  **Why per block, and not one ceiling.** The shed order removes prompt-independent lanes before the prompt's own retrieval hits, so extending it across the payload would make the hook delete retrieved beliefs to make room for a rebuild recap. That cross-block trade is unmeasured and no cross-block shed is added here; the rebuilder's own budget stays with #1546.

  **What the rationale must not say, because it is false: that the trade has never happened.** An earlier revision of this entry and of the docstring said "nobody has measured whether that is the right trade" without qualifying it, and inside the `<aelfrice-memory>` envelope the trade already runs — for the `<cadence-resume>` recap (#871), not for the cadence checkpoint. The recap is prepended to the session-start sub-block and emitted inside the envelope, so it is charged to this ceiling, **and the dropper sheds it**: `_maybe_read_cadence_resume` wraps a body the context rebuilder rendered, whose `<belief>` elements are ordinary droppable elements, and only the `<cadence-resume>` wrapper is exempt — by not being a `<belief>` element at all. A lock inside the recap is not exempt either, because the rebuilder writes `locked="true"` where the dropper reads `lock="user"`. And because the recap sits outside `<core>` and `<recent-work>`, `_ceiling_drop_order` buckets its elements with the per-turn hits, at the *head* of a bucket that is shed tail-first — so the prompt's own hits go before the recap is touched. `scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py --resume-drop` measured it on a real P1 resume cache against a 40-lock / 20-core / 20-hit store: the recap kept 65 `<belief>` elements untrimmed and 34 after the ceiling, while prompt-matched beliefs reaching the model fell from 6 without a recap to 0 with one, counting an element and a `seen` pointer alike so #1547's dedupe is not miscounted as a loss. That is the behaviour as this branch found it; [#1564](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1564) is where the operator ruled on it and changed it, and the two per-arm numbers above are its before-values rather than what the producer emits today. The per-block ruling is unchanged; what changed here is that its stated reason no longer rests on a false claim, and the code's actual position is named — the within-envelope trade happens, the cross-block one does not.
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#resume_recap_elements_untrimmed = 65 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#resume_hits_without_recap = 6 -->

  **`_ceiling_drop_order` claimed the opposite, and this branch is what disproves it.** Its docstring said "elements outside both named sections are the per-turn hits by construction" — a #1552 claim, true of `<locked>` and false of the recap. It now says so, and says that the consequence was an ordering the function's own first paragraph argues against: the prompt-independent recap outranked the prompt's own hits. Re-bucketing it changes what the hook injects, and #1560 is a documentation ruling, so this branch recorded the behaviour and [#1564](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1564) altered it.

  **The sum is a measured figure, from a committed producer.** `scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py --cadence` fires one store of user locks, `<core>` beliefs and prompt-matching hits — sized by the `CADENCE_LOCKS` / `CADENCE_CORE` / `CADENCE_HITS` constants it seeds from — with every writer live, and reports each writer's share. The writers are enumerated from `user_prompt_submit` itself rather than from the two that carry a budget: the cadence write; the memory envelope through `_write_memory_block`, whose two call sites are the mutually exclusive retrieval and `elif gate_skip:` branches, so at most one of them fires; and the two phantom notes, `<aelfrice-phantom-opportunity>` (#980) and `<aelfrice-phantom-promotion-opportunity>` (#1132), which carry no token budget of any kind — each is bounded only by a per-session fire budget and a per-entry topic length. That fire emits 11926 estimated tokens on stdout: 5813 of cadence checkpoint, 5898 of memory envelope inside its ceiling of 6000, 99 and 116 of the two notes. The payload is roughly twice the ceiling while the block the ceiling governs is inside it, which is the arithmetic the contract makes explicit. `--emit-figures` publishes each of those sizes, and the two bounds they are read against, so CI re-derives them, and the arm refuses to publish a total from a fire that was missing a writer, whose memory envelope overran its own ceiling, or whose payload did not exceed it. **Those figures exclude the `<cadence-resume>` recap**, and the arm now refuses to print them if that stops being true: the recap is read from a cache a prior session's Stop-side cadence fire wrote, and this arm's work directory is fresh, so nothing is prepended. The recap's own charge is `--resume-drop`'s subject, not this sum's.
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_payload_tokens = 11926 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_memory_tokens = 5898 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_phantom_opportunity_tokens = 99 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_phantom_promotion_tokens = 116 -->
  <!-- derived: scripts/measure_block_ceiling.py#cadence_fire_block_ceiling = 6000 -->

  **The contract is tested at the emit boundary, on the payload the two candidate contracts disagree about.** `tests/test_hook_payload_per_block_bound_1560.py` drives the real hook entrypoint and asserts on captured stdout, not on either block in isolation: a payload whose blocks are each inside their own bound and whose sum is outside `HOOK_BLOCK_TOKEN_CEILING` is emitted whole. A test that checked each block separately would pass under a single payload ceiling too and would pin nothing. A second test fires the same store with cadence on and off, on a fixture whose memory block is over its ceiling so the trim is live, and requires the emitted envelope to be byte-identical across the pair — which beliefs the ceiling deletes cannot depend on whether a sibling block shares the payload. `tests/test_hook_recap_shed_order_1564.py` pins the other half: that the dropper sheds a recap's `<belief>` elements, and where in the shed order it does so. Those claims were wrong in prose for exactly as long as nothing asserted them; the module was named for #1560 when it recorded the defect and is named for [#1564](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1564) now that the ruling has changed the behaviour it pins.

  **The exposure is cadence-enabled-only.** `[cadence] enabled` is unset by default, so `_maybe_run_ups_cadence_checkpoint` returns `None`, no Stop-side fire writes a resume cache, and a stock install gets neither the second block nor a recap; the measured fire enables that lane and both phantom lanes explicitly, and a test records the default-off absence — by *omitting* the `[cadence]` section rather than writing `enabled = false`, since an explicit off pins a setting instead of the shipped default. This is not something every user is exposed to. It is, however, the configuration behind the largest envelope rows [#1547](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1547) measured in the field, which is why the sum is worth having as a figure.

  **AC2 is `NOT_APPLICABLE`, recorded rather than left unchecked.** The issue's second criterion — "if one bound: the shed order is stated and tested across blocks" — is conditional on the option that was not chosen. Under the per-block ruling there is no cross-block shed order to state or test, so it is marked not applicable, on the #1160 convention that an n/a criterion says so rather than going quietly unmet. No payload-tokens field was added to the hook audit record and no stderr threshold note was added; both were considered and not selected.

### CI

- **A published figure now names the script that re-derives it, and CI checks it ([#1469](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1469)).** Six of eight PRs reviewed in the 2026-08-10 board sweep shipped at least one stale or false published figure; every one was caught by a reviewer re-deriving it by hand, and none by CI. A figure now carries a machine-readable marker naming its producer, the key that producer emits it under, and the published value — `<!-- derived: benchmarks/published_constants.py#stop_prompt_max_items = 20 -->` — in the same syntax in a CHANGELOG entry and in a Python comment, because six of the seven instances shipped in source as well as in the CHANGELOG and #1445's number shipped in three files. **A marker's value must also be a figure in the text it annotates**, and that is the link the rest of the gate does not make. Producer checks bind the producer to the marker; self-consistency binds markers to each other; neither reads the number a reader sees, so both stay green while the prose says something else — the failure mode this entry is about, reproduced through this entry's own gate. The session-end item cap is 20, so a marker over it is now held to a 20 being there: editing a published byte count and leaving its marker alone is a hard failure, and so is the repair path of bumping every marker to a new value while leaving the sentences around them unchanged. In a Python file the scope is the comment run the marker sits in, not the paragraph, so the assignment two lines below cannot stand in for the sentence above. The direction is marker → prose only; an unmarked figure stays grandfathered. **Scoping is per file type.** A `- ` line anywhere in a file used to put the whole file into changelog-bullet mode, so a list in a Python docstring switched the overclaim rule off for that whole file, and every line above the first bullet was discarded. **How many files that was is deliberately not published**: the only honest denominator is the scanned corpus, which moves on every merge, so the share would be stale the day after it was written — which is the defect this entry exists to stop. Bullets are now markdown-only, and every non-blank line lands in exactly one entry, asserted over the whole corpus rather than claimed. **The gate is split, and the marker says which half a figure is in.** A marker with no `corpus=` is store-free: `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode producers` runs the producer and hard-fails on any difference between what it emits and what the prose published. A marker carrying `corpus=<label>@<date>` is store-backed — `stop_prompt_block_bounds.py`, `scan_admission_funnel.py` and `sidecar_rebuild_rate.py` read a real belief store, a public runner has none, and the lab corpus does not go there (#1456) — so the marker records which store on which day, and CI checks the two things it still can: that every file publishing one figure agrees on its value (**hard**; this is the check #1449 needed for its 44,683-against-44,687 in one PR, and no store is needed to run it — though neither of those two figures is annotated yet, so that instance is still only in `--list-unmarked`), and whether the producer's source has moved since the figure was stamped (**advisory**; a producer edit does not prove the figure moved, and #1445 is the clean example of one that did). **Running the store-backed producers in public CI is not attempted rather than attempted and skipped.** A job that fails for want of a corpus gets switched off within a week, and a check that reports a pass it did not run is the #1160 defect this repo has already paid for. **An unmarked figure stays legal**, because the annotation backlog is the whole corpus and a gate that failed on it would last a day — but `--list-unmarked` enumerates it, so grandfathered is not the same as invisible. **That last clause was false as first written, and a code span whose whole content is a number is now a figure.** Inline code was masked before extraction, so the hard overclaim rule below was satisfiable by typing two backticks, and the two headline shares of `CHANGELOG/v4.md`'s #1356 entry — published in code font, which is this repo's style for a measured value — appeared in nothing `--list-unmarked` printed. A span carrying anything else stays masked, because a false positive there makes the overclaim rule unsatisfiable, which is the same as deleting it. The narrowing is priced rather than asserted: `--mask-delta` reports how many figures each candidate rule makes visible on the tree it is run against, and no share is published here because that denominator moves on every merge. **The residue is documented, not closed**: a figure inside a span that also carries words is still invisible. **The one claim that is not grandfathered is the overclaim sentence.** The shape `` `<script>` re-derives every figure here `` shipped in #1445 and #1447 over scripts emitting about a third of the entry's numbers; it is now permitted only in an entry where every figure carries a marker, and that check is hard. The one live instance in `CHANGELOG/v4.md` did not survive it: `benchmarks/spine_fan_in_baseline.py` emits six of that entry's figures and not the rest, and the sentence now says so. Quoting the sentence inside backticks is how it stays discussable: inline code is a citation, not a claim, and this entry would otherwise fail its own gate -- which is how the exemption was found. **The stamp is excluded from the bytes it stamps** — found by running `--restamp` twice and getting two different hashes, because a producer that documents its own figures has no fixed point otherwise. The seven #1469 figures were re-verified rather than taken from the issue. `RetrievalCache`'s ship version was wrong wherever `docs/design/bfs_multihop.md` and `docs/design/bayesian_ranking.md` dated the *wrapper*, and every one of those sentences now reads v1.0.3: v1.0.1 is what the spec in `docs/design/historical/lru_query_cache.md` targeted, but `class RetrievalCache` is absent from `src/` at v1.0.1 and at v1.0.2 and present at v1.0.3, added by `0c27a937`. No count of corrected sites is published, because the first attempt at one was wrong in the direction this entry exists to stop. **Not every `v1.0.1` near the cache is wrong, and the first pass over-corrected the ones that were right**: the store-level wipe-on-write policy really did ship at v1.0.1 — `add_invalidation_callback` is present at that tag and `insert_edge`, `update_edge` and `delete_edge` all call `_fire_invalidation()` there, and the policy outlived the class #1418 deleted — so the cache-invalidation bullet under `bfs_multihop.md`'s § In scope, the "Cache invalidation rule?" row of its § Open questions resolved by this spec, and the literal in `src/aelfrice/retrieval.py` are about the policy rather than the wrapper and read v1.0.1. **The gate cannot catch this class and is not claimed to**: `_MASKS` strips dotted version strings before figure extraction, so a version literal is checked by reading it. **The gate does not check an enumerated count against its own list, and #1451 is not claimed as closed by it.** #1451's shape is a published 13 over a list of twelve. The marker binds that 13 to a producer and to a figure reading 13 in the annotated text; deleting two items from the prose list moves neither, so `--mode all` stays green over exactly the defect. No enumeration-length rule was added: counting items means deciding which commas separate them, and the live sentence carries a parenthesised triple a comma counter reads as three more mutations — a rule that mis-counts legitimate prose is a rule that gets switched off, and one that works on a single sentence shape reports green over every other, which is the #1160 failure already paid for here. What the instance gained is a producer for the count. The two belief-count figures need a store to re-derive and are left as measured, now carrying the corpus and date that distinguish them. Where each of the seven stands, and why the four corrected on `main` are marked or exempt, is in `docs/design/derived_figure_markers.md`. **The guard for that bytecode rule could not itself be run under the rule's own advice.** `test_a_producer_runs_the_source_on_disk_and_not_a_stale_pycache` poisons a `__pycache__` entry by compiling a helper in a child, and it stripped `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX` from that child's environment but not `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` — so with that variable exported nothing is written and the test reds on "nothing was cached to go stale", for a reason that has nothing to do with the gate. That is the exact situation this repo puts an author in when it tells them to run a same-size mutation that way. Both names are now stripped, and the guard still reds on `now emits 21` when `env=producer_env(...)` is dropped from `check_producers`.
  <!-- derived: benchmarks/published_constants.py#stop_prompt_max_items = 20 -->
- **`pyright` is enforced, and the four documents that claimed it already passed are corrected ([#1503](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1503)).** `RELEASING.md` step 6 ran `uv run pyright src/` with the comment `# strict`, implying it passes; the baseline that landed with #1503 — `git show ce9e5461:pyright_baseline.json` — records **987 errors over 76 files**, and no workflow anywhere ran pyright, so the tick was self-reported and unenforced. `CONTRIBUTING.md` asserted "`pyright --strict` must pass", the pull-request template carried "strict, no new errors", and the cost is on the record — `CHANGELOG/v4.md` notes a `NameError` that reached `main` behind this check. The operator ruling of 2026-08-19 was to drive the count to zero with a required CI job, and the ruling of 2026-08-26 **cancelled that burn-down**: the ratchet is the whole answer, so the count falls only as a side effect of other work and nothing drives it on a schedule. `scripts/check_pyright_baseline.py` holds a **per-file** baseline in `pyright_baseline.json` and fails when any file's count rises — a repo-wide total would let a fix in one module pay for a regression in another, by unrelated authors in unrelated changes. The `pyright-ratchet` workflow runs it on every pull request with no `continue-on-error`, because a gate that cannot fail is the self-reported tick wearing a CI badge. Scope is `pyright src/`, the command the documents named, over the code that ships: `pyproject.toml` also includes `tests`, and the count over both trees is several times the `src/` one; freezing that today would make every test edit fight the ratchet — so `tests/` is explicitly not gated and the script's docstring says so rather than letting the job name imply full coverage. No document records the wider figure, because it moves with every test edit; `uv run pyright` with no path argument reproduces it. `tests/test_pyright_ratchet_1503.py` pins that the baseline exists and is non-empty (an empty baseline passes everything), that the workflow runs the checker and can fail, and that no document re-asserts the unqualified claim. Two things are reconciled at the close. The **baseline was one turn behind**: `check_pyright_baseline.py` reported 973 errors against a recorded 974, improved in `src/aelfrice/hook_search_tool.py` (11 to 10), and an unlowered baseline silently re-admits the error it just lost, so the baseline is regenerated here at **973 over 75 files**. And the script's docstring described the cancelled burn-down and quoted 991 errors over 77 files, which matched neither the issue body nor any tree; the historical figure is now the one the #1503 baseline commit records — **987 errors over 76 files**, both halves reproducible from `git show ce9e5461:pyright_baseline.json`, where the issue body carries the 987 total alone and no file count — stated once and attributed, with the docstring naming the ruling that stands and pointing at `pyright_baseline.json` for the live count. The docstring also records the environment the count has to be measured in, because the documented developer setup could not reproduce it (#1548).

### Internal

- **`benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py` can now see the charge-vs-emit defect it was blind to ([#1547](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1547)).** **Nothing is fixed here.** The packer still charges a compressed form no renderer emits, and this change only makes the size of that gap measurable; #1547 AC4 requires the producer before any claim is made about the issue, and this is that producer. Two properties had made the defect invisible. The corpus held **zero** snapshot beliefs — `_synthetic_store` passed no `retention_class`, so every row landed on the `RETENTION_UNKNOWN` default, which `compress_for_retrieval` maps to `verbatim`, and every charge equalled its emission by construction. And no lane composed: seven lanes each rendered one block, while the live 66,165-character row that prompted the issue came from the **first-prompt envelope**, where `hook._build_session_start_subblock` and the per-turn pack are rendered into one `<aelfrice-memory>` block by `hook._format_hits_with_session_start`. There is now a `first_prompt` lane that renders exactly that composition, registered in `_RENDERERS` and `LANES` like any other, and a snapshot arm built from a `SNAPSHOT_EVERY` stride beside the existing `LOCK_EVERY` and `SPECULATIVE_EVERY` ones. `corpus_shape` reads `retention_class` back off the store, reporting `snapshot` and `snapshot_unlocked` separately because a *locked* snapshot renders verbatim — locks override retention class — so the published counts are measured rather than taken from the generator's arithmetic. On the arm's corpus that reads 43 and 42 of 300; on the control corpus it reads 0 and 0, which is the old blindness now printed rather than assumed. `corpus_shape` reports a `sentence_headline` count beside those, because the retention class is only half of what the arm's corpus adds: the count is the beliefs carrying a sentence boundary at or before `compression.MAX_HEADLINE_CHARS`, taken from `compression._first_sentence_end_outside_fence` rather than from a second copy of its rule, and it reads **300** of 300 on the prose and snapshot corpora against **0** on the control. All three shapes are now read at the top of the arm's grid rather than at its foot: at 92 content characters no belief closes a sentence, so a shape read there could confirm the class was written but never the text change.

  **Charged against emitted, one belief, by retention class.** One constraint had to be met before the arm measured anything, and the old generator never met it: a sentence boundary — a `. ` or `.\n` outside a code fence — ending at or before `compression.MAX_HEADLINE_CHARS`. That boundary is the only thing that takes `compression._headline` down its first-sentence branch. The guard that returns the content unchanged is conjunctive — no boundary **and** no longer than the cap — so length on its own is neither necessary nor sufficient, and the table below shows it: at 150 and 200 content characters, both under the cap, a `snapshot` belief reports strategy `headline`, charged 44 tokens, ratio 1.2x and 1.4x. Past the cap with no boundary the function hard-truncates instead, which is a third code path with a third byte count. The generator now closes a sentence every `SENTENCE_CHARS` (120) characters, and `LENGTH_GRID` runs `(40, 92, 150, 200, 300, 1000, 6004, 7170, 18600)` — the first two below `SENTENCE_CHARS` and therefore the arm's inert control, 200 at `hook_search_tool.PER_LINE_CHAR_CAP`, 300 the old top of the grid, then p90 of live per-turn `<belief>` element size, the 6,004 characters at which the two arms' `<core>` accountings part company (below), p99, and the 18,600 characters #1547 measured at.

  *Charged tokens over emitted tokens, one belief per cell. Re-derive the whole table with `uv run python benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py --emit-figures`. The **bold** cells and every figure carrying a `derived:` marker at the foot of this entry are re-run by `scripts/check_derived_figures.py` on every PR and fail it when they drift; the rest of the table is that command's output at the time of writing.*

  | content chars | `fact` | `snapshot` | `transient` | `unknown` |
  |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | 92 | 1.0x | 1.0x | 1.4x | 1.0x |
  | 150 | 1.0x | 1.2x | 2.0x | 1.0x |
  | 200 | 1.0x | 1.4x | 2.5x | 1.0x |
  | 300 | 1.0x | 2.0x | 3.5x | 1.0x |
  | 1,000 | 1.0x | 6.0x | 10.5x | 1.0x |
  | 6,004 | 0.2x | 7.2x | 12.7x | 0.2x |
  | 7,170 | 0.2x | 7.2x | 12.7x | 0.2x |
  | 18,600 | 0.1x | **7.2x** | **12.7x** | 0.1x |

  **The ratio has a ceiling, and the verbatim control changes sign at it.** Both are [#1552](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1552), which caps the content the renderer emits at `hook.BELIEF_CONTENT_CHAR_CAP = 1200`. The `snapshot` charge is a fixed-size headline — flat at **44** tokens from 150 content characters up — and the emitted side is now flat too, at **317** tokens, from the character past the cap up. So the undercharge stops growing with belief length: it reaches **7.2x** at 1,201 characters and is still 7.2x at 18,600. The `fact` and `unknown` columns are the same cap seen from the other side: they are charged the whole content and emit the capped line, so past the cap they **over**charge — 4,663 charged against 317 emitted at 18,600 characters, 0.1x — and that is published in the same columns rather than filtered out. `test_a_snapshot_belief_is_charged_less_than_the_lane_emits` asserts the ceiling as an equality across every above-cap grid length, so removing the cap reds it rather than quietly restoring the older, larger figure.

  **This table is `agent_context`'s and no other lane's.** `_emitted_chars` renders through `hook._split_belief_lines`, which is the shape `ups`, `first_prompt` and `agent_context` all emit — but #1551 and #1552 wired `hook._ups_belief_line_cost` onto the first two as a `belief_cost_fn`, and that short-circuits `retrieve_with_tiers._cost` before compression is consulted. `agent_context` passes none, so it is the one lane in this producer that still pays the compressed charge for a line it renders in full, and `SNAPSHOT_ARM_HEADLINE_LANE` records why the arm's charge-vs-emit claim is quoted from it.

  **This still disagrees with the issue's prior, and #1552 moved the disagreement to the other column.** #1547 reads 150.4x and 388.6x at 18,600 characters; this measures 7.2x and 12.7x. The charged side is **44** tokens here against the issue's 31 and 25 against 12, all of which is the 13-token `<belief>` element `retrieval._render_wrapper_tokens` adds to every compressed render — [#1526](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1526)'s correction, which postdates the figure the issue quotes; net of it the two charges agree, 31 against 31 and 12 against 12. The emitted side used to agree exactly at 4,663 tokens and no longer does, because the cap means the element the issue measured at 18,600 characters is the element this measures at 1,201. The gap the issue reported was real and was measured on an uncapped renderer; what is left of it is bounded by the cap. The ratio is still a property of the corpus as much as of the defect — it is set by where the first sentence ends — so the charged and emitted token columns are published beside it and the multiplier is never quoted alone.

  **Three corpora, so the class effect is attributable.** The arm is measured against a middle corpus that adds the sentence boundaries and nothing else, because a two-corpus comparison would confound the retention class with the text change needed to make it fire. `control → prose` is that text change. On `ups` it is byte-identical at 92, 1,000 and 18,600 content characters and moves at 300 (6,427 → 6,763 bytes) and 7,170 (46,402 → 46,641); on `first_prompt` the same shape plus a byte at 1,000 and at 18,600 from the `<core>` section packed above the hits; on `agent_context` it is byte-identical at every arm length, which is what makes that lane's class effect attributable to the class alone. `prose → snapshot` is the class — and on two of the three lanes it is now **nil**, which is the finding rather than a gap in the measurement.

  On `ups` at 18,600 characters the prose and snapshot packs are the same pack: **8** admitted beliefs, 114,982 bytes, both ending on `token_budget`, of which the **2** non-locked ones are charged **634** tokens against **634** emitted, **1.0x**. #1551 and #1552 closed the charge-vs-emit gap on that lane by giving it a `belief_cost_fn` that *is* the line it emits, so the retention class no longer reaches the charge at all. The composed lane reads the same shape one envelope up: `first_prompt` emits **119,928** bytes and admits **8** beliefs on the prose corpus and on the snapshot corpus alike. An earlier revision of this entry read "admitting 22 beliefs charged 723 tokens whose emitted text is 74,616 tokens, 103.2x", which was a mixed denominator as well as a stale figure: 22 was `Arm.n_items`, including the six locks [#379](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/379) exempts, while the charge and the emission were summed by `_pack_charge` over the 16 non-locked hits, and no single set of beliefs was both. The admitted count, the non-locked subset, the charge and the emission are now lifted to scalar keys as a set by `_arm_cell_keys` and re-derived together, so they cannot drift apart in a sentence again.

  Where the gap survives is `agent_context`, the one arm lane that passes no `belief_cost_fn` and therefore still charges the compressed form. At **300** content characters the snapshot corpus admits **10** beliefs against the prose corpus's **9** and emits **4,075** bytes against **3,723** — **1.09x** on the same unchanged budget — and the **4** non-locked hits in that pack are charged **266** tokens against the **352** their emitted text costs, **1.3x**, where the prose corpus's **3** are charged **264** against **264**, **1.0**. A budget reporting that it is doing its job is the defect there, not the absence of one. **Every figure in that sentence is re-derived, and none of them was until this pass.** `_arm_cell_keys` was applied at the top of the grid only — where this lane is lock-starved and publishes the zeros below — so the entry's headline claim was the one claim in it `scripts/check_derived_figures.py` could not reach, and the entry could have quoted any number and stayed at exit 0. The whole row is now lifted under its own key names, both corpora, and the arm length is published beside it as `snapshot_arm_quoted_chars` so a sentence read off a different row fails the marker check. The length is not a literal either: `test_the_snapshot_arm_admits_beliefs_the_control_cannot_afford` derives it from the produced figures — the largest arm length at which the class admits a belief the prose corpus does not — requires the constant to equal it, and checks every lifted scalar against the cell it was lifted from. Both halves bite: moving `SNAPSHOT_ARM_QUOTED_CHARS` to another arm length takes `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode all` from exit 0 to **14** hard failures and reds that test on `assert 300 == 1000`. At 7,170 and 18,600 characters that lane is lock-starved rather than measured: its six user locks are exempt from `BELIEF_CONTENT_CHAR_CAP`, they spend the whole budget before the pack loop reaches a candidate, and all three corpora return the same **6** items and **0** non-locked hits, so the pack ratio is undefined. Those zeros are published rather than suppressed, and `test_the_snapshot_arm_admits_beliefs_the_control_cannot_afford` asserts them. Both budgets are varied on all three sides, so every cell still names the cap that ended it, which is the property [#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546) requires of any new measurement.

  **What the composed lane measures, and the `<core>` cost function it needed.** At the corpus median of 92 content characters the first-prompt envelope goes 19,999 → 13,365 bytes, **-33.2%**, against the per-turn lane's 9,259 → 6,763. The composed change is larger than the per-turn change because the envelope also carries `<core>`, whose accounting #1526 changed as well — and the first revision of this lane did not measure that. `_legacy_accounting` rebound the retrieval-side names only; `_build_session_start_subblock` packs `<core>` through `hook._core_belief_cost`, which was not among them, and unlike the standalone `core` lane it takes no `cost_fn` to pass a legacy body into. Every composed before cell was therefore a hybrid of pre-#1526 retrieval cost and post-#1526 `<core>` cost, reading 15,861 bytes where a consistent before arm reads 19,999 and publishing -15.7% for a -33.2% change. The rebind set is now a table, the `core` lane's explicit plumbing is gone in favour of it, and `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py` scans `src/aelfrice` for the #1526 cost functions and fails when one is in neither the rebind table nor the named-exception table.

  **The AC2 dedupe now has a producer too.** The envelope dedupe that shipped in this issue could not be measured by any lane here, because it only fires where a belief appears in both halves of a composed envelope. On the `first_prompt` lane at 92 content characters, 13 of 41 hits are already rendered in a 6,813-character session-start sub-block and the envelope comes out 13,577 → 13,365 bytes, **-1.6%**. The lane's `<recent-work>` section is built on a cwd git cannot discover a repository from — enforced by the barriers above rather than inherited from wherever `$TMPDIR` points — and its size is read back out of the sub-block the lane rendered rather than from a second call to the builder: a separate probe agrees with the lane only while both resolve the same cwd, so it constrained nothing. Read off the block it measures **0** characters, and handing the same builder a git cwd outside a producer run — where no barrier is up — moves that figure and the composed block's size with it.

  **No published #1526 figure moved.** The generator's word-list re-join was quadratic and was most of the time one 18,600-character store took to build, which is the mechanical reason the grid could not previously reach the lengths the defect lives at; it now accumulates a running length instead and emits byte-identical content, checked by `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode all`, which re-runs this producer and diffs every marker naming it against the value published beside it. The extended grid did surface one new measurement: above `DEFAULT_SESSION_START_CORE_TOKEN_BUDGET` a single `<core>` line no longer fits **in the before arm**, so at 6,004 content characters and above the pre-#1526 charge `max(1, len(content) // 4)` overruns that budget, `_pack_core_candidates` skips every candidate and the section emits **0 bytes**. The after arm cannot empty at any length: #1552's cap holds the shipped charge below that budget for content of any length, so `<core>`'s after-arm curve bottoms out above zero. Those zeros are measured values, not suppressed cells, and `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` admits them — and no others — by re-deriving the condition that produces them: a zero cell must come from `<core>`, at a length where one `<core>` line costs more than `DEFAULT_SESSION_START_CORE_TOKEN_BUDGET` in the currency the emitting arm packs under. Every other lane and length reaches that same branch and fails it, which is where the floor lives. `test_the_core_section_empties_once_one_belief_exceeds_its_budget` pins both halves independently. The producer run is also now pinned against the ambient environment: `figures()` itself clears every `AELFRICE_` variable for the duration of a run, because the resolvers it reaches read one *before* the budget their caller passes in, and a value exported in a shell must not move a published figure. It is cleared in the producer rather than in the test fixture because `scripts/check_derived_figures.py` runs the producer as a subprocess with the caller's environment inherited, so the two callers were measuring different things; `test_the_producer_is_hermetic_against_an_exported_budget` asserts the resolver precedence first and then that an exported budget moves no published figure. The same run reads through a second ambient channel, and it was open: `figures()` composes the shipped session-start sub-block from inside its tempdir, `<recent-work>` resolves a branch and a commit log from git plumbing run under that cwd, and "the tempdir is not a git work tree" was a property of `$TMPDIR` rather than of this tree. Point `$TMPDIR` at any directory inside a checkout and eight published scalars move — `first_prompt_bytes_before` / `_after`, `dedupe_bytes_before` / `_after`, `first_prompt_recent_work_chars`, the composed lane's two arm byte counts and `first_prompt_pct` — seven of them markers this gate re-derives on every PR. `_hermetic_environment` now clears `GIT_DIR` / `GIT_WORK_TREE` / `GIT_COMMON_DIR` beside the `AELFRICE_` prefix and then raises two barriers inside the tempdir: `GIT_DIR` at a repository under it that does not exist, and `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` at its parent. Two, because the ceiling alone does not cover every `$TMPDIR`: git reads it as a `PATH`-style list, splitting on `:` and discarding every entry that is not absolute, so a tempdir whose parent path contains a colon — a legal character in a POSIX directory name, and the list has no escaping form for it — gets no usable ceiling and the ascent runs to the root anyway. `GIT_DIR` is single-valued, git never splits it, and a `GIT_DIR` naming a directory that does not exist makes every spawn exit 128 rather than discover anything. Measured three ways: under the default `$TMPDIR` the emitted figures are byte-identical before and after the change; under a `$TMPDIR` planted inside a git work tree they go from **8** scalar differences over the full grid to **0**; and under one planted inside a work tree whose path contains a colon they go from the same leak — `<recent-work>` filled, the composed lane's byte counts moved — to **0**. `test_the_producer_is_hermetic_against_a_tempdir_inside_a_work_tree` plants both work trees, requires git to resolve a branch from inside each before it asserts anything, and then requires each produced blob to be the one the default `$TMPDIR` produces; deleting the `GIT_DIR` line reds it on the colon planting's `first_prompt_recent_work_chars`, and deleting both barrier lines reds it on the ordinary one. Deleting the ceiling line alone reds neither: with `GIT_DIR` in place nothing in the tree distinguishes the ceiling, and it is kept as a second barrier rather than sold as a load-bearing one. That guard is the first test in `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py` to spawn a subprocess, and it turned up a hole in a neighbouring gate: [#1307](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1307)'s subprocess-budget check read only the literal `@pytest.mark.timeout` spelling, while this file binds the mark once as `_pays_for_the_producer_run` and decorates thirteen tests with the name. It reported the new test as unbudgeted, and the only way to clear the report was a second timeout decorator beside the alias — the duplicated-budget failure that gate's own `pytestmark` rule exists to prevent, one scope down. It now accepts a module-level name bound to a `pytest.mark.timeout` mark in the same file and nothing else, with both directions asserted: dropping the alias decorator from the spawning test still reds it. The same walk is narrowed in the other direction at the same time, because it had been reading a mark that sets nothing as a budget: `pytest-timeout` takes the number off `marker.args[0]` or a `timeout=` keyword, so a bare `pytest.mark.timeout` and an empty `pytest.mark.timeout()` leave the test on the suite default, and a module-level name bound to either would have cleared the gate for every test it decorated. The call and a value inside it are now both required, and `test_a_mark_that_sets_no_timeout_is_not_a_budget` pins both spellings in all three positions the rule reads a mark — module alias, `pytestmark`, class decorator — against a control with the argument put back; dropping the call requirement reds it on the alias set. The prefix clear is also no longer sold as covering more than it does — it covers a later resolver that reads an `AELFRICE_` variable, and the git names it does not cover are why they had to be enumerated. That gate also runs every producer under a `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX` it owns: CPython validates cached bytecode on the source's (mtime-seconds, size) alone, so a same-size mutation and its revert inside one mtime second would otherwise leave it re-deriving figures from code that is not in the tree.

  **The producer is soaked, not assumed stable.** A review reported the published `agent_context` cell reading two different sets of values on a clean tree. It does not reproduce: `scripts/soak_producer_figures.py` runs the producer N times under one bytecode-cache prefix, hashes stdout each time and exits non-zero unless every hash agrees, and `uv run python scripts/soak_producer_figures.py --runs 28 --jobs 4` reported 28 of 28 identical at sha256 `fec711012dbf93f141c4159c769ebd6eac3c718b6f2a0a28d5c3b81269a4bd37` on this branch, four runs at a time on a loaded machine. Both arms of the soak are tested in `tests/test_soak_producer_figures.py`, because a soak that cannot fail measures nothing. What the report *is* consistent with is stale bytecode — a same-size mutation and its revert inside one mtime second, which is how the mutations in this entry are performed — and that channel is closed above.

  **A second report named a population that soak cannot see, and the soak grew an arm for it.** A later review reported `test_the_core_section_empties_once_one_belief_exceeds_its_budget` failing on run 4 of 11 consecutive runs of the committed tree, and observed correctly that a soak of cold subprocesses is blind to anything a *warm* interpreter carries between runs — a memoised resolver, a module global a caller rebound, whatever an earlier test in the same session left behind. `scripts/soak_producer_figures.py --in-process` is that arm: the producer is imported once and `figures()` is called N times in one interpreter. It is tested the same way the default arm is, on an emitter that moves on module state, and the same emitter is asserted to pass the default arm — "the new arm passes" says nothing unless the old one is shown to be blind to what it catches. It does not reproduce either. On a machine at load average 12-18 with three pytest sessions running at once: **12 of 12** consecutive full-module runs green, **32** in-process `figures()` calls inside pytest sessions that had already run the module's other tests — the population the report named — all byte-identical, and **12 of 12** runs of the named test on its own, where the fixture's producer run is charged to it. Instrumented with a counting wrapper around `hook._git_text`, each run spawns one git subprocess per distinct `_SESSION_START_BLOCKS` key and none per memo hit — 28 against 45 on the full grid, reproduced across runs — and every one of them returned None without coming within an order of magnitude of `hook._RECENT_WORK_GIT_TIMEOUT_S`. The `$TMPDIR` defect above is a real non-hermeticity in the same function and is fixed; it is **not** offered as the explanation for that report, because nothing here reproduced it and a fix nobody watched fail is not a fix.

  ---

  *Everything below this line is the review record: one paragraph per adversarial pass, each a time-stamped account of what a command printed when that pass ran. Read it as history. The current state of the tree is the section above and the markers at the foot.*

  **Three of the guards over this producer could not fail.** An adversarial re-review of the tests above found each one, and the shape is the same in all three: an assertion written against the thing that produced it. `_recent_work_chars_in` replaced by `return 0` left the suite green, *including* the cwd mutation the reader exists to catch — a reader that always answers zero cannot see the lane move, so the one assertion on it held either way. The snapshot arm's lane set was checked with `set(arm) == set(SNAPSHOT_ARM_LANES)`, comparing the produced dict against the constant that produced it, so dropping `first_prompt` from the constant dropped the composed envelope out of the arm and stayed green. And the middle corpus had no assertion that it differed from the control at all: generated with its sentence boundaries left off it is byte-identical to the control at every length, and every assertion written against it stays true while it attributes nothing. Each now carries a check that must go the other way — the reader is asserted to find the section in a block that carries one, the lanes are named literally, and the middle corpus must differ from the control both in `sentence_headline` and in rendered bytes at some length in the grid. All three are mutation-proved against the exact mutation that exposed them.

  **Two more, including one the repair above introduced.** A second adversarial pass found that the lane-set repair had kept its unfalsifiable half, and that one measured zero had been paid for with a floor across the whole grid. The set equality `set(arm) == set(SNAPSHOT_ARM_LANES)` was kept beside the literal lane names to catch "a produced key no constant declares", which it cannot do: `snapshot_arm` builds its dict by iterating that constant and nothing mutates it afterwards, so the equality is an invariant of the function under every mutation of every file. It is replaced by `set(arm) <= set(LANES)`, which the arm is not generated from and which fails when the arm measures a lane no #1526 curve covers. Separately, the per-cell floor had gone from `> 0` to `>= 0` across all eight lanes and every grid length to admit the single `<core>` cell measured to be empty, so any lane could emit nothing at any length with the suite green: an oversize guard on `_render_session_start` turns that lane's published **112,456**-byte cell at 18,600 content characters into a zero, and under the relaxed floor and the per-lane floor beside it the file stayed green. The floor is `> 0` again, with the one zero admitted by re-deriving the condition that empties it, and the same mutation now reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on `assert 'session_start' == 'core'`. Both are mutation-proved, each against the mutation that exposed it and against a control that shows the replaced assertion passing on it.

  **And two more inside that repair.** A third adversarial pass read the exemption the floor was traded for and found both of its assertions inert. The exemption branched on the packing arm's accounting, and the claim that a zero is read in the currency of the arm that packed it does not hold up: the two currencies never separate on this grid. Against a `<core>` budget of 1,500 tokens one line costs 10 pre-#1526 against 27 as shipped at 40 content characters, 250 against 267 at 1,000, 1,792 against 1,810 at 7,170 and 4,650 against 4,667 at 18,600 — they cross the budget at 6,004 and 5,934 characters, and the grid stepped 1,000 → 7,170, so deleting either half of the branch left the file green. That pass replaced the branch with `min(legacy, shipped) > budget` — one line overrunning the budget under *both* accountings — which a fourth pass found no better; see below. Beside it, an `else: assert row[arm] > 0` was reachable only for a negative int — the `isinstance` check above it and the zero branch beside it leave nothing else, and every value it guarded is an `Arm.n_bytes` summed from lengths — and replacing its body with `pass` left the file green. It is deleted rather than restated: the floor is the zero branch, which still reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` under the `_render_session_start` oversize guard, on `assert 'session_start' == 'core'`.
