  **The last one needed a grid length, not an assertion.** A fourth pass read the `min(legacy, shipped) > budget` repair above and found it inert for the same reason the branch it replaced was: `min` of that pair is identically the pre-#1526 half, because `max(1, len(content) // 4)` is strictly below the shipped charge on the rendered line at every content length — 0 exceptions over 1..20,000 step 37 — and replacing the `min` with either operand alone left the file green. The reduction was also the wrong currency for the claim, which is that a zero cell is legitimate when the arm that packed it could not afford one line **in its own accounting**. The exemption now reads the emitting arm's half, and `LENGTH_GRID` gains **5,950** characters so that the branch decides something: the two charges cross the `<core>` budget of 1,500 tokens at 5,934 shipped and 6,004 pre-#1526, and the grid stepped 1,000 → 7,170 straight over that gap. At 5,950 one line costs 1,487 pre-#1526 against 1,505 as shipped, so the before arm packs it and emits **6,017** bytes while the after arm packs none — the first grid cell where the two accountings disagree about whether a `<core>` line fits. Restoring `min`, and separately swapping the two arms' halves, each red `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on `assert 1487 > 1500`; removing 5,950 from the grid and restoring `min` together left the file green, which is what the length buys. The only published figure the new length moves is the charged-vs-emitted table, which gains its 5,950 row above; every headline byte count and every derived marker below is byte-identical to the run before it.

  **The fifth pass found that repair pinned to nothing but itself.** Two halves, one shape. The 5,950 grid entry was load-bearing and referenced nowhere else, so trimming `LENGTH_GRID` restored the inert state above with no test, no marker and no reviewer signal: dropping it back to the eight-length tuple, and separately moving it to 5,900 — still a ninth length, 34 characters below the window — each left the file green, and the module fixture's setup is a large fraction of the 30-second per-test timeout CI pins, which is standing pressure to trim. The length is now held by a condition rather than by its value: some grid length must charge one `<core>` line at or below the `<core>` budget in the pre-#1526 currency and above it as shipped, which is the window 5,934 to 6,003 whatever number occupies it. Both mutations now red `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on the `any(...)` over the grid, as does a third that moves the window instead of the grid: raising `DEFAULT_SESSION_START_CORE_TOKEN_BUDGET` to 2,000 puts the crossing at around 8,000 characters, off every grid length, on a grid that still holds 5,950. That paragraph also claimed one half of the exemption could not be refuted by any cell; the claim is deleted, because it was drawn from a mutation set that was entirely test-side.

  **The sixth pass found the published window red at its own lower edge, and the pin blind to the curve.** The window 5,934 to 6,003 is the condition that holds the ninth grid length, but the sibling guard `test_the_core_section_empties_once_one_belief_exceeds_its_budget` charged the rendered `<core>` line without the newline `"\n".join` puts after it — which is what `_core_belief_cost` charges, and therefore what decides whether the section empties. One character understated the packer, so the guard enforced a lower edge of 5,935 while the docstring and this entry published 5,934. The guard now charges `len(line) + 1`, and the window is unchanged because it was always the packer's. Second, everything holding that length was arithmetic re-derived from `LENGTH_GRID` and the budget scalar, so a producer that stopped emptying the crossing cell restored the inert state above with the grid untouched. The asymmetry is now asserted off the produced `<core>` curve as well — some cell must pack a line in the before arm and none in the after arm — and two producer-side mutations prove it. Packing both arms under the pre-#1526 cost function in `_render_core` makes 5,950 emit 6,017 bytes in both arms and reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on the curve assertion `assert []` and `test_the_effect_is_length_dependent_and_changes_sign` on `assert 0.0 < 0.0`, where before the curve assertion it left the grid pin green. Refilling an empty pack from the top candidate reds the same two guards — the curve assertion and the sibling's own `assert []` — where before it reached the sibling alone.

  **The seventh pass found that sixth repair revertible, and a published mutation pair that no longer reproduces.** The newline charge above is the whole behavioural content of its commit, and reverting it left the file green: it moves the window's lower edge from 5,935 to 5,934, and the only grid length inside the window is 5,950, 16 characters in and over the budget under both charges. The condition that holds the grid length only asks that some length fall *inside* the window, so nothing written over `LENGTH_GRID` can see an edge move. Both edges are now charged directly: at 5,934 and 6,003 one `<core>` line must cost at or below the budget pre-#1526 and above it as shipped, and at 5,933 and 6,004 it must not. Dropping the newline from `_core_pack_costs_at` reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on `assert (1500 < 1500) is True`; dropping it from the sibling guard reds `test_the_core_section_empties_once_one_belief_exceeds_its_budget` on `assert 1500 > 1500`; dropping both reds both. Raising `DEFAULT_SESSION_START_CORE_TOKEN_BUDGET` to 2,000 now reds the sibling too, on `assert 1501 > 2000`, because the published edges have to track the budget. Separately, this entry published a two-step pair showing the exemption's before-arm half is load-bearing: an oversize guard in `_render_core` returning `Arm(0, 0)` for the 6,017-byte cell reds the exemption naming the before arm on `assert 1487 > 1500`, and with that guard in place, charging the before arm in the shipped currency was said to go green again. Step one still reproduces; step two does not — it reds the same test on the curve assertion `assert []`, which was added after the pair was measured and which the guard also breaks. The two assertions overlap exactly on this grid, 5,950 being the only cell inside the window, and charging the before arm in the shipped currency on the shipped producer leaves the file green. The half is kept as the stricter of the two currencies and as the better failure message, and is no longer published as mutation-proved. Every figure in this entry is a failing test name and a failing assertion rather than a pass count, because the rebase onto `github/main` at bc35845d took `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py` from 43 collected cases to 52 and made every published count stale by three at once. No published figure moves: `benchmarks/injection_budget_bytes.py` and `src/aelfrice/hook.py` are byte-identical to the commit before this one, and `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode all` re-derives every marker naming this producer (77 across 965 files at the time of that pass; the tenth pass below prints the current count).

  **The eighth pass re-ran every mutation this entry publishes, and found one figure the tree contradicts.** The two blocking artefacts raised against the seventh pass were re-measured here on `github/main` at bc35845d, and both hold as published. The newline charge is no longer revertible: dropping it from `_core_pack_costs_at` reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on `assert (1500 < 1500) is True`, dropping it from the sibling guard's `charged()` reds `test_the_core_section_empties_once_one_belief_exceeds_its_budget` on `assert 1500 > 1500`, and dropping both reds both. The upper rim is enforced too, not just the lower one the newline moved. That pass recorded the mutation against `_CORE_CROSSING_HIGH`, a name the tree no longer carries — the rim pair collapsed into the single `_CORE_CROSSING` in the same commit that re-derived these guards for #1552's inversion — so it is re-measured here as it stands: moving `_CORE_CROSSING` to 6,002 reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` at 6,002 content characters, on `assert (1500 < 1500) is True`, and the sibling on `assert 6004 == 6002`. One constant now charges both rims, at `_CORE_CROSSING` and one character below it. And the mutation pair that had stopped reproducing behaves exactly as the seventh pass restated it: the `_render_core` oversize guard reds `test_the_producer_names_which_budget_ended_every_pack` on `assert 1487 > 1500` naming the before arm; charging the before arm in the shipped currency *with that guard in place* reds the same test on the curve assertion `assert []`; and charging it in the shipped currency on the shipped producer leaves the file green. What did not survive was a duration. The fixture docstring published "about 8 seconds" for the module-scoped producer run while this entry published 15.9 — two figures for one measurement, and the tree matches neither. That pass measured **15.5 seconds**, ranging 14.7 to 16.4 over seven runs, read as the `setup` row of `uv run pytest tests/test_render_cost_1526.py -q -p no:randomly --durations=0` — a third figure that the eleventh pass below retired in turn, because it does not reproduce on another machine either. That figure is load-bearing rather than incidental, because the per-test timeout covers fixture setup: a `@pytest.mark.timeout(5)` on the first test that requests these figures errors *inside* the fixture with `Failed: Timeout (>5.0s) from pytest-timeout`. So the first consumer spends roughly half of its 30 seconds before its own body starts and has about 14 left, and the headroom is now published beside the duration, because a reader sizing the grid needs the margin and not only the cost. Separately, every commit body on this branch that published a mutation result as an absolute pass count now gives the failing test name and the failing assertion instead, which is the currency the docstrings and this entry already use; those counts were invalidated by the rebase that took this file from 43 collected cases to 52, and the rewrite changes no tracked byte.

  **The ninth pass found the eighth pass's duration correction half-applied, and a count nothing prints.** No guard moved: the reviewer re-ran all four mutations this entry publishes and invented eleven more, and every one is red. Both findings are published figures. First, `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py` still said "the run costs about eight seconds" in the section banner over the #1547 block, 450 lines below the fixture docstring the eighth pass corrected to **15.5 seconds** — one file, one measurement, two numbers, and the stale one sits exactly where a reader decides whether to split these tests into a second module with a second module-scoped fixture. That reader would have priced the split at eight seconds and paid 15.5. The banner now carries no number and names the docstring that does, so the duration is published once. Second, this entry and `_synthetic_store`'s docstring both said the corpus is read by "twelve markers", which no command emits: `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode all` prints a count of derived-figure markers and of the files they were found in — 77 across 965 at the time of that pass — split into store-free and store-backed, where `store-backed` means carrying a `corpus=` attribute, which no marker naming this producer does. The numeral is dropped from both; each sentence now names the command and the property it checks — the producer is re-run and every marker naming it is diffed against the value published beside it — which is the claim that was always the load-bearing one. A third figure of the same class turned up beside it: `SNAPSHOT_ARM_LENGTHS` said building the arm's corpus at "all eight" grid lengths would double the runtime, written when the grid was eight lengths and left behind when the fourth pass added 5,950. Since the ninth length is held by a condition and not by its value, the comment now names the grid rather than counting it.

  **The tenth pass was the [#1552](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1552) rebase, and it moved the defect out from under two of this producer's three arm lanes.** #1552 capped the content the renderers emit at `hook.BELIEF_CONTENT_CHAR_CAP = 1200` and wired `hook._ups_belief_line_cost` onto the UPS lane as a `belief_cost_fn`, and this producer was still modelling the cost path that argument short-circuits: `_render_ups`, `_render_first_prompt` and `_pack_charge` all called `retrieval.retrieve` without it, so the arm published a discount from `retrieval.py:4802`, a branch those two lanes never reach. That was a producer defect, not figure drift, and re-deriving 106.0x to 7.2x without fixing it would have published a number from a dead code path. The three call sites now resolve through one `_lane_belief_cost` table, `_lane_hits` gives the renderer and the charge the same retrieval so a pack a byte count is read off cannot be a different pack from the one a charge is summed over, and `hook._ups_belief_line_cost` is listed in `LEGACY_COST_NOT_REBOUND` with the reason its `hook_search_tool` sibling already carries. `test_every_1526_cost_function_is_rebound_or_named_as_an_exception` had already caught the missing table row and was not touched.

  **Two more published claims inverted with it, and each moved rather than being bumped.** The `<core>` crossing: the shipped charge is now capped at 320 tokens and crosses the 1,500-token budget nowhere, while the uncapped pre-#1526 charge crosses it at exactly 6,004 characters, so `legacy <= budget < shipped` is unsatisfiable at every content length and the 5,934-to-6,003 window is a half-line at 6,004. `LENGTH_GRID`'s ninth entry moves from 5,950 to that edge, where it is load-bearing again — it is the first grid length whose before arm packs no `<core>` line, and `test_the_core_section_empties_once_one_belief_exceeds_its_budget` asserts the emptied set against the lengths whose pre-#1526 charge overruns the budget and asserts its first member is the published edge. That guard's old premise, that the *shipped* arm empties somewhere, is unreachable by construction now; it is replaced by the two facts that replaced it, both stronger than what they replace: the shipped arm cannot empty at any content length (320 tokens over a sweep to 120,000 characters, 1,220 for content that is entirely angle brackets, against 1,500), and the before arm empties at 6,004 and above. The saturation probe: `SATURATION_PROBE_FACTOR = 4` was sized for a grid topping out at 300 characters, and it multiplies the *lane's* budget rather than what a belief costs — at 18,600 the cells that read `pool` first move at a `token_budget` near 9,300, twice a single belief's charge, and the only 4x probe in this module that reaches it is `cli_search`'s 9,600. Every other lane's sits below it, `ups`'s 6,000 included, so a 4x probe could not admit one more belief there and 20 of the 45 `pool` labels among this module's 189 arms were false. `_probe_budget` replaces the factor with a bound read off the store — the sum of the largest per-belief charge any packer here can bill — the factor survives as a floor, every cell publishes the probe its label was established at, and a `pool` is re-rendered at twice the probe before it is published, so a probe that turns out to be too small raises `PoolProbeTooSmall` instead of emitting the label. Twenty arm labels flip `pool` → `token_budget`, none flip the other way, and all 18 `session_start` arms stay `pool`, so [#1546](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1546)'s finding is intact. No published byte or item figure moves for the probe — all 189 arms are byte- and item-identical between the two probes, checked by running the producer under each — because every arm renders at its unchanged shipped budget and only the label reads the probe.

  **And the headline moved to the lane that still carries the defect.** `agent_context` is the only lane this module drives that passes no `belief_cost_fn`, so it is the only one still charging the compressed form, and `SNAPSHOT_ARM_HEADLINE_LANE` records the reasoning and the verification: `grep -rn "belief_cost_fn=" src/aelfrice/` returns five lines, of which exactly two bind a cost function rather than forwarding one — `hook.py:3385` (`_ups_belief_line_cost`) and `hook_search_tool.py:866` (`_belief_line_cost`). The other three are `belief_cost_fn=belief_cost_fn`, each passing its own caller's argument through (`retrieval.py:4587`, `retrieval.py:5370`, `hook_search.py:149`), and that argument defaults to `None`; a grep that counts lines rather than bindings reads five where the claim needs two. The `>10x` claim is not relaxed to fit the smaller number anywhere; on the single-belief table it moves to the `transient` class at **12.7x**, and `snapshot`'s **7.2x** is published as a ceiling the cap imposes rather than as a measurement that shrank. Six derived markers were stale and are re-derived: `undercharge_top_emitted_tokens` 4663 → **317**, `undercharge_top_snapshot_ratio` 106.0 → **7.2**, `undercharge_top_transient_ratio` 186.5 → **12.7**, `snapshot_arm_first_prompt_prose_bytes` 113434 → **119928**, `snapshot_arm_first_prompt_snapshot_bytes` 412121 → **119928**, `snapshot_arm_first_prompt_snapshot_items` 22 → **8**. Ten are added — the `ups` cell's five, the headline lane's item and non-locked-hit counts, the single-belief charge, and the corpus's two snapshot counts — taking `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode all` from 124 markers to 134, and the fourteen the headline sentence above needed take it to **148**. The composed lane's sign at 18,600 characters flips with them: `first_prompt` reads 113,434 before against **119,927** after, **+5.7%**, because `<core>` emits 5,120 bytes in the after arm and 0 in the before arm.

  **Every guard rewritten here is mutation-proved, and two that were not gated now are.** Fourteen mutations, each an exact string replacement with `__pycache__` cleared either side, run with `uv run pytest tests/test_render_cost_1526.py -q -p no:randomly`. Dropping 6,004 from `LENGTH_GRID` reds the core guard on `assert 7170 == 6004`; refilling an empty `<core>` pack from the top candidate reds it on `assert [] == [6004, 7170, 18600]` and the crossing guard on `assert []`; raising `DEFAULT_SESSION_START_CORE_TOKEN_BUDGET` to 2,000 reds the crossing guard on `assert (2000 < 1501) is True` and the core guard on `assert 18600 == 6004`, and to 5,000 reds the `any(...)` itself on `assert False`; removing `_cap_belief_content` from `_core_belief_line` reds the core guard on `assert 30017 < 1500` and from `_belief_element_line` reds the undercharge guard on `assert 3 == 1`; dropping the newline from `charged()` reds the core guard on `assert 54 == 55` at 150 characters; dropping `"ups"` from `_lane_belief_cost` reds the arm guard on `assert 20 == 17`; the `_render_session_start` oversize guard still reds on `assert 'session_start' == 'core'`. Reverting `_probe_budget` to the factor does not reach an assertion at all — it raises `PoolProbeTooSmall` for `agent_context` at 7,170 characters inside the fixture, 10 items erroring — so the probe assertion is proved by publishing the factor *as* the probe, on `assert 6000 > 6000`, and the fail-closed render is proved by deleting it, on `DID NOT RAISE`. Two gates that had been passing on nothing now bite: `SNAPSHOT_EVERY = 7 → 3` took `--mode all` from exit 0 to **4 hard failures**, where the ninth pass left it green with the corpus going from 43/42 snapshot beliefs to 100/98, and `_pack_charge` no longer excluding locks takes it to **11** hard failures, where before the sentence quoting them was ungated. Three are the `ups` cell's markers at the top of the grid — its snapshot unlocked hits, charged tokens and emitted tokens. The other eight are `agent_context`, which the sentence quoting `ups` does not mention: its snapshot unlocked hits at the top of the grid, and seven of the 300-character cell's — the prose and snapshot columns' unlocked hits, charged tokens and emitted tokens, plus the snapshot pack ratio. That lane passes no `belief_cost_fn`, so the locks land in what `_charged_tokens` is summed over there too; the prose pack ratio in that cell is the one of the pair that holds, because that column charges what it emits with the locks in or out. What no longer reproduces: every mutation result published in the paragraphs above was measured before #1552, and the `+ 1` newline charge the sixth, seventh and eighth passes fought over is no longer distinguishing through the crossing — the shipped charge is capped and one character in it cannot move any comparison. It is pinned instead by a direct equality against `hook._core_belief_cost` at every grid length, which is the mutation at 150 characters above.

  **The eleventh pass removed a wall-clock risk instead of documenting it, and stopped publishing a duration for the third and last time.** Two items sat at 74-79% of the 30-second per-test timeout `ci.yml` pins unscaled with `AELF_TEST_TIMEOUT_SCALE: "1"`, and neither carried a marker of its own. Three changes, each measured. `_measure` rendered all three widenings and the pool-confirm render before reading any of them, but the four labels are a precedence — the first widening whose bytes differ from the arm decides — so each is now rendered only if the label above it did not fire: **781 renders to 461** on the full grid, and the producer's CPU time from 16.9 s and 14.2 s to 12.8 s and 12.0 s over two runs each, with `--emit-figures` byte-identical. `main()` now forwards the `--lengths` knob `figures()` already had, so `test_the_producer_says_the_session_start_number_is_its_own_probe` — which asserts a legend that reads the same at every content length — no longer pays a second full-grid run to read it: **16.2 / 18.9 / 21.4 s to 1.8 / 2.1 / 3.5 s** over three runs each, and reverting `_budget_label`'s `session_start` branch still reds it. And the module-scoped `producer_figures` run, which is charged as `setup` to whichever consumer collection order puts first — nothing in the file pins that, and the `pytest-randomly` the comment used to name is in no dependency group, has been measured at **33.7 s at load average 30** — a healthy run over the budget — so all ten consumers carry a 120-second `timeout` marker, four times the ini default, the same step `tests/conftest.py` takes for a loaded machine; shrinking it to 1 s errors inside the fixture, which is where it has to bite. No duration is published for that run in either file any more: "about 8 seconds", then "15.5 seconds", then a reviewer's 17.06 and 17.58 on the same tree and 21-34 s here — three revisions, none of them reproducible off one machine — so the docstring names the `--durations=0` command and stops. The sibling "about 5 seconds" and the two remaining "15.5 seconds" go with it.

  **The other five findings were published figures, and one of them was the premise under this entry's own probe repair.** Six sites — this entry, the producer's module docstring, `_probe_budget`, and three in `tests/test_render_cost_1526.py` — said "at 18,600 one belief costs more than four times `ups`'s whole budget, so a 4x probe could not admit one more belief". `ups`'s budget is 1,500, and the largest per-belief charge any packer here bills at 18,600 content characters is **4,667 tokens** in every currency: strictly below 6,000, and the `ups` cell does move at its own 4x probe. The conclusion is right for a different reason, re-derived by bisecting `token_budget` against the rendered bytes with `l25_token_subbudget` at its default: the factor multiplies the *lane's* budget rather than what a belief costs, and a cell still flat at 4x first moves at **9,300** on the before arm of every lane and **9,326** on `agent_context`'s after arm — twice one belief's charge — which only `cli_search`'s 4x probe of 9,600 reaches. `_probe_budget`'s own "largest single-belief charge" bullet put that threshold at 12,000, 2.6x, and attributed it to the six locks' cumulative 27,978; it is 9,326, 2.0x, and the cumulative reading is dropped. The 45-to-25 and 20-flip results are unchanged. `_probe_budget` also called its sum an upper bound on every cost path this module drives while maxing over four of six — the two line costs #1551/#1552 wired onto `ups`/`first_prompt` and the PreToolUse lanes were missing — so both are now in the max and the claim holds by construction; neither exceeds the four-way max for any belief of any corpus built here, so every probe, label and published figure is unchanged. Three more counts nothing prints or gates are gone: `synthetic_content` still named "the twelve published figures" the ninth pass had dropped from two of its three homes; `_legacy_accounting` said `LEGACY_COST_NOT_REBOUND` holds "two" cost functions where this branch made it three, and its sibling "five" comes out beside it rather than being corrected; and the `_PROBE_BUDGETS` comment justified the memo with "1,192 calls against 21 stores", where instrumenting `figures()` gives 189 calls from one call site against 19 stores. Finally, `grep -rn "belief_cost_fn=" src/aelfrice/` returns five lines and not the two this entry claimed; the claim now names the command and what separates a binding from a pass-through. `_pack_charge` also selected its cost function with `... or _charged_tokens`, a truthiness test on a value whose `None` is meaningful, now an explicit `is None`.

- **The premise that an observational store open is worth optimizing is refuted, the four components are measured apart, and the invariant underneath them now has a guard ([#1561](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1561)).** `aelf search`, `stats`, `locked`, `speculative` and `show` open through `db_paths.open_store_for_read()`, which attempts the ordinary writable open first and falls back to `mode=ro` only when that open is refused for lack of write access ([#1416](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1416)). Against a writable store — every ordinary store — the handle is the writable one, so an observational command pays the whole open-time write window: the schema battery, the one-shot migrations, the scope-id mint, and the [#1314](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1314) expired-lock sweep. **The open order is unchanged, and nothing in `src/aelfrice/` moves here.** `benchmarks/store_open_cost.py` measures what the writable-first attempt costs at the repo-store layout, by installing a SQLite trace callback on the connection `MemoryStore` opens and reading the row count off that connection's own `total_changes`: 87 statements — 56 `CREATE`, every one of them `IF NOT EXISTS`, 26 `SELECT`, and 5 `PRAGMA` — against 5 statements for a `mode=ro` handle, so 82 statements are the whole of what reversing the order could recover. **The four components are counted apart rather than as one keyword census**, because a total cannot answer which part a short-cut would be short-cutting: 58 statements are the DDL battery, 23 the migration check, 1 the scope-id mint, 1 the expired-lock sweep, and 4 the connection `PRAGMA`s that precede all of them. Attribution is by execution nesting — each component's entry point in `aelfrice.store` is wrapped, and the innermost open span owns the statement — so the split survives a one-shot being added or reordered, which a positional slice of the trace would not. The producer refuses to publish any per-component figure unless the parts sum to the whole, which is what catches an entry point being renamed on one side only.
  **The decision is that no component should be short-cut, and the split is why.** The DDL battery is 58 of the 87 and is the only part worth a mechanism, but it is also 56 `CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS` against a schema that already exists — the cost of establishing that is the cost being measured, and it is 0.282 ms. The migration check is 23 marker probes on `schema_meta`, one pair per one-shot, and skipping it is what `mode=ro` already does: it is the degraded semantics [#1416](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1416) rejected, not a saving. The scope-id mint is a single `SELECT`. The sweep is a single index probe when nothing is due, and it is the one component that must run for correctness of what these commands print. There is no component whose removal is both material and semantically free, so nothing is proposed and the five callers keep the open they have.
  **The zero-write figures hold on a *settled* store, which is two conditions and not one.** Every one-shot has already run, **and** no time-boxed lock is due to expire. On such a store the open issues 0 `INSERT`, `UPDATE` or `DELETE` statements, mutates 0 rows, and changes 0 bytes of the database file. Neither condition is a hedge, and the second is the one an ordinary user meets repeatedly rather than once: on the same store with one lock past its `lock_expires_at`, the same open issues 95 statements — the sweep's own 9 of them — performs 6 `INSERT`/`UPDATE` statements, mutates 6 rows, and changes the file's bytes. That is the writable-first order working exactly as designed, and it is measured and published rather than described, under the `expired_lock_*` keys, because a reader who takes the zero as unconditional is reading a false statement about their own store. **The counts are size-invariant but not state-invariant**, and the entry says which: the producer refuses to publish a figure unless the census agrees at 200, 2,000 and 20,000 beliefs, so a count that starts moving with store size fails the gate instead of being averaged into one — while the 87-versus-95 gap above is a property of what the store holds, is not visible to any size grid, and has its own arm for that reason. On one machine at 20,000 beliefs the avoidable component's median is 0.267 ms against `aelf stats` at 9.3 ms in process, an avoidable share of 2.86% of the work the command does. **Those latencies carry no marker, deliberately.** A wall-clock reading is a property of the machine that took it, `--emit-figures` emits none, and `docs/design/derived_figure_markers.md`'s own rule is that a marker over a value nobody can recompute publishes a second unchecked number beside the first. The statement counts are store-free instead, because the producer builds its fixture from shipped code in a temporary directory: `scripts/check_derived_figures.py --mode producers` re-runs them on every pull request and hard-fails on a difference, which is the stronger of the two marker classes and the one a count is eligible for. **`--store` has no default, and it copies what it is given.** Nothing in the file calls `db_path()`, a run with no mode selected exits 2, and a named database is copied into a temporary directory before it is opened — opening a store is a write, so measuring one in place would mutate the corpus under measurement, which is [#1328](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1328) exactly. **Two layouts are published, because a census taken at the wrong path disagrees with itself**: outside the repo-store layout `repo_identity_from_db_path` returns no identity, the project-context backfill's marker probe drops out, and the same open issues 86 statements rather than 87 — a one-statement gap that is the likeliest way for a re-derivation of this issue to contradict the one recorded here.
  **The permanent deliverable is the invariant, not the number.** `tests/test_store_open_cost_1561.py` reds if a write enters the observational path on a settled store: strip the completion-marker guard from `_maybe_backfill_project_context` and the open stamps `schema_meta` on every fire, which turns every read command into a writer and which nothing else in the suite would see — `tests/test_readonly_store_1416.py` compares the file bytes only on stores frozen at 0444, where the engine forbids the write regardless. **Both halves of the precondition are asserted rather than hedged.** One test builds a store carrying an unrun one-shot, counts the rows it is due to stamp off the fixture, and pins the write it performs; another back-dates one lock's expiry on an otherwise settled store and pins that the open flips the lock, changes the file, and settles. So the zero-write guard cannot be misread as "an observational open never writes", and a future change making the open unconditionally read-only reds with the semantics it drops named in both directions. **`docs/user/COMMANDS.md` now documents the open for all five callers, not one.** The `show` row already carried the mechanism in full; the same explanation now sits above the command table as its own section, naming the five commands, what the writable-first open does on a store an older version wrote, what it does to an expired lock, and what the read-only fallback gives up instead — so `search`, `stats`, `locked` and `speculative` stop being silent on a write their readers would not expect.

### Reverted

- **`query_strategy` default returns to `legacy-bm25` ([#1501](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1501)).** The `stack-r1-r3` stack did not get worse; the baseline got better and overtook it. [#1177](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1177) replaced the conjunctive FTS5 MATCH with a disjunction over the rarest tokens, which is the recall cliff R3 existed to work around. Measured across that one commit on the same 30-row labelled corpus that justified the [#718](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/718) flip: `legacy-bm25` 0.3006 → 0.9553, `stack-r1-r3` 0.5858 → 0.8229, uplift +0.2851 → −0.1324. With no AND-set left to widen, the R3 clip only removes candidates — on a 16,454-belief store it drops 1,284 of the 1,859 terms that reach it (69.1%), and its boost half cannot compensate because it cannot fire: a term is boosted only when its IDF is strictly above the 75th percentile, and on that store the 75th percentile and the maximum IDF are the same value. `stack-r1-r3` stays selectable via `[rebuilder] query_strategy`, and both R-modules stay under test. Affects the context-rebuilder path (PreCompact / SessionStart), the transform's only caller. `tests/bench_gate/test_query_strategy.py` now asserts that the shipped default is the winning arm on the corpus, so it goes red on a re-flip and on a regression of the disjunctive MATCH; it stamps the corpus digest into the failure text. Found by the bench-gate tier at the v4.3.0 cut, its first run under [#1477](https://github.com/robotrocketscience/aelfrice/issues/1477).
